what exactly is the data that we're collecting on this? what methods? who collects the data and if member states do, is the methodology consistent? if intercountry adoption is carried out either within the union or with the assistance of a third party, what is the selection and pre-preparation of parents and children? how do we check the readiness? do we have a set of rules for this? what are the differences between member states' practices? is there an age limit for both the adopter and the child? do adoptive parents receive ongoing professional support? ensuring a secure food supply for the union will be one of the major security policy challenges of the coming decades. this is the biggest value of the sârbu report, which indicates that in a decade or two, food and fresh water will be as strategic as oil and natural gas are now. the timing of the report is also good, given the ongoing debate on the future of the cap, the budgetary planning for the period after 2014 and the many calls for the cap budget to be cut. it is very important that this common agricultural policy has adequate resources in the future. to do this, we must convince the finance ministers, the decision-makers, that the food industry in the european union not only produces food, but also public goods, environmental, social public goods, which we could not guarantee or produce in any way without agriculture.
on the subject of food safety, i find it disturbing that the report treats the countries of the european union as a single entity. the situation in the new member states is quite different from that in the old ones. let me ask you two questions about hungary. how is it possible that in a country with wonderful agricultural capabilities, we can talk about child hunger? in the heart of the european union, children and adults are starving. my second question is how is it possible that our agriculture has changed in such an unhealthy direction that we produce raw materials on huge areas in an industrial way, first of all - excuse the expression - but in a colonial way, and of course the processing of this food takes place abroad, there is produced the profit, which is then - again only by bringing back to hungary the processed food - realized at high prices. there's something fundamentally wrong with all of this.
mr. president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, the report makes it clear that the agricultural sector cannot go on as before. we can no longer have people shipping from one end of the world to the other, or big companies shipping agricultural products. well, that could work in an unnatural way. they import apples, peppers, tomatoes into europe, including hungary, and not from spain or holland, but mostly from china, brazil, and meat products. it's okay, but you have to put those products on a transportation vehicle, say a ship, and they travel for weeks or months. they have to be preserved with various preservatives that are toxic to humans if consumed in large quantities. now people in the big retail chains get these products and then they wonder what all these diseases, tumors, cancers are. well, obviously, food coloring and artificial additives are the culprits. i should add that small farmers must be supported so that they can be europe's lifeline in the face of an emerging food crisis, and that is why small farmers must be supported instead of multinationals.
mr president, we are discussing the poorest of the poor – the european roma – and, together with the new hungarian presidency, we are all addressing the european roma strategy. among inter-sectoral issues that concern the poorest of the poor, agriculture is very rarely addressed, notably because no land is owned by the poorest of the poor in europe. so we are talking not just about food security but about having food on the table. as one of my colleagues has already said, this is an essential question for the eu today. i believe that the agricultural sector – and the commission’s work on these documents – has to be in line with all the eu micro-regional development projects which are concerned with fighting poverty and securing food on the table for future generations.
on behalf of the ppe group. – madam president, in my view the report on serbia is excellent. i congratulate the rapporteur, jelko kacin. it seems to me that serbia has finally made up its mind that it genuinely wants a european future for itself. the majority of the political elite believes that this is the most favourable option. this determination has to be seen in the light of the turbulence of the last two decades. serbia experienced the end of communism, the collapse of yugoslavia, the wars that ensued, the rule of slobodan milošević, the bombing of 1999, burgeoning mafias and criminality, violence, refugees – a depressing and traumatic history. the decision to opt for europe, therefore, is a considerable achievement and signals that serbia has become a factor of stability in the region. two major political problems remain: the arrest and transfer of ratko mladić to the hague, and coming to terms with the independence of kosovo. both these point towards psychological barriers. mladić has his protectors, and the loss of kosovo is painful for a sizeable section of serbian society; any loss of territory is painful. to these may be added several other areas that need attention, notably the upgrading of public administration and of the administration of justice, the establishment of market conditions and full openness towards serbia’s past, including opening up the archives. the auguries for serbia’s road towards europe are reasonably good. there is a clear commitment in this direction. the tasks faced by the elite and by society are recognised and the technical preparations are going ahead. if all goes well, serbia should receive the opinion this autumn and candidate status after that. the road to europe will not be easy, but europe as a whole benefits from a stable, democratic serbia.

mr. president, fellow members of the house, madam secretary of state. i personally support the report on the enforcement of patients' rights in cross-border healthcare. many examples demonstrate that many people turn to other member states for adequate healthcare. we are all familiar with the kohl case in luxembourg, which is proof of this. unfortunately, the external and internal differences in the eu, and i mean the differences between the western and eastern parts of the eu, are not just economic differences, differences in social care, but also a deep gap in health care, and it is the task of the european parliament to reduce these differences, that is, to reduce the differences between western and eastern europe, because with uniformly good health care, the number of people who need to use health care from one member state to another can be reduced. in addition, it is essential to harmonise the role of health insurers, so that the principle of free movement is not undermined. in addition, i consider it necessary to coordinate the recommendations of the college of experts so that the same treatments are applied in each member state to the same groups of diseases. health cannot be a privilege, and the same disease cannot be treated differently in hungary just because it's eastern europe and the financial situation is worse, and in sweden just because it's western europe and the financial situation is better.

member of the commission. − mr president, i have listened carefully to the views expressed by members of this house. the asylum procedures directive is an essential element of the common european asylum system that will need to be established before the end of 2012. the commission welcomes the commitment of the european parliament to proceed with the negotiations on the asylum procedures directive. as regards the negotiations in the council, the commission appreciates the commitment of the member states to discuss the proposal in an open and constructive manner. several elements of the proposal proved, nevertheless, to be difficult indeed. thus, to facilitate the adoption of the directive the commission intends to adopt a modified proposal before the start of the polish presidency. the commission’s overall objective regarding this directive is to progress towards a common procedure and to facilitate more consistent and efficient application of procedural standards. the modified proposal will continue to promote respect for fundamental rights and high-quality decision-making at first instance, which will result in solid decisions and faster conclusions of procedures, hence leading to a reduction of costs. in the modified proposal the commission will aim to simplify certain provisions with a view to facilitating their application. the commission will continue to endeavour to ensure proper balance between fairness and efficiency of procedures. concerning specifically the financial impacts, the commission fully understands the importance attached to the cost-related elements of this proposal, and will continue to be attentive to the cost aspects during the next stages of the negotiations. let me close by stressing that the opinion of the european parliament is, of course, a key benchmark which will allow the commission to take into account parliament’s position when preparing the modified proposal. we will continue to work with parliament and the council towards progress on this proposal within the context of the wider asylum package.

mr president, competition policy is a cornerstone of european legislation. we agree that innovation, creativity and being better necessarily mean market advantage, but we do not accept that, just because someone is bigger and stronger with better connections to information and, in particular, special connections to government, these kinds of advantage should apply on the market, and we are very happy that the commission is on guard here to ensure that they should not. i would like to mention once again a new phenomenon: it is not market players but rather governments which seem, from time to time, to be playing by giving special advantages, in terms of competition policy, to some of their favourite companies. this is a very bad phenomenon and it goes back to party treasuries. i would ask the commission to move in this direction: let us have innovation, creativity and small and medium-sized enterprises, but do not allow any kind of misuse of information or power, either economic or political, on those territories.
mr. president, ladies and gentlemen of the house. as we said yesterday, the hungarian presidency's priorities include strengthening the internal market and supporting job creation. as a prerequisite for these priorities is a european competition policy based on a level playing field in all sectors, the preparation of this report is a great pleasure. but, my fellow congressmen, that's in the past. he's changed over time. however, i think it is important to draw the lessons from the report and to highlight the fact that the european parliament - and we as members - can play a more active role in shaping competition policy in the future. i would ask the commission to help me in this. first of all, i ask and consider it necessary that parliament be kept regularly informed of all the initiatives taken in this area. secondly, i ask the commission, as the only competition authority with competence throughout the eu, to report annually to parliament on the use made of its recommendations and on deviations from them. i consider it important - and i join my colleagues in this - that competition policy cannot be dealt with vertically. there is a need for coordination in all functional and regulatory areas. however, this is the commission's task. therefore, it is essential to align union policies and priorities set out in the eu 2020 strategy to support growth and jobs. finally, i would like to mention small and mediumsized enterprises, whose role is essential and unavoidable in the european economy as a whole - and, of course, in terms of increasing employment. however, the conditions of competition must be equal. competition policy must ensure this. i ask the commission to give priority to smes and to ensure that they are able to compete on a fair and non-discriminatory basis.

mr. president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, i am pleased that the european council is discussing together the issue of energy security and innovation. there is no doubt that energy security cannot be negotiated without innovation. we are of course in favour of a north-south pipeline being built alongside the east and west lines, but this will not solve the energy supply problems that are arising. and the addiction doesn't decrease significantly, just the direction of the addiction. the only way to minimise dependence in the long term is to shift the focus from fossil fuels to renewable energy sources. it is in this spirit that i regret that the renewable energy issue is not sufficiently highlighted as one of the priorities for innovation. in this context, we must focus on two things in particular: firstly, renewable energy must be affordable, so that the population is not charged extra for using renewable energy. secondly, in order to prevent the dependence outside the european union from being converted into dependence within the european union, it is necessary to include the old member states, which are currently major renewable energy powers, such as belgium, the netherlands or austria, and therefore the more backward newly joined countries, such as hungary, poland and the czech republic, in the list of renewable energy sources. only by closely linking energy security and innovation can a prospective energy crisis affecting europe be avoided.
i congratulate the hungarian presidency of the european union on finally bringing this very important subject to the highest level of discussion in the council. affordable and accessible energy is in the interest of all eu member states. the european union must make use of all possible sources of energy. but we have a lapse that no one has noticed. hungarian-born nobel prize winner professor george oláh has discovered the methanol energy economy. china already has a lot of power plants, and iceland is the only country in europe with this kind of power. there is no methanol-based energy economy in the union at all, although it is extremely cheap, as it produces energy from carbon dioxide, water and electricity, which can be used to run cars, generate electricity, heat and other sources.
the energy summit on february 4th could be a real breakthrough, because words and deeds are finally meeting. the council also discussed the strategy and the infrastructure needed to implement it. i hope that the results will be as expected and that on 4 february we will witness the birth of a genuine common european energy policy. one more thought, if i may, mr. president. i would like to welcome the exchange of letters between the european commission and the hungarian government on the hungarian media law. i'm glad that the dialogue is taking place in the usual procedural manner in such cases. that should reassure everyone who's interested in solving the problem. i'm confident that the case can be closed in a few weeks. further malicious and baseless political attacks are thus rendered unjustified. i believe that this is in the interest of the union, and not what we have unfortunately witnessed today in the house, by virtue of a speech.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen of the house. in recent years, the number of tb patients and deaths from tb have increased alarmingly in hungary. it was partly the result of nation-destroying liberal thinking. i'm thinking of the shameful 2006 hungarian healthcare law, which was based on hospital closures and profit-driven healthcare. poverty, such as increased homelessness and increasingly difficult access to health care, plays an important role in the development of the disease. i think it's important to strengthen the preventive-curative role of health care, to expand screening, and not just to develop improved vaccines, but to make them available to everyone. these steps would mean a healthier nation, a healthier europe and a healthier society in the long run.
in writing. – throughout the history of mankind, tuberculosis has killed more people than any other disease. this highly contagious, airborne bacterium can infect ten to fifteen other people before the symptoms are noticed. overcrowded housing, slumification and the lack of proper hygiene have always been a serious risk factor for tuberculosis, and therefore tb is the disease most strongly related to and partly caused by poverty. despite the efforts of the who and national authorities, the number of people dying of tuberculosis amounts to 1.5 million every year and the number is still increasing. i full-heartedly support the tuberculosis vaccine initiative and sincerely hope that it can provide more vaccines for sufferers in europe and around the world. but it must be emphasised that the fight against tb must walk hand in hand with the fight against poverty and social exclusion. it is necessary to identify the vulnerable groups as well as to determine and eliminate the barriers preventing these groups from accessing the full range of tb prevention, diagnosis and treatment. it would also be beneficial to modify the agenda of tb services in order to respond better to local needs and to address the widespread discriminatory practices in health systems.

financial instruments, including this instrument for the promotion of democracy and human rights, has been a subject of interinstitutional debate for a year. the treaty of lisbon has strengthened the european parliament's right of democratic control, which is in fact the institution of 'delegated acts' which we believe should be enforced and applied in the present situation. this particular package, the case of the 'delegated acts', would be the first significant example of the european parliament's role actually being increased after 1 december 2009. so in the second reading, and in writing. i consider it extremely important that waste electrical and electronic equipment is disposed of properly. increasing the proportion of waste collected separately is also essential, but it is important that the entire recycling process can be traced through appropriate documentation. this requires a responsible attitude on the part of industry, and more effective control of the behaviour of large manufacturing and processing companies. however, it should be noted that member states, especially the newly acceded ones, do not yet have sufficient recycling facilities and that emphasis should be placed on their development in order to ensure that the waste collected is treated in a truly professional manner.
in writing. - mr president, i have to go. the current revision of the waste electrical and electronic equipment directive is of great importance not only from the point of view of environmental protection and public health, but also from the point of view of resource efficiency, which is so much talked about today. it is well known that the union is the destination of a large number of illegal shipments of e-waste every year, and it is therefore in the vital interest of our industry that member states keep valuable raw materials within the union by professionally collecting and, where possible, recycling their increasing amounts of e-waste. of course, consumption patterns differ from country to country and this should not be ignored when setting targets for effective recovery. in hungary, for example, the average consumer will not replace his television in two or three years, but if he does, he will most likely take it to his holiday home rather than to the collection points. in view of all this, the rapporteur, recognising this problem, was able to secure a widely supported compromise in which the basis for the collection targets was not the quantity of products placed on the market but the quantity of electronic waste generated. although there are still many technical details to be worked out, i believe this could be a good direction. in particular, the commission's own study of the capacity of the member states contradictorily considers the original recovery objective to be realistic only for the old member states and not for the new ones.

mr president, a hungarian computer technician can legally go to the czech republic to work. that is great. of course, he went in the past too, but on the black market. one of our greatest achievements is that one can now move legally. only eight per cent of smes make 15 per cent of their turnover in the european markets. if we simply doubled this, so that 20 per cent of smes made 30 per cent of that turnover in the internal market, we could achieve what we want. after one year, we are on the way to doing this. i would like to say thank you to all those who worked on the services directive in the past and those who implemented it in the member states. it has a great pulling effect on each single government to change to e-governance and use new ict technologies. the minor problems with the languages have to be looked at carefully and this we have to improve, but the great success of the services directive is that it will have a great impact on the european economy in the coming years. beside the austerity programmes, this is the achievement, so i would like to congratulate the rapporteur and those who participated.
mr president, knowledge creation within the european union is always a priority, which we manage successfully. however, knowledge management is not so well dealt with in the european union. i was very sorry to hear that the belgian presidency could not finish the patent directive as planned, but i hope that the hungarian presidency will be committed to doing its best in the coming months to get to the next phase, which is enhanced cooperation. time is the only resource which is not renewable. the time we lose never comes back. we cannot experience europe solely through austerity programmes. we need exploration of knowledge, we have to expand, and therefore it is of critical importance for europe that patents, whether with enhanced cooperation or with the common european directive, get off the ground. i would like to wish great success to those who participate in this and to those who are in favour of it. being hungarian we will lose the hungarian language, but that is a small price to pay compared to the advantages we will all benefit from.
mr. president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, unfortunately, the hungarian nation experienced firsthand the severe food contamination caused by the nuclear accident during the chernobyl disaster. we must do everything we can to ensure that the chernobyl-type disaster does not happen again. one element of this prevention is the need to control extremely strictly existing and operating nuclear power plants. but i think it is acceptable for the future to focus on renewable energy sources and not to increase but to reduce as much as possible the role of nuclear energy in the energy supply. this is also essential for the european union in terms of energy security. it's the only way to increase our own security. we should not support the nuclear industry and take it into account, but only the health of the european people, our electorate, what is good for them and what is bad for them. i think it's important to have a contingency plan for a potential disaster, but i think our first priority is to reduce the risks, to minimize them. it is possible, but it will require political will and the appropriate determination on the part of my colleagues. we need to address the root of the problem, not wait for disaster to strike.

author. - mr president! - what are you doing? my dear officer. my fellow members of the house of representatives! what are we talking about when we talk about the european danube strategy, when we talk about the danube region? this region links 14 european countries and 115 million european citizens. it is perhaps no exaggeration to say that the european danube strategy is the strategy of the future. not just the future of central europe, maybe not even the future of the european union, but the future of europe in a broad sense. it is not simply looking back, it is not simply seeking to solve problems that we have brought with us from the past, but it is seeking to develop a recipe for cooperation that can solve the tasks of the future. in drawing up the danube region strategy, we used an old-fashioned method which may have really brought the union, the union's initiative, closer to the european citizens, since the commission, after listening to and taking account of the opinions of many, many interested parties over a very long period of time, developed the vision published last december. it is also a very, very important issue that the countries, regions and people of the danube region have been particularly affected by the crisis, the economic and financial crisis, and so the strategy of the danube region must be a strategy for recovering from the crisis, which must in any case focus on increasing employment. i think that we can all welcome the approach of the hungarian presidency of the european council here in the european parliament, which considers the strategy of a developing and enriching danube region and the strategy of a liveable danube region to be extremely important. in this respect, it is particularly important that the eu resources that have not been used so far by the eu member states concerned by the danube strategy in their cohesion policy are made available for the implementation of the cohesion strategy.
on behalf of the s&d group. it's good that mr. hahn is the commissioner in charge. it is also from a danube country, because there is a lot of work ahead for both the hungarian presidency and the european commission to create harmony between the 11 policy areas. there are too many priorities, colleagues and officers. how will balance and harmony be achieved between the different priorities? i have no doubt, because i too want this strategy to succeed, and the best thing about this strategy is that it forces cooperation, it forces the countries along the danube, where cooperation was often lacking. however, it is questionable how the sometimes conflicting objectives of the danube strategy, such as water quality, environmental improvement, navigability, energy recovery and ecotourism, can be reconciled. the commission therefore has a lot of work to do to bring the 11 countries and the 11 policies into harmony after the adoption in june.
in writing. - mr president, i have to go. it has long been a commonplace in science that mankind has three major strategic treasures, for which wars could be waged. these treasures are the earth's oil, food, and freshwater resources. the danube is the largest freshwater river in europe. all other benefits - from transport to energy production - can only be secondary to the importance of water as a strategic asset. as i have stressed in several speeches to parliament, i therefore believe that the danube strategy should be a means of protecting drinking water resources in the first place. this position is also consistent with the eu's efforts to meet the eu water quality requirements set out in the water framework directive. i therefore believe that when the interests of water protection and energy production, or of water protection and inland waterway transport, may conflict in the practical implementation of the strategy, this first priority should be given priority. in view of all this, i am surprised that the commission's communication, in the context of the development of danube navigation, contains specific objectives which require a maximum depth of 2.5 metres to be achieved on the river every day of the year. this is particularly strange because, as far as i know, not only the hungarian water experts, but also their austrian and german colleagues, have not been happy with this statement.
in writing. the movement for a better hungary considers it important and necessary to launch the european union strategy for the danube region as soon as possible. however, the main problem is, as usual, one of transparency and funding. the support available from the cohesion fund and from territorial cooperation programmes should reach the local level. the current regional disparities can only be reduced by a well-thought-out and transparent strategy that efficiently distributes unused resources, directly supporting local entrepreneurs and investments. member states wishing to join the eu, such as croatia and later serbia, will also become natural partners of the danube region. the strategy should therefore place a much greater emphasis on crossborder cooperation and the role of the new member states.
high-quality police training in the european union is essential to the implementation of our first security strategy and to the successful fight against terrorism and organised crime. a police training that can harmonise and bring to a common level the very different police traditions and very different capabilities of the different member states. the european police college will play a distinguished role in carrying out an important task. it is sad that cepol, the european police college, has become known in the european parliament for the previous management of cepol, the european police college, for its irregular and corrupt functioning, but it must be said, and perhaps fewer people know this, and fewer people care about scandals, that the appointment of new heads in 2010 has started a very positive process in the functioning of the european police college. so it's important that we not only look at the past, but also look at the future. the future objectives require two things: to ensure the organisational autonomy of the european police college and the development possibilities necessary for its renewed operation. and for that, i would like your support and your attention.
the situation of national minorities depends primarily on the constructive cooperation between the majority society and the political representative of the minority. over the past 20 years, it has been proven that cross-border hungarian minority communities have been successful where the cooperative skills of the minority political leadership have met the constructive attitude of the political leadership of the majority society, which considers the minority as a partner, whether they are included in the government or not. there is no alternative to this cooperation. i am very pleased that the current slovak government has embarked on the path that may one day lead to a historic hungarian-slovak reconciliation. we must put an end to the nationalist bidding, the nationalism of one country provoking the nationalism of another country. i wish that in hungarian-slovak relations, not nationalisms, but minority-based cooperations would compete with each other. i hope that the historic hungarian-slovak reconciliation will have serious results, because there is no other alternative between the two countries.
i welcome the report on the rights of bus passengers. this service is also more accessible to eu citizens, including people with disabilities. the compromise was reached after a long negotiation. in the meantime, the european parliament and mr cancian have stood by people with disabilities. unfortunately, the european council has been more short-sighted and inflexible. i am very sorry that the european council is unable to recognise that we live in a society which is aging rapidly. the number of people with disabilities is going to skyrocket overnight. we should recognise that every eu citizen will suffer from this problem and that sooner or later everyone will need help. and in closing, i want to emphasize that this is not the end of the work. the compromise has been reached in four areas of travel and now it is time for the single european act to be adopted, which will help to ensure that passengers' rights are respected in the future.
on behalf of the ppe group. – mr president, those who know russia will know that what passes for a legal system there does not measure up to the normal criteria of the rule of law. this affects russia’s relations with the rest of the world and equally with its own citizens. it follows that, when the european union deals with russia, it must take into account a variety of non-legal factors, notably political interference, corruption, and occasionally violence. the relationship between russia and the west, then, is subject to uncertainty, not to mention the complete absence of transparency and accountability. where russia’s own citizens are concerned, they lead lives more affected by arbitrariness and violence than can be reconciled with the european union’s commitment to universal human rights. attempts to transform russia have failed, so the european union has no alternative but to live with the awkward interface between its own legality and russia’s non-legality.

madam president! i think it is important, as many others have said, that there should be as much coordination as possible at european level on pensions. one is because the crisis raises the question of macroeconomic stability and raises the question of how to ensure security in old age, a dignified old age. we see a number of changes happening in the member states. in some countries, we see reforms towards strengthening or establishing the private pension fund pillar, while in others we see a reversal of the wheel of time. but whatever the type of change, it's very important that the sustainability of your retirement, the stability of your retirement, the dignity of your retirement, is the main consideration. any changes should be coordinated with the social partners, allowing adequate time for transitions. people, citizens, need to be informed about what changes are coming, how they will affect them, so that they can make informed decisions, and any change needs to be made in a civic way.
the european union has done a lot for people with disabilities. unfortunately, the pension systems of the member states do not really reach this social group, which is not sustainable. people who were forced to join the disability pension system through no fault of their own, there are also many people who have been forced to join the system by exploiting their loopholes, so this problem is mainly affecting the pension system of east-central europe, there are many people. so much so, that the very foundations of the pension system are shaking. this situation is contrary to the eu 2020 strategy. they get non-personalized services instead of real help, while they can be in the job market and work. in europe, current projections suggest that the number of dependent children will soon double. meanwhile, the number of people of working age is falling by 1 million a year. all of this means that a sustainable pension system does not exist at present. without people with active disabilities, it's especially not. i am very much in favour of the rapporteur's idea that in the eu, where 50 million people with disabilities live, some 40% of them are employed. what about 60% of them? so they should work, too.
member of the commission. − thank you very much for your supplementary questions. i agree with you that the figures you mentioned might not seem as ambitious as you would like them to be in the future, but the truth is that since we started with equal opportunity policies within the commission in 1995, we have managed to increase the presence of women in top positions fivefold. this means that now there are five times as many women in these positions than before. of course, we have to look at the baseline. i agree with you that it was very low. we also have to compare in what kind of environment in europe we are operating. this is also part of the response to the second question, namely that policies for creating these conditions for women, not only in the eu institutions but also in the member states, must still be improved. first we need to create suitable conditions for women so that they can actually work in these high positions, which means adequate childcare, adequate working facilities, flexitime and other conditions which actually allow women to be in these high positions. of course, these conditions are being created gradually. i think we have achieved quite high standards in the european institutions and you can see the various levels at which these conditions have been secured among the member states. therefore my colleague, vice-president reding, is now proposing a strategy which will be called gender balance in business leadership. so far, this is a staff working document, which will be discussed with business leaders in two months’ time, and then the public consultations will start. what are the lessons to be learned in this process? should we go through an obligatory quota system? should we go for the system we have in the commission, where we are trying to create a conducive environment and managerial incentives for those dgs which can actually fulfil the goals established for women? i think that after that we will see appropriate results.
the next item is the statement by the commission on the situation in albania.
since the parliamentary elections in 2009, political life in albania has come to a standstill. parliament cannot exercise its power of control over the government, and parliamentary institutions are unfortunately not functioning effectively. the fact that the socialists are accusing the government of electoral fraud and corruption, demanding its resignation and the early holding of elections in 2013, and that prime minister berisha is refusing to resign and accusing the opposition of seeking to take power by force, indicates that negotiations with the european union will soon collapse. the european union considers it essential that the candidate countries step up their efforts to combat corruption and organised crime. this is not only important for the union, but also for the societies of the countries concerned, not to mention the fact that the image of the candidate countries can be significantly improved in this way. the restoration of democratic institutions is therefore absolutely necessary to resolve the situation.
the situation in which albania finds itself is best described as a cold civil war. political parties play the role of warring sides and are pulling the country into a downward spiral. the explanation is that both left and right believe that political parties should rule exclusively, as the communist party once did. once a polarisation of this kind has begun, it is very difficult to transform the situation into one that resembles the democratic norm, because in this context exclusive power is completely rational, which is tragic. is there a way out? yes: catharsis. but only a major crisis or a grand historic compromise can bring this about.
member of the commission. − mr president, i would like to thank you for this lovely debate and, before i share with you my concluding remarks, let me address the two questions which were put during the debate. the first one, put forward by mr belder, i will answer in written form, taking into account our primary focus in today’s discussion. but there was another question: whether we are considering sanctions. no we are not. why is this? because in issues of enlargement, conditionality works very fairly, as is clearly the case here. if you fulfil the conditions, you are allowed to proceed with accession and eventually join the european union. if you do not fulfil the conditions and requirements, the process is slowed down and eventually stopped. unfortunately this is the ultimate price that is being paid not only by the politicians but also by the citizens of that respective country. the high representative and i will continue to strive to facilitate dialogue between albania’s majority and opposition. we have a clear idea of how to do this. we also need your support to make sure that the leaders of the main opposition party and the prime minister of albania understand our message. this could lead the country out of the political crisis and could allow positive steps towards its integration into europe. the european union made a commitment to all the western balkan countries that they would enter the european union. we stand by our commitment and will step up our efforts, provided the countries fulfil the criteria. albania urgently needs to implement the 12 key priorities of the opinion. these include addressing the fundamental principles of democracy, such as the proper functioning of parliament, separation of powers, the conduct of elections and respect for the rule of law. all of this will only be possible once the current political situation has taken a fundamental positive turn with the resumption of a full and constructive political dialogue in parliament, respect for all state institutions and the realisation that the citizens of albania have aspirations that require attention. the leaders of albania must act now for the benefit of their people. the european union stands ready to assist.
i'll close this discussion. the commission shall be assisted by the committee established by article 15 of regulation (eu) no 182/2011.

member of the commission. − mr president, this has been a rather heated debate, but i would like to emphasise three points which i believe are fundamental. the accession process to the eu – and my country went through this – is not an easy one, but when a country is accepted into the eu we really believe that this member state has the ability to be the guarantor of fundamental human rights and also of the implementation of eu law. for this particular sector we have specific legislation. this is the directive concerning the processing of personal data and protection of privacy in the electronic communications sector. so the commission has a very long, and i would say a very respectable, track record on following eu legislation being implemented in member states. i reported to you where we start now and i believe that you should have full trust that the commission will act if eu law is breached. if it is not breached, it will not act. so the commission is monitoring the situation and, if there is the need, the commission will act, but i think we should not go beyond the structures that are in community law and the commission will definitely stay within community law.

madam president! the financial and economic crisis has turned into a social and employment crisis, which is why it is particularly important that the recognition that has been born in europe of the need for closer coordination in the field of economic and employment policies should be realised. this also means that any country that implements structural reforms, changes its tax policy or even reviews the opening of shops on sundays, for example, must consider whether it is helping the development of the employment system and whether it is contributing to the growth of employment. consultation with the social partners is also a key element in any decision. the european semester will be the first test of this recognition, when europe, together with the hungarian presidency, will be able to see for the first time to what extent these directives are being transposed and whether they are really being implemented. i believe that the hungarian presidency, the hungarian government, has a key role to play, not only in its presidency but also in providing a model, taking this into account in all economic policy changes and employment policy decisions and providing a model for other countries. i have to ask my hungarian colleague again. does subsidiarity in pensions and wages mean that workers and pensioners in hungary and the post-communist countries will consistently, continuously and forever have the same proportion of their pensions and wages as western europeans, because that's very sad, if that's what you mean. that's the way it is. a nurse earns 70 000 forints, a teacher 90 000 forints, and pensioners have fundamental difficulties in making a living. what does that mean?
no, madam president, that is not the way to understand it, but the question of pensions is fundamentally dependent on the economic performance of the economy. it depends on what we can produce and on that basis we can provide adequate pensions. so the strategy we are talking about, which is to make europe competitive, viable, successful, this means the opportunity to raise pensions in countries that are even more disadvantaged today, but it is not possible to introduce balancing rules, because there are huge differences at the moment. clearly, a gradual approach is needed in the future.
in writing. the eu 2020 strategy not only overtook the lisbon strategy when it was formulated, but had to be adapted to the new crisis period, and now also to reflect the period of economic governance and the rescue of the euro. the new member states must also ensure that they remain sufficiently mobile and competitive to address the structural and employment problems that have persisted and deepened since the eastern european transition. because of past mistakes and serious governmental failures, the situation of families must also be addressed: the labour market is not only made up of employers and employees, but also of more or less closely linked, multi-generational families, which are not only the foundations of society, but also the backbone of its future. closer intergenerational cooperation alone can have a major impact on employment (e.g. placing young mothers in work, promoting the integration of people with disabilities, keeping older people active even in the civil sphere) and on joint planning (e.g. housing) to avoid individual and family debt crises and bankruptcies that can already force families into the grey economy due to high repayment installments. this old road is no longer viable. new approaches will also be needed, as the old recipes are no longer sufficient, and the european union must recognise this. the eu must support even the most unusual solutions.

in writing. three factors are causing the sharp rise in food prices: yield losses due to natural disasters, increasing food demand mainly from china and india, and commodity market activity, price speculation. french president sarkozy very rightly asked the question a few weeks ago: 'if financial markets are regulated, why are commodity markets not? other we need to act much more effectively against speculation at eu and global level. the main cause of food shocks is the huge fluctuations in supply. only 10 to 20 percent of cereals are traded on the global market, yet a drop in production can cause panic. in view of the forecast decreasing stocks, importers are raising prices. it is not fair to producers or consumers that export prices also determine domestic prices, since at least 80% of production is consumed where it was produced. the former or current position of russia as an exporter has a fundamental influence on the development of the european cereals and oilseeds market. supply shocks are further exacerbated by speculation in futures markets, and a food shock occurs. so the real problem is not the physical shortage of food but the price, which makes it impossible for the poor to afford it. thus, food is kept in storage rather than being sold at a fair price. high prices are a barrier to consumers, especially the poor.

mr president, officer! you guys! as the shadow rapporteur on the discharge of the european investment bank, my colleague audy and i are treating the eu guarantees to the european investment bank as a very important issue. the european investment bank is a key player in financing major projects both inside and outside the eu, and its role will continue to grow. however, under the current rules, no european institution has the right to supervise the eib, and we would like to change this in the future so that it does not hinder future growth. loans to non-eu countries require more care and attention than projects within the community, not only because of what has been said, but also because external developments are far from being subject to the same controls as investments within the eu. the solution to this is that these projects require much better preparation. at the same time, most external projects are strategic but do not always flow into politically stable areas. as a member of the committee on industry, i would like to highlight the energy issue among the projects being implemented in these regions. more than 50% of external investments financed by the eib are directed to the eastern partnership countries and russia. the security and development of these countries is directly in the eu's interest, as they are the source of a major share of the central and eastern european and baltic countries' natural gas imports and of their electricity imports. it is therefore essential that the eib focuses on supporting projects that directly or indirectly result in an increase in safety and security of supply. i therefore agree with the rapporteur, mr kalfin, whom i congratulate, that the eib should be encouraged to cooperate more closely with the european bank for reconstruction and development and the world bank in the field of non-eu lending. significant synergies can be achieved.
in writing. the budgetary guarantee granted by the european union to the european investment bank (eib) ensures the stability of the bank and covers the political and country-specific risks arising from its activities. this is particularly true for countries and projects that would have difficulty or no access to capital markets. the decision renews the guarantee mandate, sets the ceiling for the funding, lists the eligible countries and determines the rate at which they can receive support. the system of regional objectives is replaced by high-level, global objectives covering all regions, in accordance with the principles laid down in the decision. the european commission, in cooperation with the eib and in consultation with the european external action service, shall develop operational guidelines reflecting the eu's regional strategy for all regions covered by the mandate. in the future, the focus will be on sectors such as health, education, environmental infrastructure, water supply, waste water treatment, sustainable transport development, and climate change mitigation and adaptation. the budgetary control committee was specifically tasked with reinforcing the budgetary control functions contained in the decision. in order to increase transparency, it is particularly important to forward to the european parliament all reports and information on the eib's activities, to measure the concrete results of projects supported by the eu guarantee and to maintain the european court of auditors' right of audit over the use of funds from the eu budget.

rapporteur. − madam president, i would like to thank all my colleagues in the european parliament and also in all the civil organisations for their support and valuable input in drafting this heavy report. if you will allow me a personal comment, this is the place and time where i have to thank my colleagues in the office, my political advisor in the libe committee who has been behind this strategy for years and my head of cabinet in the last few years who has fought with me. i also owe gratitude to my family who allowed me to be in the forefront of this fight, a fight which has in the end produced a result. we have an agreement spanning six political groups, 27 member states and five very important parliamentary committees. in this way i believe we are able to send a message to the commission, the council and the member states that must be reckoned with and we have therefore made a great step towards an eu-level effort to alleviate the poverty and social exclusion of our continent’s largest ethnic minority. a significant proportion of european roma face such substandard conditions – almost totally disconnected from the economy resulting in their exclusion from basic human rights – that fostering social inclusion cannot be viewed within the framework of general policy ratification but must be handled as bridging one of the most enormous gaps in the fulfilment of constitutional and human rights in europe. the eu-level effort to alleviate the poverty and social exclusion of european roma must therefore place its primary emphasis on the fulfilment and promotion of the fundamental rights to employment, housing, healthcare and education. let me briefly outline the most important principles and recommendations of my report. above all, the strategy must be an internal eu strategy, the general oversight of priority areas and objectives must therefore be within the community structures and it should in no way be divisive for the eu, creating splits among the member states. on the contrary; establishing a new body to coordinate and supervise the strategy would be unnecessary and would create a wasteful overhead. the role of supervising, coordinating and monitoring must therefore be fulfilled by the commission and, to this end, it would be worth considering retaining the roma task force as a permanent body. the commission should report annually on the progress of the strategy, evaluate the results and submit its upgrading proposals to the council. in this regard the european roma strategy scoreboard, a mechanism similar to the single market scoreboard, could be developed. the appointment of an administrative body or a government official to be the national contact point for the implementation of the strategy’s objectives, and furthermore to act as a source of advice and reflection for the commission supervisory strategy, would also bring significant practical benefits. the strategy must also take into account the territoriality of exclusion since the geographic distribution of social disadvantages is not uniform throughout the member states, but poverty and social exclusion are concentrated in underdeveloped micro-regions, which in many of the new member states are predominantly inhabited by the roma community. common, comparable and reliable indicators are essential to present a realistic analysis of progress and to meet the requirements of effective monitoring. i recommend adopting the laeken indicators and their complementary components to measure social and territorial exclusion, as well as to evaluate progress. an all-european crisis map is necessary to measure and identify the micro regions by their distinctive attributes. part of the cohesion budget should be set aside in the next multiannual financial framework in a performance reserve for the strategy. this could provide vital resources and devise incentives for implementation by allocating funds on a competitive basis defined by the criterion of how the proposed project or intervention supports and implements the objectives of the strategy. this way, on the one hand, we could depart from the general practice of large contributions maximising their returns from the unabsorbed funds and, on the other hand, a direct link could be created between the allocation of financing and the reproduction of results. these are the principles and the priorities that i believe the european roma strategy must sustain and i am looking forward to your comments on my report. (applause).
the committee on employment and social affairs has adopted an opinion. - madam president! let me start with a story. b. józsef and his wife were sentenced to 10-10 months in prison by a hungarian court in 2009 for endangering a minor. their fault was that their two children were not in school. but when they came in, the kids were sent home because they had dirty shoes and they were dirty. the gypsy farm where they live has no concrete road, so the children often had to cross the fields to get to school, their feet muddy. they endured the humiliation of being called names and sent home, and then they avoided school more and more. and their parents were sentenced to prison for it. b. joseph's children did not miss school because they did not want to learn. their circumstances did not allow them to study. without proper clothing and shoes, without water and electricity at home, without teaching materials, they had no chance of completing elementary school. the process of closing down a cluster of disadvantaged communities is complex. this can only be done step by step, adapting to local needs, and in the long term. europe can help by setting targets, providing the means, holding accountable the achievement of targets and monitoring the changing situation of roma, based on real data. it's developing ways that are less bureaucratic, faster, more transparent, and that support a community over the long term until it can really take care of itself. it is not enough to build a school for b. józsef's children, they must work to earn a living, feed, clothe, send their children to school. you need decent living conditions, where you can prepare for the next day's school lessons, where there is light and heating. we need a good road to get to school and work. and a human word that can make you forget your humiliations. the european roma strategy will not bring immediate change, but it does offer a chance. the real question is, can we create rules, can we create conditions that will get aid to those who can't compete for resources, who have little capacity for advocacy? there is a need to guarantee non-discrimination, not only in the labour market but in all areas of social life. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the eu-funded programmes and will continue to monitor the implementation of the eu-funded programmes. and above all, political commitment from all those who can contribute to change the situation at european level. it is not enough to provide a framework for national roma strategies, but a genuine european roma strategy is needed, with responsibility at european level, without, of course, disputing the responsibility of the member states. i ask the commission and the council to transpose parliament's resolution in full and to report regularly to parliament on the results.
mr. secretary of state, officers reading and andor. first of all, let me congratulate járóka lívia on her excellent report and on all the work she has consistently done here since 2004 to protect the roma communities in europe. i'd like to take this discussion today from the perspective of the children. we must ensure that every child in europe today has access to opportunities and to advancement. whether it's a minority or a majority child. it means giving every child the chance to develop physically and mentally, which means the right to a daily hot meal as well as the right to an education. all of this, which today should appear as a right of the child, on the one hand, and the roma child on the other, should be seen as a duty and responsibility of the parents. today, the proportion of children who are disadvantaged and living in extreme poverty is 19%, at eu level, and a large number of these children are roma. a roma strategy at eu level, based on the járóka report, should therefore strengthen cohesion at member state and societal level, for example through crisis maps, and encourage local initiatives. because it's in all of our interests to prove that it's possible to change the way we think about our environment. i welcome the fact that the hungarian presidency is making this roma strategy one of its top priorities and that it is working closely with ms járóka on this.
madam president, i would like to congratulate the hungarian presidency and commissioners andor and reding on their very sincere commitment. but, mrs reding, the basic principles of protecting the rights of national minorities in europe should be clarified. firstly, the situation of any national minority is not a purely national matter, it is a european matter. secondly, effective integration of national minorities could be implemented on the basis of mutual but asymmetrical shared co-responsibility of the majority and minority. the majority has more responsibility, but the minority still has the responsibility. the integration of national minorities should be implemented without forced assimilation. in the end, the prohibition of discrimination is not enough. preferential treatment of positive actions could only contribute to the genuine integration of national minorities.
rapporteur. − madam president, i will be very brief. i would like to thank you very much for all your valuable comments. they reinforce my and my very good shadow rapporteurs’ view that political slogans will never become a reality without a proper legal basis, financial support and incentives as well as an eu mechanism to coordinate the actions of the stakeholders, ranging from local governments up to the council and harnessing the advantages of multi-level governance. this will place the whole situation in a new light. i firmly believe that, on the basis of the tasks, objectives, principles and instruments defined by all the treaties and the charter of fundamental rights, and furthermore based on the shared competences as well as the supporting, coordinating and complementary actions of the union, a very strong strategy on roma inclusion can come into existence. to conclude, i would like to express the hope i have formed, over the past six years, that the upcoming commission communication will be able to endorse these points and provide a very good basis on which the hungarian presidency can pledge its commitment to launching this european roma strategy. i am very grateful for this. i am aware that this european strategy is not going to solve the problem; i know that a huge amount depends on us, the roma. words and promises mean nothing without actions outside of brussels and in the roma settlements. governments and roma must do what they have signed up to: take determined action to deliver tangible results. we understand our responsibility in this and we are fully in favour of our common partnership. this process for us roma, and i believe for the whole european union, is about much more than mutual trust. it is also about building a community of values that we can all share, and that we can all enjoy, to be able to really challenge the difficulties that we have been discussing here over the last six years. i thank you very much for all your support and i would like to join renate weber and the other shadow rapporteurs in thanking the secretariat of the committee on civil liberties and all the advisers who, with us, have been fighting very hard for this.
in writing. first of all, i would like to express my sincere appreciation for the fact that the hungarian eu presidency has included the issue of roma inclusion among its priorities. the hungarian government's commitment to this is clearly demonstrated by the fact that the hungarian people's party delegation includes rapporteur járóka lívia, the only roma representative in the european parliament, and that the rapporteur of the case, zoltán balog, is leading the newly established state secretariat for the elimination of disadvantaged gypsies. on this basis, we can rightly say that the hungarian eu presidency is setting a good example for europe in the settlement of the roma issue. with regard to the roma strategy, i consider it important to stress that the question of the integration of the 12 million roma living in the union is a common european issue, which requires the involvement of the individual member states and the eu institutions. prime minister viktor orbán rightly pointed out that if we do not want the problems of roma living in deep poverty and social and ethnic exclusion to be "exported" to the developed countries of the eu by way of "nomadisation" emigration , then we must find a solution to their unsustainable situation by acting together and using common efforts. i wish the european parliament, the european commission and the european council, and not least the hungarian eu presidency, a fruitful work towards the preparation and final adoption of the landmark roma integration strategy by june this year.
madam president! i was pleased to note that the industrial policy report refers in several places to the crucial importance of smes for the present and future of european industry, european workers and all of us. from now on, every document, every topic should focus on the promotion of smes. these small companies are the ones that provide the majority of jobs, they are the ones that are able to renew themselves the most, they are the ones that can play the biggest role in innovation, in stopping the breakdown of the european economy. and they also pay most of the taxes into the budgets, so they're essentially the ones who maintain the bureaucratic apparatus. in return, the bureaucracy has created a legal, institutional and economic environment, along with a banking system, that seeks to make it impossible for smes to operate. this short-term approach to profit-making by multinational companies cannot be continued. the report rightly states that speculative money market activity should be discouraged, but today more needs to be done. the commission has also proposed that the commission should be able to impose sanctions on banks that do not promote the provision of capital to smes through their credit policies. the report also suggests that industry is in decline in the newly joined countries and that the eu needs to find ways to stop unfair behaviour by large companies. it must be said openly that, with the assistance of the eu bureaucracy, the multinational companies of the old member states have in recent years bought up and destroyed much of the manufacturing industry of the newly joined member states, then bankrupted these companies and took over their markets. the result is millions of jobs and no vision, easy profits in the east and big tax revenues in the west. also, with the assistance of bureaucracy, unfortunately, a lot of the technology that was accumulated in europe was transferred to china.
the economic crisis has shown that the service sectors are much more sensitive than industrial policy, and europe needs to put industrial policy back on the map. i therefore welcome both commissioner tajani's approach and the report by our colleague, mr bernd lange, on this subject. it was a pleasure working on this report. each of my colleagues wanted to contribute something. we didn't argue about what was difficult, but how we could speed up the process. we need to understand that the european union has a leading role to play in setting its own objectives and in drawing the member states along. the european union must coordinate, lead and hold the member states to account for what they have done in this area. industrial policy offers europe the opportunity to gain a competitive edge in manufacturing, biotechnology, nanotechnology, chemicals and even space. we have to understand that in the industrial policy of the 21st century, the intensity of knowledge is increasing and the efficiency of the use of materials is improving. at the same time, vertical links from education through research to market opportunities need to be strengthened. it is necessary to develop supply chains, including small and medium-sized enterprises, and horizontal links. we think social dialogue is inevitable.
madam president, commissioner, when my grandmother was born, she was one of the two billion living on earth; when i was born, i was one of the five billion on earth; and when my granddaughter is born, she will be one of the nine billion living on earth. so extensive technologies must be replaced by intensive technologies for all resources, including rare materials. therefore i would like to remember somebody who, a hundred years ago, received a nobel prize for chemistry for the separation and purification of radium and polonium: maria skłodowska, who is known as madame curie. we need madame curies, we need scientists, we need breakthrough technologies. i would therefore like to ask you, commissioner, to do your utmost to ensure that chemistry remains a place for young talent in europe.

in writing. - my fellow members of parliament! gas produced using non-conventional technologies could play a key role in the eu energy market in the future. the commission considers that the measures are necessary to ensure that the union market is sufficiently open to competition and that the union market is sufficiently open to competition. it is also important that the deployment of new gas sources within the union could contribute to reducing greenhouse gas emissions in a cost-effective manner. in the united states, new technology has already revolutionized the gas market in recent years. with more than half of current us production coming from alternative gas sources, gas prices have fallen significantly and there is no longer a need to rely on imported sources. in europe, the world's largest gas market, the exploitation of alternative gas sources is still in its infancy. despite the successes in the us, the results of the european pilot projects so far have fallen short of expectations. in addition to the different geographical features, investors face greater regulatory and environmental risks in europe. in order to translate the successes of the us into the eu, it would be important for europe to have an appropriate strategy for alternative gas sources. a comprehensive study at european level would be needed to clarify the opportunities and risks of alternative gas sources. europe cannot afford to give up the luxury of alternative gas production within the union at a time when the traditional gas resources of the north sea are being depleted.

in writing. in its communication of 23 february 2009 on a community approach to the prevention of natural and man-made disasters, the commission inter alia undertook to collect existing sources of information on prevention measures and disasters. i would like to take this opportunity to point out that there has been a remarkable and exemplary practice in new zealand of informing the deaf and hard of hearing about the recent disaster. the new zealand government issued a number of information aids and a dvd at the end of 2010 to prepare affected and competent actors and the sign-language population for a possible disaster. the result was a measurable recovery in lives and property, which - despite the unfortunate tragedies that have unfortunately occurred - has been a source of pride for the queensland government and the australian communications and media authority (acma), according to local ngos and the world federation of the deaf (wfdeaf.org), due to the excellent preparation and cooperation, particularly in the implementation of live and instant communication to the deaf. in this light, i hope that the commission will look at this good practice in non-eu countries and promote and encourage it among member states and in the use of eu funds.

president van rompuy described the euro plus pact as a new stage in future economic coordination, and it is indeed a completely new era in macroeconomic coordination. the same was said by prime minister viktor orbán, the eu's president-elect, who described it as the pillar of the future european economic system. it is regrettable, however, that hungary's name was not included in the list that mr van rompuy mentioned, since hungary is not, unfortunately, a party to the euro plus pact. many hungarian citizens and economists do not understand why the hungarian government has taken this decision, since hungary is a small country with an open economy, and the argument that this would have hindered tax competition is not true, it would only be a question of harmonizing the corporate tax base. it's a pity hungary is not included.
