vice-president of the commission. − madam president, i am presenting, together with my colleague, lászló andor, the decision which the commission has taken to present a new framework for national roma integration strategies. the eu framework comes at a moment when the situation of roma has become a real focus of attention in europe. the eu has made available to the member states a range of legal, policy and financial instruments to address the situation of the roma. we also have a whole legal framework based on non-discrimination, free movement of people and the enlargement strategy. however it is very clear that the economic and social marginalisation of the roma persists, and this is neither acceptable nor sustainable in the european union of the 21st century. parliament, with support from across the whole political spectrum – and i would like to underline this – has drawn attention to the urgency of promoting the integration of roma, through its recently adopted resolution on the eu roma strategy. i would like to thank the rapporteur, mrs járóka, for her excellent work. determined action is needed, both at national and at european level. we need to join forces and step up our efforts to end discrimination against roma and make sure they enjoy the same rights as any other european citizens, because that is what they are. when i say that, i of course have in mind a two-way process which requires a change in the mindset of the majority of our european citizens and of members of the roma communities. we all agree that there is a need for effective policy planning and implementation as well as for a strategic common approach. we all know that the primary responsibility for the integration of roma lies with the member states. we also acknowledge that the situation of the roma varies greatly from one member state to another. that is why the commission decided to present an eu framework to enable the member states to take action commensurate with the challenge they have to overcome. the roma task force which was set up in september last year, and reported in december, showed clearly that, very often, not enough is being done. the eur 26.5 billion which are available under the structural funds are not being utilised as they should be. only eur 100 million of this money was earmarked for specific roma activities, while 70% of the means for technical assistance, which would have been necessary in most member states, was simply not used. so the measures in place so far are not adequate. it is now time to ensure that national, regional and local policies focus on roma needs in a clear and specific way. in our framework we concentrate on four main issues: education, employment, health and housing. we want the eu and the 27 member states to commit themselves to closing the gaps and to concentrate on the common goals in those four areas with targeted actions and sufficient funds to deliver them. we want all member states to build these goals into their existing roma strategy, if they have one, or to develop a roma strategy if they do not have one, for the period up to 2020. in order to check whether these efforts achieve their objectives, we need regular monitoring. therefore the eu framework lays the foundations for a robust monitoring mechanism to ensure concrete results for the roma. this monitoring mechanism will ensure that the national roma strategies are implemented, that money intended for roma integration reaches its final beneficiaries, and that there is progress towards achieving the roma integration goals. the commission will report annually on progress made towards integrating the roma population in the member states. the eu framework provides an opportunity for all stakeholders, at all levels, to join forces with the eu population in general and the roma population in particular in order to put an end to the exclusion of more than 10 million european citizens, which we can no longer afford for the future.
member of the commission. − mr president, honourable members, the socio-economic situation of roma is at the top of the eu agenda. last month this house adopted the járóka report on an eu strategy on roma inclusion. and today, the commission adopted a communication on an eu framework for national roma integration strategies. both documents make significant contributions to the wider debate on roma, and will feed into the conclusions to be adopted at the next council meeting of employment and social affairs ministers on 19 may, which will be exclusively devoted to the integration of roma. those documents articulate the eu’s political determination to address the situation of millions of roma who are suffering from economic and social exclusion. i would like to make three key points here. firstly, the commission has set out an eu framework for national roma integration strategies. it acknowledges the member states’ prime responsibility for designing and implementing roma integration strategies. it also reaffirms the commission’s deep conviction that the european union cannot tolerate any form of racism, xenophobia, discrimination or social exclusion of roma. the union has a moral and political obligation to support the member states in their efforts with all the policies and instruments at its disposal. secondly, as you know, the union has agreed on a comprehensive strategy for developing the european economic and social model in a balanced way. the europe 2020 strategy for smart, sustainable and inclusive growth is the right policy context for addressing the multifaceted problems facing roma. we must harness europe 2020’s objectives, targets, flagship initiatives and reporting structures for the cause of the roma. the key message is that excluding millions of eu citizens from economic and social life is not smart, is not sustainable and is certainly not inclusive. in short, there is no excuse for roma exclusion to continue in the europe we want to live in. in particular, the europe 2020 headline targets for employment, poverty reduction and education are key benchmarks for measuring progress in roma integration policies. the commission’s communication on roma identifies four areas that are of strategic importance to overcome roma exclusion. these are as follows. access to employment: the big gap between roma and non-roma employment rates has to be addressed. we will not achieve our europe 2020 targets if we do not do it. access to education: all roma children should have access to quality education and not face discrimination or segregation. as a minimum, all roma children should complete primary school. access to healthcare: reducing the gap in health between roma and non-roma. member states should provide access to quality healthcare under the same conditions as for the rest of the population. access to housing and essential services: where the situation of the roma people is indeed much worse than that of the rest of the population, for example where access to water, electricity and other services are concerned. the lack of an integrated approach has a negative impact on the overall integration of roma people in society and leaves them in poverty. thirdly, the union must put its money where its mouth is. the commission is currently drawing up a proposal for the multi-annual financial framework, which will be followed by specific proposals on the structural funds. to my mind, the structural funds need to be brought into line with the europe 2020 strategy. this applies to the policy areas for which i am responsible in general – employment and social inclusion – and it certainly applies to the socio-economic integration of roma. making our financial instruments more effective is a key objective for me. let me stop at this point, so i can listen to your views and answer any questions.
president-in-office of the council. − the hungarian presidency welcomes the commission communication on the eu framework for national roma integration strategies. i would like to express my gratitude and appreciation not only for the work done, but also for the personal dedication of commissioner reding, commissioner andor and all those who have cooperated on this truly significant achievement. with the presentation of this strategic document, we have moved a giant step closer to the realisation of one of the chief priorities of the hungarian presidency. i am convinced that it will prove to be an important contribution to the improvement of the economic and social situation of the roma in europe, together with the recently adopted resolution of the european parliament on an eu strategy for roma inclusion based on the report by lívia járóka. extreme poverty, discrimination and marginalisation continue to affect large numbers of roma throughout europe, so it is high time for improvement. we cannot afford to say that it is their problem, and not ours. moreover, people must never be considered as a problem, but rather as a chance, an opportunity. given the current economic and demographic situation in europe, the improvement of the economic and social status of the roma can open up new human resources, thus contributing to economic growth in the long run. the hungarian presidency has always believed that, besides the necessary fight against discrimination, a new policy mix is necessary, with three key elements: employment, education and housing. if followed up properly by the member states, integrated policy action in these three fields, complemented by adequate monitoring as proposed by the commission, will make a difference. the member states will also have to make an effort to reach the goals of the eu 2020 strategy. measures for the social inclusion of the roma can be mainstreamed in various policy fields. it is now widely recognised that measures inspired and coordinated at eu level can create synergy and added value. what is next? the hungarian presidency intends to present and discuss the communication with the two commissioners during the next roma platform on 7 april in budapest, to which i invite all who are interested. the council will deal with it in a horizontal manner. firstly, the april justice and home affairs council will discuss it on 12 april. then it will be the turn of the employment, social policy, health and consumer affairs council on 9 may, with the adoption of council conclusions. the education council will also receive the document, on 20 may, before the hungarian presidency presents its report on the results of the discussions to the general affairs council on 23 may. the final moment of this itinerary will, hopefully, be an endorsement in the june european council. it will, of course, just be a new beginning for coordinated action at eu and national level for helping this truly european minority.
on behalf of the ppe group. madam president, distinguished officers and secretary of state. based on the report from livia the traveler, we are pleased to be moving forward with the implementation of the roma strategy. speaking of which, april 8th is international roma day. the fifth meeting of the european roma platform is being held in hungary on this day. this is another important step towards a joint action plan coordinated at eu level but able to take account of local specificities. the european people's party has been calling for community action on roma inclusion for many years. and at the summit of heads of state or government on 24 june, we hope that the european roma strategy can really get under way. they can thus provide a european-level response to the union's most pressing human rights problem. it is primarily the responsibility of the member states to ensure that their citizens enjoy fundamental human rights. the union can only provide a framework for this, but the individual countries can add content to this framework. the european roma strategy can be a kind of professional minimum. but i am convinced that this is a long-term plan of action across parties and cycles that can effectively address the poverty and social exclusion of the roma. some important points about the content of the strategy: it defines its target group on an economic rather than ethnic basis, promotes the full range of human rights and reinforces the principle of equal opportunities, takes into account the territorial nature of exclusion and mitigates disadvantages concentrated in underdeveloped micro-regions. the success of the strategy requires a long-term, strong, joint effort by the relevant union institutions, member states, ngos and roma communities. that's why this case should not be used to create controversy, to attack party politics. there's no room for group stigma either. neither the collective guilt of the roma nor the collective responsibility of the majority, the oppressors, should be set against each other. the solution to the roma issue is a priority for the hungarian eu presidency. i hope that the european council will adopt the strategy in june. i ask the european parliament to support me in this.
on behalf of the ecr group. – madam president, eu leaders are living in a bubble of illusion and self-delusion. the best example of that happened this morning. leaders of eu institutions were saying that the financial crisis is largely behind us. nothing could be further from the truth. as wolfgang münchau wrote yesterday in the financial times, europe is facing a stark choice between default and bailout. both imply the absorption of a huge loss. the only difference between the two is how the loss gets distributed. in the case of a default, german, french, british, italian and spanish banks will suffer and the taxpayers of those countries will share the cost with greek, irish and portuguese taxpayers. in the case of a bailout, the total burden will be put on the back of greek, irish and – soon – portuguese citizens. it is in this context that i say that the proposal to increase the budget of the parliament by 2.3% is still too high and unacceptable. let us go back to the drawing board and reduce it to 1%, as suggested by commissioner janusz lewandowski. to show an increase well below inflation would earn some badly needed legitimacy for parliament in the eyes of european citizens. not only the increase, but also the structure can be modified. there are many ways to save money for the european taxpayer. as helga trüpel has already said, we do not need to travel business class within the european union. an amendment jointly tabled by the greens, the united left and the ecr proposes to reduce travel costs significantly by using flexible economy class tickets for flights under four hours in length. we do not need a shiny, glitzy fleet of black mercedes-benz cars here in strasbourg. use the tram. we do not need the house of european history, as europe is losing competitiveness and geopolitical weight. it will be a museum anyway. there is huge scope for savings on energy costs. our offices have no temperature control or individually adjustable heating and air conditioning. we waste a tremendous amount of energy every year. there is large scope for savings on security and it services. in both cases, we need far better service for much less money. we should review and revise our health insurance costs as well. there is no need to have access to such an extraordinarily generous range of health care services. budgeting is about setting priorities, both positive and negative. there is no shortage of positive ones, but responsible and prudent budgeting requires both, especially in times of crisis.

mr. president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, the report before us is one that can be basically agreed with. the question is, how much can we actually fill it with content? because the free movement of people, the free movement of capital, these are basically beautiful words, but what have hungarian people seen so far? they saw that western capital could come into eastern europe, but only people could come from eastern europe to the west, so people could flow freely. how unthinkable would it be for hungarian or czech or polish entrepreneurs to buy up, say, the german volkswagen company and then close it down in order to create a market for themselves? now german, french and english entrepreneurs have done this to hungary, when they abolished the sugar industry, the food industry, the canning industry, the textile industry in hungary. in fact, back in 2004, they campaigned to join the european union so that hungarian entrepreneurs could open a confectionery shop in vienna. it's not that hungarian entrepreneurs can't open a confectionery shop in vienna, it's that they can't open a confectionery shop in a small village. so we have to rebuild the food industry, the textile industry, the canning industry in eastern europe, so that we can be equal members of the european union, and respected members.

in writing. the single market is the main engine of development in the european union, which creates a framework for social inclusion, job creation and competitiveness. proposals to complete the single market - whether they are about removing administrative barriers for small businesses or encouraging e-commerce - should benefit citizens. the implementation of the strategy must not lead to a reduction in social rights and a reduction in welfare gains. there are still too many obstacles to the exercise of workers', buyers' and consumers' rights without internal borders. the implementation of the free movement directive is not complete, although it is particularly important to increase the number of people working and to manage the european workforce better in order to recover from the crisis. the smooth functioning of the internal market is supported by the implementation of the joint european action against corruption and organised crime and the stockholm programme for an area of freedom, security and justice. we need to take steps to achieve recognition of professional qualifications within the eu, portability of pension rights, access to basic banking services. we also have further work to do in ensuring the free flow of services and respect for the rights of posted workers.'
mr president, the officer! you guys! nuclear safety is a human face! the design, construction, operation, inspection, closure and regulation of a power plant are all human activities. so the most important element of security is knowledge, the maximum use of human activity. maintaining and applying this knowledge is essential both when operating power plants and long after power plants are shut down. i agree to do the stress test, and i agree to incorporate the experience into the nuclear safety directive. i agree with the idea of cross-border checks, if for no other reason than to reassure the public. i agree that we should do our utmost to contribute to international organisations with the best of our knowledge. i particularly agree that we should do r&amp;d to get rid of the nuclear waste of the last 60 years. and one thing i particularly agree with is that whatever we decide on energy, we must insist that 500 million europeans have access to electricity. thank you for listening.
in writing. the japanese experience is expected to lead to a number of countries rethinking their nuclear energy strategies. germany has already taken concrete steps to shut down all nuclear power plants built before 1980 for three months. if, as a result of the events in japan, several member states across europe are also rethinking their nuclear energy strategy, this could have a significant impact on european energy markets, as nuclear power plants will have to be replaced by other sources of electricity. i believe that in the current situation, sudden action can have the same harmful consequences as a potential disaster. instead, a deliberate accounting of the effects of each scenario is the most useful. this is the only way to avoid the consequences of the aforementioned measures in germany, which are to increase electricity prices and to avoid the security of supply challenges faced by member states which normally export prices. europe needs to think carefully about the impact of a rethinking of its nuclear strategy on the energy market. it is also not clear which technologies could be suitable to replace nuclear power plant capacity. the impact of a change in strategy on gas markets, security of gas supply and the eu's co2 reduction targets is also an important issue. finally, i would like to welcome the european commission's swift reaction and announcement that european nuclear power plants will be subject to stress tests. the most important question is what further measures europe is planning to take to ensure the safety of nuclear energy production.

in writing. the main objective is to increase the transparency of the eib's activities for the ep and to make better use of the financial instruments it provides. we recommend that consideration be given to the proposal to extend prudential supervision to the eib in order to accurately measure the quality and performance of its financial position and to ensure compliance with the rules of efficient and effective business practice. i would like to make it clear that we are not proposing this because we have doubts about the regularity of the eib's activities, but because we generally consider that, as the g20 london declaration made clear two years ago, regulation and supervision should be extended to all systemically important financial institutions, to the asset market. recommend that the european commission be invited to carry out a legal analysis of the possibilities for prudential supervision of the eib for the ep by 30 november 2011. under the current rules, no european institution has the right to supervise the eib. this should be changed urgently, also because of the increased role of the bank and the community guarantee provided to it. the enhanced professional and social controls should be developed in line with the recent practice in other nfis. the eib's lending activities should in future be more selective, efficient and results-oriented, in particular in the area of sme financing. to this end, information on disbursed loans should also be collected and published in a more systematic manner.
in writing. - i welcome the fact that the eib's annual report is being appreciated by the european parliament, albeit from a rather bird's-eye view. the eib is playing a crucial role in overcoming the crisis, but the question of transparency, of measurable results, remains a question of doubt for me. the eib focused its activities on the three areas most affected by the crisis, smes, convergence regions and climate action. smes play a key role in boosting the european economy and fighting unemployment, and it is therefore appropriate to facilitate their access to development capital. in this respect, it would be useful to have an annual assessment of the availability and effectiveness of these loans, which would ensure transparency of the ultimate purpose of the loans and improve the administration process. in this context, i also consider it essential that the eib updates and strengthens its policy on offshore financial centres. the eib plays a very important role in enhancing convergence in the european union, and its technical assistance and co-financing enable regions eligible for the convergence objectives to make use of an increasing proportion of the resources at their disposal, so i consider it appropriate and desirable to further enhance this role. finally, i suggest that consideration be given to the proposal that transparent regulatory oversight should be introduced to ensure the quality of the eib's financial position, the accurate measurement of its results and the observance of the rules of efficient and effective business practice.

the rapporteur. - mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your kind words. as the president has informed us, mr rivellini is the rapporteur for the seven motions on our table. our colleague has worked extremely hard and productively over the past six months, but unfortunately he is not here today and i have the honour of taking his place in today's debate. as i said, the rapporteur for the motion for a resolution, mr rivellini, is a very good one. if i may, i would like to mention the main eu institutions whose work on the proposals for the discharge of the institutions is linked to mr rivellini. the discharge for the european council, the economic and social committee, the committee of the regions, the european court of justice, the european court of auditors, the ombudsman and the european data protection supervisor and for the management of their respective offices was drawn up by mr rivellini. i would like to thank mr rivellini personally and to thank in particular the european court of auditors, whose work has been essential in the preparation of the proposals on our table. i would also like to thank the members of the committee on budgetary control of the european parliament and their colleagues for the work that has led to today's results. my fellow members of the house of representatives! i would like to speak about two reports in particular. on the one hand, the discharge of the european council and, on the other hand, the discharge report of the european economic and social committee. the issue of the european council's discharge is always a complex one, as the two institutions, the european parliament and the european council, debate the discharge procedure from year to year. let me be clear and unambiguous. the european parliament has a firm and clear position that it has the same right and authority to grant or refuse discharge to the european council as it has to any other union institution. mr rivellini proposes to postpone the granting of the european council's discharge in order to allow for further consultations between the european council and the european parliament on dispute matters concerning the discharge procedure. we followed a similar procedure last year and the year before that. the competent committee of parliament requested a deferral of the council's discharge and the discharge was granted at a later date. this year, although the proposal is similar to the previous one to postpone the discharge , we have made clear progress on the european council discharge procedure, and for that reason i thank and congratulate the hungarian presidency of the european council. as point 8 of the report on the european council's discharge states, the progress mentioned above is due to the positive attitude and efforts of the hungarian presidency. i congratulate the members of the bureau. year after year we see this progress, but it is nowhere near the level that we would like. we welcome the council's recognition that the discharge and the budgetary procedure are two separate processes and should be treated independently. it is also welcome that representatives of the council are present and involved in this exchange of views during the debate on the present proposal for a discharge. unfortunately, the invitation to the committee was not accepted by the european council this year either. they did not participate in the committee discussion of the discharge procedure, nor did they grant access to important documents which would have been essential for granting the discharge, nor did the european council reply to questions put by the parliament's committee. these are the problems that we need to get over. i am confident that, after the postponement of the discharge, there will be a way and an opportunity to reach a final agreement on the issues of the council's discharge procedure between the competent committee of the european parliament, the european parliament and the european council, with a view to many years ahead. finally, i should like to refer to the proposal for a discharge by the european economic and social committee. the problems encountered in the management of the commission are not of such a serious nature as to justify a delay in granting the exemption, and we therefore support granting the exemption. moreover, the management problems identified in the committee's work are not linked to the year following the year of the exemption. my fellow members of the house of representatives! as mr rivellini's deputy, i can therefore say that we shall propose to parliament the resolution contained in the motion for a resolution on the seven motions for resolutions.
the committee on employment and social affairs has adopted an opinion. − as the rapporteur for the committee on employment and social affairs, i would like to call on the committee on budgetary control to make some suggestions and ask it to include them in its report. we acknowledge the commission's efforts to improve financial management, but we regret that, despite the financial crisis, member states are not calling up the european social fund in full. this percentage decreased from 54% in 2007 to 36%. we think we need to make a little more effort here. we would also urge increased efforts to reduce the frequency of reimbursement of ineligible costs and call on the commission to speed up the decision-making processes on financial corrections. the european social fund is very welcome to have focused on the employment strategy, but at the same time, as i said, they have not used all the resources available to them. so here we need to make a further effort. there have been some minor problems with the agencies, and i don't have time to go into that, but on the whole, we have to say that their management is acceptable and supportive.
the rapporteur. - my fellow members of the house. allow me to thank all of you who have contributed valuable contributions to this debate. as i have indicated as rapporteur, and more specifically as rapporteur for mr rivellini, there are seven exemption motions on the table of the european parliament. these seven exemption proposals concern seven very important eu institutions. in his submission, mr rivellini proposes that the european parliament should grant discharge to the economic and social committee, the committee of the regions, the european court of justice, the european court of auditors, the european ombudsman and the european data protection supervisor, as well as to the offices of those institutions. today's debate has also confirmed that there is a consensus in the european parliament on this. one of mr. rivellini's presentations is about the discharge of the european council. in this respect, there is also a consensus among the members of the european parliament, party families, to decide to postpone the discharge. i would like to welcome the remarks made by mr becsey, secretary of state, in today's debate in parliament. i consider important the steps which are linked to the work of the next hungarian presidency. if these steps continue, it will finally be possible, when parliament puts the question of the european council's discharge back on the agenda, perhaps this autumn, for the european parliament to give the european council its discharge by reaching an agreement between the european council and the european parliament on the procedure for the discharge of the european council many years in advance. with these thoughts in mind, i would like to thank once again all those who have contributed to the fact that parliament has been able to take a decision on these seven motions today.
chairman of the committee, madam commissioner, madam minister! the schengen system is one of the union's most tangible achievements. when you ask european citizens, they will mention their freedom of movement without border controls as important to them in the european union. it is therefore essential that we preserve this system and do everything we can to preserve the freedom of movement of our citizens, the freedom of europe to be free of borders. that must remain our primary objective. our values, our achievements, need to be protected, especially when they are challenged. and the challenge today is the sudden arrival of immigration and refugees. but to address these challenges, we need concrete proposals and solutions. it starts with separating, clarifying what we're talking about, separating the refugee issue from illegal immigration, using our existing principles that we can build on, like solidarity, cooperation, using our existing institutions like frontex, or our existing rules. i welcome the hungarian position, the position of state secretary győri, and that mr barroso has also said: the schengen acquis must be strengthened, not withdrawn, it must be made to work well. other

mr. president, fellow members of the house of representatives, madam secretary of state. let me divide the issue of labelling textiles into two important issues. the first important point is that the labelling should indicate the origin and the country of origin of the textile product. the other important issue, of course, is that it should be indicated precisely, precisely, what material the textile product is made of. in both cases, of course, a uniform set of rules is good. well, if we look at the first question, which product comes from where, it's worth starting with the fact that the european union is in crisis, and that's largely because those industries that are related to production, like textiles, have been outsourced to various far eastern countries for easy, quick profit, and therefore the pace of production in the european union has slowed down. we have to find the breaking points for the european union that we can avoid, that we can help to avoid the crisis. the textile industry is one such sector, alongside agriculture, machinery manufacturing and tourism. in the textile sector, european producers are particularly disadvantaged, as far-eastern dumping and their low-priced and poor quality dumped products make it impossible for european producers to make progress. it should be displayed - and we would strongly support it - even in the european union, trusting in the wisdom of the consumer, helping their own economy so that everyone can see, whether a bulgarian, a czech, a hungarian or a german, to buy a product produced in their own country. we need to protect our markets with new technologies and to showcase the quality of materials, alongside rigorous laboratory testing of materials.
the interests of consumers must be given the highest priority when discussing the report. the aim is to ensure that the labels are accurate, since these are the measures that can demonstrate the european union's added value to consumers. the current legislation allows for a derogation. this is a competitive disadvantage for the european union, which means that it is time for a legislative review in line with current market conditions. perhaps the speaker is right that there is insufficient evidence of the possible effects of the hazardous substances used. perhaps he is wrong, and this is a question worth examining more closely. it is also important to assess whether there is a causal relationship between allergic reactions and synthetic fibres, dyes used in textiles. therefore, issues related to quality, origin and traceability should be given greater prominence.

mr. president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, the apparently strict eu legislation on hazardous waste contains many loopholes in practice, which are well exploited by organised crime groups, such as the hazardous waste mafia. today, one of the most lucrative businesses is the illegal disposal of toxic, hazardous wastes. one reason is the outrageously high cost of legal disposal and recycling, and the other is the overbureaucratic nature of obtaining legal permits. the solution must be two-pronged: reduce the administrative burden on businesses that recover and dispose of hazardous waste legally, and, of course, encourage hazardous waste producers to take the legal route, and make them interested in curbing the hazardous waste mafia. on the other hand, the penal sanctions for environmental crime must be radically tightened up and controls uniformly increased at eu level.

author. - mr. president, commissioner, excuse me, i looked up at the board for a moment, because i have the good fortune to have a group of visitors from hungary here. the small business package has recently become part of european thinking. based on the practical experience of the last two years, it is time to review, update and strengthen the small business package. we, the authors of the question, have summarised the most important points in the motion for a resolution in 34 points. i'd like to highlight a few of them. it's all about execution. member states have worked with varying degrees of efficiency and commitment and, as a result, have been effective. we want a unified database and scoreboard to measure success and overreach. officer, please! we believe that the objective is not to violate the rights of workers, but to create new jobs. reducing administration is not the same as curtailing workers' rights, but rather creating opportunities for micro and small enterprises to work together. in the case of access to financial resources, it is very important that the resources available at eu level are used even more successfully. so far, we've achieved an efficiency of 75%, and about 50,000 smes have been funded. at the same time, the competitiveness programme, cip, has been successful in providing funding to around 300 000 smes. we must certainly confirm this, we must preserve it. it is very important that the network of institutions help small and medium-sized enterprises. the european enterprise network should therefore be strengthened. we'd really like to find a way to continue the erasmus programme for young entrepreneurs. we want to ensure that each of the 23 million small and medium-sized enterprises can still provide at least one job. this could make a significant contribution to improving the labour market situation. however, it is very important that the workforce working for smes is better qualified than the main ones. it is particularly important that they are able to participate in the implementation of the green economy programme and acquire digital skills. the most important thing for us is that young people, small and medium-sized entrepreneurs, see entrepreneurship as a win-win rather than a loser.
i agree with the review finding that further measures are necessary. i think it is important that the union has a better overview of how the member state governments are implementing the package of measures. the mid-term evaluation is also important because smes' problems seem to be becoming more and more persistent, including difficulties in obtaining funding, administrative burdens, over-regulation and market access. i also welcome the idea of allowing small businesses to register as european private companies, which would also reduce the administrative burden. at the same time, the implementation of smart regulation is a highly timely issue. it is of particular importance to spread the 'once-only' principle across europe and to examine whether the regulatory framework in a policy area is appropriate and, if not, what needs to be changed.
mr president, currently we are celebrating the first anniversary of the greek bail-out, but there is nothing to celebrate. one year later we are back at square one. instead of lengthening the pain and piling even more burdens on the greek economy, it would be far better to accept the inevitable and the judgment of the markets. there is no way to avoid default. an orderly default has at least five advantages. it would share the loss more fairly between greek taxpayers and external creditors; stop the replacement of private debt by an ever-growing proportion of official debt coming from other countries; offer greece more growth opportunities by alleviating debt service; make it much easier for mrs merkel to ‘sell’ the default to the bundestag, because it would mean supporting german banks rather than greece; and, finally, isolate the debt crisis from the competitiveness crisis and save the euro zone from contagion. do it! do not throw good money after bad.
, in writing. innovation is the successful practical implementation of ideas. we must not let the innovation union remain just an exciting idea, a political goal. we are working in the committee on budgets to integrate the flagship of eu 2020 into the eu strategy with a separate financial framework. the innovation union can play a role in reshaping europe's crisis-hit economy, guided by sustainable job creation. if 3% of eu gdp were indeed earmarked for research and development by 2020, 3.7 million new jobs would be created, increasing annual gdp by approximately €800 million by 2025. we must accept that research and innovation are the key to economic recovery in the union and the only effective means of doing so. we must therefore unanimously support the member states' commitment to spend 1% of their gnp on research and technological development. the willingness to take risks is a prerequisite for successful innovation, but it does not mean risky investment for member states at this time. the return is guaranteed. to maintain europe's competitiveness, we need a shift in thinking, driven by the need to improve europe's innovation potential and boost europe's entrepreneurial spirit. therefore, we have reviewed the small business act (sba) to align it with the single market act, which can also show synergies with the innovation union. we must harmonize our measures, because this will increase efficiency many times over.

it is crucial for an ageing europe that all young people can fulfil their talents, and that all can succeed in the labour market later on. this requires education that addresses the disparities of opportunity from early childhood, with particular attention to ensuring that the benefits of education, vocational training and employment are available to all young people, regardless of their financial situation, social and ethnic background, disability or any other health problem. the 2020 strategy aims to achieve a 40% tertiary level and to reduce school drop-out to 10%. to achieve this, all member states must move in this direction. and in some places, we're seeing the opposite trend now: lowering the age of enrolment, reducing the number of state-funded higher education places. europe's future sustainable growth and innovation can only be guaranteed if everyone contributes to it, if all countries move in the same direction.
, in writing. - my fellow members of parliament! i think it's important that we have a debate on education and culture, and in addition to the many important aspects, i would like to make an approach that has been missing. the basic condition for the harmonious and prosperous coexistence of traditional national communities, minorities and majorities, is mutual knowledge and respect. education plays a vital role in this. if minority history and literature were taught in majority schools in the same way that minority culture is taught in minority schools, it would bring communities closer together. language education also plays an important role in cultural learning. this is true not only in the study of world languages, but also in the relationship between minorities and majorities living together. i think that not only should minorities learn the language of the majority, but it should work the other way around. it would enrich everyone and express respect for one another. at the same time, knowing each other's languages would give the younger generations a much more positive attitude towards each other, and would make it easier for them to connect with their peers, which would be decisive for the future of these regions. thirdly, i think it is necessary to mention the importance of a thorough knowledge of cultural heritage in order to recognize and respect the cultural values of each other in communities living together. i see the incentive for all this as a concrete task for the commission and a strengthening of the principle of political support for parliament.
, in writing. my fellow members of the european parliament! i would like to congratulate my colleague, mr honeyball, on his excellent work in seeing the critical points that have perhaps received less attention in the past. among these, the link between poverty, disadvantage and poor school performance is particularly important. i would remind my colleagues that there is another link: the disadvantage is particularly acute where the child has a disability that is not understood or accepted by the parents who are not sufficiently informed. in my report i shall be paying particular attention to this problem, and the work of my colleague, mr honeyball, and in particular the application of the child-centred approach, will be a great reference point for the future. the draft report 'draws attention to the general importance of pre-school learning, with particular reference to language acquisition, and to multilingualism and linguistic diversity', which i am happy and enthusiastic to support. i myself believe that the early development of children with disabilities, the support of children in acquiring appropriate language competences, is a fundamental and never-returnable investment in human resources. equally important, in order to build the linguistic competence of deaf children, it is necessary to first teach them their mother tongue, sign language, so that they can become truly multilingual. and this is an investment to achieve the eu2020 goals of increasing employment levels in order to create a more accessible, inclusive and sustainable society.

in writing. the seventh framework programme for research is the world's largest research funding instrument, playing a key role in maintaining europe's competitiveness and delivering on the europe 2020 strategy. the union framework programme represents a significant added value in the field of european research and development. however, the mid-term review also highlighted several areas for improvement. in my view, two aspects are critical to the future success of the framework programme. first and foremost, i would like to emphasize the need to simplify administration. the excessive administrative complexity is costly and makes it difficult for smes to participate in the research framework programme. the elimination of parallel structures, the faster mobilisation of funding and the alignment with national priorities will help to attract as many actors as possible to r & d activities. as a second point, i would like to remind you of the importance of the 'excellence' approach. the most effective way to use the financial resources of the framework programme is to select research projects for funding on the basis of excellence. however, the mid-term review revealed that some countries were under-represented in terms of recovery. the main reason for this is that these member states do not have a research infrastructure that would allow them to meet the criteria of excellence. it is therefore necessary to examine the synergies between the framework programme and the structural and cohesion funds that could facilitate the development of lagging infrastructure. this is the way to ensure that all member states are treated equally in the case of a decision on the basis of excellence.
in writing. the recent food safety scandal in germany has once again drawn attention to the shortcomings of appropriate official controls. consumer confidence in the diligence of food producers and, not least, in the professionalism of the control authorities has been shaken again. in a well-functioning rule of law, the consumer has reason to trust that only food that can be put on his table, only food that can be given to his children, can be eaten safely. unfortunately, this is not the first time that trust has been shattered. it is enough to think only of the poisoned tomato scandal in hungary in may, but we could also mention the dioxin pork scandal in germany in january. all these sad events remind us again and again that the state must strengthen official controls and take the strictest possible action against any deficiency or abuse. in no way can citizens be expected to do this work for the state. we can't pretend to be food chemists and have food-testing labs in our homes. instead, the right solution is to punish offenders with the harshest sanctions, not even shying away from closing down commercial units.
madam secretary of state, madam commissioner, my fellow members of parliament. i am pleased that by voting on the coelho report tomorrow, the european parliament is sending a clear signal that it recognises the readiness of romania and bulgaria and supports the accession of these two countries to one of our most important community acquis, the schengen area. to what has meant so much to the countries of eastern and central europe for historical reasons: the possibility of free and unhindered travel. the main message from our debate today to the member states should be that romania and bulgaria fulfil the same accession conditions that the union has expected from the countries that have joined so far. it should be stressed that no more requirements can be imposed on romania and bulgaria than were imposed in the case of the previous schengen accession. member states should not apply the institution of double standards because it lays the foundation on which the schengen acquis is built: the institution of mutual trust. it is precisely the events of the past few months that have shown that europe's most important achievement needs to be defended and upheld. i would also stress that it is not even a coincidence that we should end up as a commodity link in this matter, that is to say, linking the accession of the two member states to schengen and the issue of the review of the schengen evaluation mechanism. the latter is very much needed, but it cannot be connected like this. finally, i would like to congratulate rapporteur coelho, whose constructive and consistent work has been of paramount importance in this case as well, and to thank the hungarian presidency for the concrete steps and commitment it has taken towards the early accession of the two member states to schengen.
in writing. the european commission has assessed that romania and bulgaria are ready to join schengen and that the council should therefore take a decision on the admission of these two member states without delay. we cannot apply double standards, because that would undermine the credibility of the european union, and we cannot introduce new conditions. schengen, the abolition of borders, is the step that citizens value most in the achievements of the european union, which, alongside the euro, is also the symbol of the european union. freedom of movement is also one of the keys to the success of the common market. the need to safeguard the external borders of the union is real, but achieving this does not mean keeping ready-made entrants out, but rather introducing a continuous, transparent schengen evaluation system, which, together with a single european migration policy and a refugee policy based on internal solidarity, provides a solution to many open questions. i therefore ask the council to move forward as quickly as possible in these areas.
this cycle has perhaps the most important meaning in our hands. the european parliament is the first institution to formulate its views on the next seven years, but this does not infringe the commission's right to propose, but it is the only way to avoid our right of unanimity turning into a scandal that would eventually force us to reject the council's proposal. this way, each of our partners knows what the european parliament wants and can incorporate it into their own thinking wherever possible. i would recommend to my colleagues that they read the treaty of lisbon and the report before they turn their thoughts to harsh criticism. the commitment to own resources is no more and no less than what the lisbon treaty requires. i think it is very important to send the message that the european budget should be slightly increased. i'm saying that's a little bit, because that 5% is going to be inflationary, so it's not going to be abnormal growth. it is not that we want to increase agricultural and cohesion spending, but that we should not reduce it. in the 1600s, miklós zrínyi, a croat-hungarian of common identity, said to the turks, "do not harm the hungarian!" i am quoting him: do not harm the agricultural policy, do not harm the cohesion policy, because whoever harms these does not harm only the hungarian, the romanian, the slovak, but the citizens of all european nations. the smaller the voter base behind a politician, the more he appeals to the citizens. i think that we, who have a larger political force behind us, also represent the interests of the citizens and therefore say: do not harm the agricultural policy and cohesion!
mr. president, i have to go. the crisis forces us to review both national and eu budgets in terms of how effective they are, how well they serve our objectives, and above all, how much added value they bring, european added value. i would like to speak about an area which has not yet been discussed, and that is the one which the treaty of lisbon actually introduces into community policies on the area of the competences of the member states, on internal and judicial cooperation. this should also be reflected in the next budget period, where we want to make real progress. we must ensure that the stockholm programme is funded, it is a very ambitious programme. europe needs well-educated, well-regulated immigrants, a common migration policy with adequate resources. the events in north africa have re-emphasised the importance of a common european refugee policy and the need for resourced integration and european solidarity. the last few months have also brought home to us that the defence of our common values, the issue of non-discrimination, the issue of human rights also need protection and resources, that we need to simplify the use of resources for emergency management and that all of this needs to be done with our own resources in order to have a better, stronger and more europe in the period ahead.
in writing. the draft report, supported by eight committees, takes as its starting point the challenges to be addressed by the new multiannual financial framework. the current crisis is a final warning to europe to respond to the changing world order. as with all transformations, the emerging order will produce new winners and losers. europe has no place among the losers! jean monnet once said that 'europe never existed, we must create it'. this task was not seen by the father of european integration as a one-off event, but as a continuous process. the european union must therefore be able to take action to ensure that the european union is not reduced to the status of a loser, by taking account of the specific circumstances and the conditions that are likely to arise. last year, the commission presented the europe 2020 strategy, which aims to overcome the crisis and prepare europe's economy for the next decade. the three key areas for economic growth are 'smart growth' focusing on knowledge, innovation, education and the digital society, 'sustainable growth' promoting more efficient use of resources and enhancing competitiveness, and 'inclusive growth' enhancing labour market participation. the new multiannual financial framework of the union should be defined at community level and the individual national development programmes should be aligned in order to achieve the best possible implementation of the 2020 strategy, creating the necessary synergies.
in writing. when designing the next budgetary framework, the first question we should ask is: do we want more or less europe? i believe that parliament has already taken a position on this matter. we have already said once, with the adoption of the ambitious goals of the europe 2020 strategy, that we do need more europe. we must now say loud and clear that these objectives can only be achieved if we allocate the budget to meet them. at a time when several eu member states are suffering from the 'mediterranean dilemma', that is, needing fiscal expansion to kick-start their economic growth but not being able to do so because of debt problems that force them into indefinite austerity, our response cannot be to reduce or leave the eu's budget, which is essentially for development, in place. we cannot do that, because that would deprive the regions in decline of their only chance of getting their economies back on track.
in writing. − i would like to welcome this report which draws attention to the necessity of broadening the system of vocational training in the eu since equal access to quality lifelong learning means better chances in the labour market and thereby contributes to fulfilment of the goals of the eu’s 2020 strategy. special emphasis must be put on preventing youth unemployment, for which it is necessary to harmonise training supply with labour-market demand, provide middle-term national and regional forecasts on expected labour-demand and enable national education systems to be more effective in guaranteeing the attainment of those skills that are actively sought on the labour market. according to cedefop we can expect a 12 million decline in jobs available for low skilled or unskilled labour, which presages an increasing risk of poverty for the most vulnerable. in many cases, even those who finish school are unable to enter the labour market and therefore are more likely to engage themselves in casual work or work in the black economy. consequently, they are unable to start a life on their own and often become an unbearable burden on households, creating a vicious circle of unsatisfactory education and joblessness.

i thank the rapporteurs, the advisers, the hungarian presidency and all those who have made it possible for the six-pack of economic governance to be before us today in this form. it would be nice to think that this package is no longer necessary and that europe's economic problems are solved in one fell swoop. the reality is quite different. europe is not out of the crisis, and if we do not act responsibly, it may face further crises. i agree with those responsible thinkers who say that fiscal austerity and the stability and growth pact should be strengthened. we must say that the european union has been too resourceful, lacking a crisis mechanism, and consequently unable to respond adequately to the serious consequences of the crisis, which has caused economic, financial and social disruption. we can't afford to make that mistake again, but we must learn from our past failures.
on behalf of the s&d group. we welcome the report on behalf of the socialists, the result of long debates, consultations, negotiations. we will support it unanimously tomorrow. for 60 years, we've been using nuclear technology to generate energy, to make medicine, to operate submarines or icebreakers. for 60 years, we have not been able to provide effective solutions for the management and long-term storage of hazardous waste. the directive reduces political obstacles, creates financial conditions and independence, which is why we support it, even if we do not agree with all its elements. although we have different views on energy production, we agree that the principle of 'polluter pays' means that waste generated in the european union must be stored here even if it can be reprocessed in a third country. we support the reduction of waste to a minimum and, if the results of r&d allow, the possibility of further recycling, which is why the principle of recyclability is of paramount importance to us. we're very focused on the human factor. we must guarantee this, and this is closely linked to the safety of workers, which must also be a top priority during waste disposal, but we also think it is important to mention that the current legal basis does not provide a sufficient european solution, since we do not have the right to co-decide, and that is why we think it is important to look for a new legal basis in the future. and finally, europe must lead both in regulation and in the construction of storage facilities. it's the only way he can set an example for the rest of the world.
while the european union is working on how to store and bury so-called spent fuel cells, the united states and russia are looking at them as economical energy sources. the fourth generation of molten salt reactors can be fuelled simply by the spent fuel from today's reactors. the prospects for using a proton accelerator with a lead bismuth target are even greater. the high-activity and long-life isotopes produced in any nuclear power plant are essentially harmless: they can be converted into low-activity, short-life elements, while the equipment is capable of generating power. another huge advantage is that they also operate thorium, of which the world's available supply is ten times that of uranium at present. they're powered by a fraction of the critical mass of the fissile material, so there's no way that a spontaneous chain reaction could have occurred. so here's another opportunity to break away from the world's leading research and development and economic position unless we start to see the introduction and application of new technology right away.
mr. commissioner, ladies and gentlemen of the house. radioactive waste is produced in all eu member states, so in order to protect citizens and the environment, it is necessary to ensure not only the spent fuel from nuclear power plants, but also the safe and efficient management of radioactive waste in general. it is therefore very appropriate that this issue should be discussed here today in the european parliament. i agree that the use of nuclear energy is a national responsibility. however, the formulation of eu principles on national waste management rules has a place. in the absence of such a system, the divergent rules have led to the interest of operators in having their radioactive waste treated in the countries with the most permissive rules. in view of all this, the initiative of member states to establish mandatory waste management programmes is a good one. however, this requires the designation of competent authorities that strictly supervise nuclear waste management activities and, where appropriate, are able to sanction violations appropriately. in my view, the national programmes should also clarify the scope of the authority's activities and the responsibilities of the market players. finally, the total management of spent fuel and radioactive waste is a very costly activity. without clarification of the funding issues, therefore, national programmes are worthless. it is an important principle that the necessary financial resources are earmarked for the management of nuclear waste at the time of its generation. i agree that the costs of waste management should be borne by the producer of waste. this provides a proper incentive for operators to reduce and manage radioactive waste effectively.

on behalf of the ecr group. – mr president, the greek state is technically bankrupt. the relevant question is no longer whether it needs debt forgiveness, but when. the sooner the better. european institutions are failing spectacularly to understand the nature of the problem. it is not a liquidity crisis but a deep solvency crisis, reflecting the lack of international competitiveness of the greek economy. piling more debt upon the greek taxpayer is not only useless but completely counterproductive. for greece to start to grow out of the fiscal crisis, part of the debt burden should be forgiven and absorbed by creditors, public and private alike. french, german and especially greek banks will have to be recapitalised. having purchased a huge amount of greek government bonds, and also having accepted much as collateral, the ecb is now trapped. twenty years ago, when transition started in the former communist countries, one of the most important structural reforms was the establishment of an independent central bank with strict legal prohibition of the monetisation of public debt. the ecb has basically been following the practice of communist countries. there is clearly a conflict of interest in its mandate, and now the euro zone is suffering the consequences. it is in exactly this context that we have to look at the budget for 2012. it is an illusion to expect any growth in member state contributions for the next year, in the light of the enormous burden that taxpayers are likely to assume either way. the letter from the presidents and prime in writing. i welcome the fact that the european commission will allocate more resources to strengthening the area of freedom, security and justice in the draft budget for next year. however, i am not sure about the commitments that can be translated into dealing with the consequences of unforeseen events, such as the increased immigration following the north african uprisings, or the framework for partnership initiatives. the european union must respond effectively to the changes in north africa and be better prepared to deal with unexpected events. the crisis may place a greater burden on the common budget than planned. it is right that the commission should allocate more resources to the protection of the external borders, but in parallel the framework of the european asylum fund and the funds for integration should be expanded. it is in the interest of all eu citizens to fully implement the charter of fundamental rights and to strengthen the eu's human rights dimension. we are all concerned about the rise of discrimination and the continuing spread of intolerance throughout europe. i therefore consider it important to respect fundamental rights and to extend the programmes for strengthening eu citizenship.

in writing. cohesion policy has proved its indispensability, effectiveness and flexibility in the fight against the crisis. it also makes a significant contribution to the achievement of the eu2020 objectives, although it is important to stress that cohesion policy is not the only means of achieving these objectives, and that all policy areas must contribute to an appropriate extent to the success of the union's new growth strategy. i would like to emphasise that the error rate has decreased significantly in recent years and that most errors come from areas outside cohesion policy (e.g. public procurement). cohesion policy funding should not be reduced because imbalances remain and only cohesion policy can address them. the poorest regions need more support, but in return they must be more productive. at the same time, it is important that the conditions applied in the field of regional policy are genuinely linked to areas affected by cohesion policy and that member states are not required to implement fundamental social and economic reforms in the name of conditionality. to increase the effectiveness of cohesion policy, it is essential to simplify the current highly complex regulatory system, to provide a uniform interpretation of rules and obligations, to make the regulatory environment predictable and to clearly define responsibilities. cohesion policy is not an aid policy but an investment in the upscaling of less developed areas, with positive consequences for richer regions as demand increases.
in writing. − i would like to welcome the report of mr. stavrakakis on the state of play and future synergies for increased effectiveness between the erdf and other structural funds. greater flexibility of the funds, the simplification of their administration and strategic coordination between instruments, policies and actors is a long-standing demand and a necessary tool for the social inclusion of marginalised groups within a complex policy framework of integrated policies, making use of all the eu financial resources available under the eu funds, and in particular under the erdf, esf and eafrd. the amendment of the regulation governing the erdf extending the eligibility of housing interventions in favour of marginalised communities enables member states to revise their operational programmes and re-prioritise their investments, by using a maximum of 2% of their total erdf allocation for housing interventions in favour of marginalised communities for both replacement and renovation. member states must make full use of this opportunity to complement their efforts to ensure the effective social inclusion of the most vulnerable and the european commission should come forward with a specific action plan on this regulation to speed up the use of funds and to prepare a report on their utilisation.
first of all, i would like to thank the rapporteur, mr langen, for the enormous amount of work he has done on the draft report. i agree that the precise, uniform regulation of otc transactions is a pressing issue, especially after the european financial supervisory systems have finally been established. i would go further: the form of transactions should be regulated in such a way that they are transparent to all supervisory institutions, national authorities and financial operators on the basis of uniform standards. the commission proposes to make otc derivatives trading mandatory and to involve ccps in the clearing of transactions. the additional capital requirement is unavoidable because the possible practice of banks investing in high-risk investments to keep profits and possibly to raise capital from depositors' money cannot be tolerated. the commission's proposal, which affects the us rules in several respects, is acceptable. however, it is worth considering where the reporting obligation for trade and trade repositories is better placed. should it be an obligation of the counterparties or is it more efficient for the ccp to do so? so the question is, where would these data stores be, and what exactly would they look like? a system could be envisaged that would create a lobby register-like system to aggregate the information provided to the data warehouse into a harmonised system according to a single standard. i wish this could happen in europe. the same principle could even apply to the re-registration of ccps. i think it would be appropriate and important for us to reach agreement on this important matter at first reading.
mr. stolojan's report is a fine piece of work, and more than timely. the problems raised suggest that, given the globalised nature and integration of the financial sector, the supervisory methods and procedures previously applied need to be brought into line with the risks arising in complex and extensive financial interconnected systems. i agree that the high level of contagion of financial conglomerates and the disruption of the system will determine the situation of the financial sector in the long term. i am in favour of amending the directive to restore confidence and of drawing up guidelines for transparency. and the stress tests that we're proposing can point to weaknesses, so i think risk exposure is going to decrease in the near future.

on behalf of the ppe group. madam president, i'm sorry to hear that. our resolutions on internal security issues reflect our core european values faithfully, always expressing a balance between security and freedom. it is worth pointing out that security is as much a fundamental right of our citizens as data protection. indeed, profiling is often viewed with hysterical fury by many as a diabolical tool, but do we know what exactly profiling and data mining mean? do we really know what we're talking about? it is essential that the commission should define the necessary definition - and that this should be done with scientific rigor - since the fight against terrorism and organised crime in its present form is unlikely to be successful with conventional law enforcement tools alone. i would point out, however, that in the member states' own criminal law enforcement, profiling is a widely accepted and very effective tool, without any particular problems. furthermore, it is inevitable that the eu's own pnr issue will be addressed in the context of the pnr agreements to be concluded with third countries. the union, its member states and the aviation industry would benefit from a centralised functioning of the union pnr system. if we really want to be reciprocal in the transmission of information - that is, not just giving but also receiving information from third countries - then we must create here in the union the single european organisational framework that is really able to receive and process data, ensuring equal access to information for the member states. the solution - though we have heard different versions - is yet to be found. it may be worth considering whether it would not be most effective to give europol competences that would enable it to play a leading role in the operation of the eu pnr mechanism. i suggest that we consider the options in the negotiations on europol's future mandate.
mr. president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, the exchange of pnr data is not a matter of data protection cooperation but of data protection interference. it's simply insulting that the united states of america considers europe to be incapable of screening out criminal elements. it's extremely dangerous to release the data of european union citizens, especially under the circumstances that the united states of america can keep even the most intimate, most trusted information for 15 years. i would like to ask you who will decide who is a terrorist or a suspected terrorist in europe? and my other question is, what are we going to get in return if this deal, god forbid, happens? the other important point is that the european union should wake up to the fact that it is an adult and change its style to involve only those countries that are on a parity basis, whether it be the united states, canada or australia.
mr. president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, seven years of hungary's membership of the european union is enough to make a social balance. in 2004, they drugged the hungarians with the idea that if we joined the european union, then hungarians could open a confectionery shop in vienna. they put it up on the billboards. well, the reality of this contrast is that seven years have passed and hungarian residents earn 340 euros a month, while in western europe they earn 1,000 to 1,200 euros. hungarian teachers have salaries between 300 and 400 euros, pensioners have an average pension of 200-250 euros, while in western europe they can earn eight to ten times as much, and yes, about 300 per year in hungary freeze in the 21st century, and that we can not open a candy factory in vienna, but there is no sugar factory left in hungarian hands in hungary. the reality is that eastern europe was needed by western europe not in the spirit of unification with a great european ideal, but in terms of markets and cheap labour.
the development of economic relations between the union and china is of paramount importance. but, in addition, the different approaches to human and minority rights issues must be openly discussed, as the british and german heads of government have done. unfortunately, the hungarian prime minister has failed to do so and, contrary to the eu's common position, viktor orbán has already called china a strategic ally, a country that vigorously upholds human rights, restricts freedom of speech and the press, and seriously violates the rights of tibetans and other minorities. can china be considered a strategic ally on these issues as well? during the chinese prime minister's visit to hungary, the foreign police authorities brutally prevented a peaceful demonstration by the tibetan minority. this was protested by several meps, including jozsef szájer, gál kinga, gyürk andrás from fidesz. the credibility of their exemplary protest would have been supported if they had also expressed their reservations when the hungarian media law and the new hungarian constitution were adopted.
in writing. help in emergencies is an integral part of the single market and the freedoms it offers. the 112 emergency number was introduced in my country in 2006 with the aim of making emergency services more accessible. i am proud to say that the hungarian government has committed itself to the full reception of the 112 emergency call, which means that from the end of 2012 through the improvements from 19 million euros, the existing numbers will be phased out. now, many of you may be wondering, why 2012? i believe that the technical implementation and the awareness of a number to be embedded in the public consciousness should not be a short-term process. technical expertise is particularly important. in the 21st century, europe cannot afford to have a single it infrastructure for emergency services or to have no data search or location system, since such a system would not only promote but also make it more difficult to organise assistance with a single approach. the aim and content of the report are therefore to be welcomed. it's time to make the public aware that in any emergency, they should call 911. all member states should continue to be asked to do their utmost to ensure that 112 becomes a fully reliable service available throughout europe.
in writing. - my fellow members of parliament! the key energy challenge of the decade is to guarantee affordable, secure and sustainable energy for all european citizens. however, we cannot expect to achieve a breakthrough unless we have an efficient energy infrastructure with adequate flexibility. i agree that diversification should be a top priority for the european union. as the report emphasises, the energy density of the energy transmission networks in the countries of south-eastern europe is not adequate. it is in europe's interest that alternative supply and transit routes reduce the region's unilateral vulnerability, increase its market liquidity and allow for proper competition between different energy sources. in this light, i consider it a great step forward that the report clearly identifies the criteria on the basis of which a priority investment can also count on community funds. the criteria include diversification of transit routes and sources, market integration and increased liquidity, elimination of energy islands and integration of renewable energy into the grid. i agree that socio-economic factors should be taken into account in the assessment in addition to purely economic indicators. finally, i would like to turn to the sources of funding. the commission estimates that eur 200 billion of investment is needed in the energy transmission sector alone by 2020. eu funds are expected to cover only a fraction of the total financing needs, so it is essential to create a stable regulatory environment and new innovative financing instruments to stimulate private sector investment.
