mr. president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, in the european union, certain economic lobby groups are constantly trying to create the impression that europe must choose between quality and quantity production. europe has such exceptionally good natural resources that it is possible to produce both quality and quantity without the need for gmo technology. in the explanatory statement for the report, i would like to quote two important points. one is that no serious surveillance studies have been carried out yet and the other is that control systems are not in place. under these circumstances, how do we want to promote or support gmo technology? the possibility of using gmos should not be left to the discretion of the member states but should be prohibited until the production of gmos is safe.

in writing. − in my extraordinarily brief speech, i would like to explain why i voted no to the motion for a resolution, tabled by the left-wing and liberal political groups, on the subject of the revised hungarian constitution. there are several reasons for this, but i will highlight only two of them, as follows: (1) the resolution states that the hungarian constitution 'creates a legal basis for actions which neighbouring countries may interpret as interference in their internal affairs' (paragraph i). (2) the resolution calls on the hungarian authorities to explicitly guarantee in the constitution, including the preamble, that hungary will respect the territorial integrity of other countries... (see paragraph). i firmly reject tendentious and insinuating formulations reminiscent of the political discourse of the national communist regimes that have passed through history, namely the doctrine of non-interference of the ceauşescu dictatorship in romania, and i consider them offensive both to hungary and to the hungarian national communities living in neighbouring countries. the hungarian basic law expresses hungary's responsibility towards hungarian communities in foreign countries, which continue to suffer severe discrimination, and does not pose any threat to the territorial integrity of the countries concerned. i also strongly condemn the initiative taken by the socialist meps göncz kinga and tabajdi csaba in adopting the european parliament resolution, which borders on betrayal of hungarian national interests.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your kind words. let the first word be praise. the commission has been grinding between two millstones in its attempt to reconcile, and has done so skillfully, the need for funding for the common european objectives supported by the european parliament and the member states' budgetary consolidation objectives. let me highlight some of the elements that seem to be good at the beginning, at the first approach. unlike some of my colleagues, i am pleased that agricultural subsidies and cohesion remain the two most important items in the budget. i'm glad to see more money going to small businesses, research, and cross-border cooperation. i'm glad that the reimbursement systems are getting simpler and they're going back a little bit. i think it is a step in the right direction and i am pleased that the commission has had the courage to start reforming the revenue side. but i cannot be happy that the internal ratios in cohesion policy have been shifted in the wrong direction. the share of the amounts allocated to convergence regions is significantly reduced in favour of the more developed regions. reducing the ceiling for cohesion support will be a major problem for many countries, and more developed member states will be less affected, thus at the expense of less developed member states. the commission proposal does not deal sufficiently with the problems of large cities, the capital cities. the level of agricultural subsidies is falling significantly, although the details are not yet known. and there is a danger that a piece of legislation which parliament has the right to adopt unanimously may not contain elements which require a codecision procedure. finally, colleagues, everyone is talking about the crisis. we're looking at seven years. we're going to be in recession for seven years? we're going to get out of this. do not judge the possibilities of the next period by the problems of today or last year.
my fellow members of the house of representatives! the result of the negotiations on the directive on food labelling was disappointing. the commission is proposing to make it compulsory to indicate the country of origin of all fresh meat products, eggs and honey. i am very sorry that the proposal to make the marking of traffic lights and the daily recommended intake mandatory has been rejected. consumer and health organizations believe that this label would have helped consumers eat healthier. it would also have been better to inform consumers by labelling on the front of the packaging, as proposed by glenis willmott and the socialists. it is not clear why the right wing opposed the interests of the liquor lobby to prevail, when it would have been extremely important to indicate the energy content of alcoholic beverages. that would have been a healthier diet.
i agree with the excellent report by mrs dagmar roth-behrendt that we must overcome our previous fears. the european parliament, two months ago, in the context of the protein deficit, has already accepted, on the basis of the opinion of the environment committee, that we should review the ban on animal protein sources for non-ruminants. i fully agree that with the current health regulations, there is no reason why non-reproductive animals should not be able to get animal protein. this must be resolved as soon as possible and this has been confirmed in today's debate, and many of my colleagues have said, and i can only confirm, that there is such a degree of food shortage in europe, such a degree of vulnerability, that we are importing genetically modified feed from outside the union from the united states, which is also why we must accept the proposal of mr roth-berendt.
in writing. - mr president, i have to go. many may recall the smallpox epidemic of the late 1990's, which was caused by feeding bse-infected cattle animal protein. in response to the devastating epidemic, the eu created the so-called tse regulation in 2001, which banned the use of animal protein in food-producing animals. thanks to strict measures and regular monitoring programmes, the incidence of infectious spongiform encephalopathies has decreased significantly from 2167 positive cases in 2001 to 67 in 2009. in view of the more favourable epidemiological situation, it is therefore appropriate to consider relaxing the feed ban on animal protein. we must also take account of the significant protein deficit within the union, which means that two thirds of europe's protein feed needs have to be met by imports. the use of certain animal protein, not produced from ruminants, could reduce the demand for soybean imports, mainly from south america, and thus make european livestock farming more profitable. despite all these arguments, i think it is important to stress that the ban can only be relaxed if other measures provide adequate guarantees that a situation similar to that of the 1990s will not reoccur in europe. although technical progress allows for some changes, the current ban can only be mitigated by adhering to strict safety standards, such as the exclusion of cannibalism, the use of reliable testing procedures and strict separation of production lines.
in writing. i welcome the european commission's proposal to allow the use of certain animal proteins as feed, subject to strict food safety requirements. europe currently produces less than a third of its protein feed needs, with the rest to be met by imports, mainly from south american soya. this not only increases the vulnerability of european livestock farmers, but also raises serious environmental and climate concerns. the growing demand for soybeans is spurring growers in south america to cover more and more land with soybeans. and the only way to increase agricultural land is to destroy the rainforest. more than 10% of the eu's arable land would be needed to fully replace protein imports. it is therefore clear that in the present circumstances, increasing the production of european protein crops alone will not eliminate dependence. moreover, in most parts of the continent, the climate does not allow the production of the most competitive protein crop, soybeans. at the same time, the use of animal protein could reduce the demand for soybean imports and thus make european livestock production more sustainable and profitable.
in writing. the report by my colleague pascual de grandes was extremely timely. i hope this report sends a strong signal to the european commission about the content of the legislative proposal. i would like to stress that it is of the utmost importance that this should be made compulsory in order to take account of the special situation of passengers and their fair and individual treatment. thus, passengers in a vulnerable health or communication situation, such as pregnant women, children, elderly persons, persons with disabilities, persons with implanted medical devices, or passengers carrying in their hand luggage medical devices and medicines necessary to maintain their health, should be given special treatment when using body scanners. i therefore consider it necessary to draw up a european code of conduct on the use of body scanners, covering not only treatment but also the health effects of body scanners and the consideration of human rights. i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on her initiative.

in writing. hungary has not just handed the reins of leadership to another member state, but to a friendly country. poland is embarking on an ambitious presidency programme for the challenging six months ahead, which deserves our full support. i agree with the polish prime minister who warned in his first statements about the dangers of a new euroscepticism. we know that leading politicians who say they are committed to integration in brussels, make decisions and statements that are radically opposed to it at home. like donald tusk, i myself am concerned about the double standard, the aggressive pursuit of national interests at the expense of communities. i appreciate that, after a series of failed initiatives, the polish government is finally looking to boost relations with our eastern neighbours, with the continuation of the enlargement process and the creation of energy security as its main objectives. unlike its predecessor, poland has managed to get its finance minister to attend eurogroup meetings as an observer, which i consider particularly important for strengthening coordination between the member states participating in and not participating in monetary union. i also welcome the fact that the polish government is organising a conference on the long-term eu budget with the involvement of all those concerned, including the european parliament. i hope that the parliamentary elections in poland in the autumn will not interfere with the work of the next presidency. we know from experience how much of a disadvantage it is when domestic events overshadow the work of the presidency.
in writing. the polish presidency of the union is taking place at a difficult time. the post-lisbon institutional framework is still being shaped and a reassuring solution to the effects of the crisis must also be found. it is good to see that the polish/cypriot trio's programme pays particular attention to gender equality even in these difficult circumstances. the programme states that the principle of equal opportunities is essential in strategies for economic growth and prosperity and identifies specific issues (e.g. reconciliation of work and family life, women's employment and educational status, maternity leave, violence against women) where the presidencies want to make progress. it is important, however, that the presidency should also take care to ensure that the equal opportunities approach is applied horizontally, covering all policies. with regard to the chapter on poverty in the programme of the trio presidency, i would like to ask whether the presidency intends to give special attention to the situation of female pensioners who are most at risk of poverty. i also hope that the presidency will make the necessary efforts to bring the negotiations on the maternity leave directive to a successful conclusion, as the proposal has so far failed to be adopted because of opposition from the member states. i am very pleased that the polish presidency intends to act as an integrating presidency. this is something that europe needs very much today. i hope that the integration of women's interests will not remain a matter of decision.
we are discussing one of the most important reports of recent times, and the european parliament is the first institution to give a detailed opinion on the causes of the crisis, the ways out and how we can avoid another crisis, and what the 'more europe' we have been talking about a lot lately means. the decisions to strengthen the euro area are slowly and laboriously being made, and it is good that parliament has laid down its ideas in this respect. we cannot allow internal imbalances to undermine the european project. we must uphold our principles of cooperation and solidarity within the euro area and outside the euro area. the berès report stresses the importance of preserving the welfare acquis and the social assistance system, which are also economic stabilisers. the objectives set out in the 2020 strategy can only be achieved by providing adequate financial resources, and it is therefore important that the report calls for a significant increase in the eu budgetary resources in the long term.
mr. president, ladies and gentlemen of the house of representatives, the movement for the right of hungary (jobbiks) already on april 11 this year initiated that the hungarian parliament condemn the armenian aggression in nagorno-karabakh in 1992-93, and that hungary initiate the international community to put the hitherto unresolved nagorno-karabakh conflict on the agenda. as a result of the soviet armenian army's attack, azerbaijan lost 20% of its territory, 20,000 civilians were killed and about 1 million people were displaced. the armenian genocide in nagorno-karabakh was one of the most forgotten genocides of the 1990s. it is important that the european union, which is so sensitive to human rights, does not look selectively and does not apply double standards when it looks at a series of national tragedies. as a representative of magra, i would like to assure the azerbaijani people that we follow their fate with brotherly love and trust, and at the same time we call on everyone to exercise restraint in relation to azerbaijan.
mr president, prior to the kazan summit of 24 june the international community expressed high hopes that a peace deal between armenia and azerbaijan would be concluded on the nagorno-karabakh region, populated for centuries by christian armenians and muslim azeris who lived intermingled in the south caucasus, coexisting under different foreign rules. sadly no agreement was reached. instead, all international hope was destroyed, leading to the further frustration of two broken nations and a powerless community to mediate. to avoid further suffering, the eu cannot linger any longer and let other actors in the region, such as russia, shade the process according to their strategic influence. we have to continue to pressurise and support both countries in a balanced way so that they will adopt the basic principles and we must help these two nations with european aspirations build peace through reconciliation. the time to resolve the humanitarian crisis has never been more pressing, so that the hundreds of thousands of refugees and internally displaced people can return to their homes in dignity. the only solution for real and sustainable peace is dialogue and compromise, together with a shift in thinking, so that all actors reflect on the needs of others and the suffering of their people. peace came to the balkans. it is high time that peace also came to the neighbouring south caucasus.
mr president, as is very clear from this debate the nagorno-karabakh dispute has been frozen for a long time but there is now a serious danger that it could unfreeze and trigger off a major crisis. we know the background: at the end of the war between azerbaijan and armenia in 1994, armenia occupied around a fifth of azerbaijan’s territory and made about a million people refugees. but there are other actors as well to complicate matters: russia, turkey, iran too, all have interests in this area and could become involved if the karabakh conflict were to erupt again. in addition azerbaijan has used the money from its energy wealth to acquire a major quantity of modern weaponry; armenia has likewise made military preparations. last month, as we know, russia attempted yet another mediation between the two parties in kazan but this failed. the level of trust between the two antagonists is too low. now the outcome is frustration in baku, and frustration can be a bad counsellor, though it is understandable when a sizeable part of one’s territory is under foreign occupation. azerbaijan’s disappointment at the inaction of the world, and of the west in particular, is further indicated by its recent decision to join the non-aligned movement. now an escalation of the conflict – maybe by accident on the ceasefire line – cannot be excluded; that is in no one’s interests and further mediation should be pursued urgently. i agree with those who have argued in favour of an eu involvement.
first of all, i would like to thank mrs ashton for her letter to me the other day in connection with my speech on 11 may. in the context of the elections in russia on 4 december, i would like to stress the importance of establishing the status of eu election observers. in march of this year, i myself attended the regional elections as an observer at the invitation of the russian civil society organization civil control. on this occasion, i was able to speak with the chairman of the russian electoral commission, viktor tsurov, who informed me that there is currently no agreement between the two countries on the sending of election observers. i therefore consider it very important that the osce negotiations be concluded successfully and that the european parliament's representatives be able to participate officially in the elections in russia. i would also ask commission president fleckenstein to make sure that this question is put to the russian negotiating partner at the next meeting of the commission in warsaw in september.
i agree with officer šefčovič. schengen is indeed the most important acquis of the european union, next to the euro. it also has a symbolic meaning and is for many people the most visible sign of the european union's existence. the european union needs trust to function effectively, and to maintain that trust we need to find solutions to the problems that address the real causes. the unilateral introduction of border security is not like that. the appropriate response to the wave of migration is to strengthen the external borders and to develop a common migration and asylum policy. the fight against crime is particularly helped by schengen cooperation. temporary reintroduction of border controls can only be accepted by a community decision and regulated only under article 77 of the treaty, in the framework of a co-decision procedure with the full involvement of the european parliament.
in writing. - my fellow members of parliament! in view of the foregoing, many are outraged that some european union countries have temporarily reinstated border controls. they feel that this violates one of the most fundamental principles of the european union. however, it is not the fault of these member states but of the leadership of the union that they have done so. this step is a consequence. the eu's external borders are weak, and as a result, they are unable to stem the tide of immigration. we must stop the immigration wave, we must reduce crime, so that the people of europe can feel safe in their own country again! thank you for your attention.
on behalf of the ppe group. – madam president, i wish to thank the commissioner for the very clear exposition of the commission’s position. my particular thanks also to andrew duff, as cooperation on this report has been excellent. one of the proposals in the report on direct elections to the european parliament has turned out to be unusually challenging. there is considerable opposition to the idea of a pan-european list which would represent the european interest and strengthen the european identity. my position is that this controversy deserves further exploration. after all, there is generally a parliamentary majority that supports the broad project of european integration, yet the pan-european list, which is certainly about just such integration, is proving unacceptable to many. my sense of it is that opposition to the list derives primarily from those who place the short term before the long term. it is true that in the short term europe faces an economic crisis of far-reaching proportions, the future of the euro is far from assured and instruments to resolve the crisis are still being elaborated. those who focus on the short term will say that this is no time to be experimenting with a pan-european list – yet. in reality the case for the opposite position is stronger. whatever form the resolution of the crisis will take, it will demand more european integration, not less. that will unquestionably mean more transfers of competences and powers to the european union. the power flows so transferred will require representation – that is the heart of democracy – and will demand that representation at european level. this is precisely what the pan-european list wants to achieve: to represent europe and to represent the citizens of europe, which is why concentrating on the short term is short-sighted and futile.
the duff report acknowledges and confronts the fact that the eu institutions - including the european parliament - are irrelevant, opaque institutions in the eyes of the majority of europeans, but that they only add to the chaos instead of solving it. he's dealing with election systems that are barely understood by the average person, and mathematics and in-house bargaining. the point is that european parties, which devour a huge part of the parliamentary budget, which go by different names, but are essentially globalist, neoliberal, and want to abolish nation states, suddenly started a feverish reform of electoral law so that the patriotic, anti-centralization forces could not, or only very difficultly, start in the european parliament elections in 2014. the report's biggest flaw is that, while leaving the current system unchanged, which does not even guarantee the proportional representation of each state, it is expanded with a transnational list, that is, ordinary citizens, who vote less and less from cycle to cycle because they hardly understand and see the benefits of the ep, its activities are hardly followed, will also be able to vote for international lists, so-called european parties. do you seriously think that a german, english or hungarian voter who is largely unknown to them, who does not know their problems, will vote for spanish, french or finnish names in 2014? do you believe it is possible for nearly 500 million europeans in 27 countries to campaign in person? it is almost certain that the confusion will increase and that even more citizens will avoid the european elections.

the stoa panel in the european parliament has chosen the management of natural resources as the theme of its annual general meeting this year, on my initiative. i am very pleased to hear mr bütikofer's excellent report, because this is indeed one of the greatest challenges facing europe in the 21st century: what will happen to natural resources, including raw materials? the buttikofer report is about the fact that we have to focus very much on recycling. and you can see this not just in raw materials, but in water. in israel, it's 75%, in europe, spain is the highest, 25%. but i want to draw your attention to the fact that we have no illusions about recycling. when the red mud was poured in hungary, that was the tragedy. scientists then came together to figure out how to recycle the red mud tanks as waste. even though there are good technologies, recycling is so expensive that there is no business entrepreneur who would take up recycling. the direction is good, but we have to make the economic sphere interested.

in writing. – during the year-long preparations the epp group presented a unanimously firm position by expressing its discontent over the tone and subject matter of the report. i had a negative opinion about the report. reservations were twofold: a matter of both principle and the practical approach. i was critical of the tone determined by prejudices, an assumption that counter-terrorism keeps violating human rights, operates with excessive budgets and exaggerates threats. these propagandistic assumptions were based on hypotheses for which no evidence was provided by the rapporteur. the goals laid down in the report are hardly feasible in practice but the rapporteur showed no intention of reflecting on this. as data is classified for security reasons, to what extent and on which benchmarks should the proportionality between the cost of a preventive counter-terrorism operation and the threat level be assessed? as supporters of transparency and accountability, the epp group stands for overseeing counter-terrorism policies; however, within the established competence of designated authorities, not bodies made up of ambitious civilians or self-declared politicians. undermining the morale of counter-terrorism, especially nowadays, is proof of irresponsible behaviour, thus the epp is not in a position to support a report reflecting any political campaign jeopardising security.
in writing. - my fellow members of parliament! following the disaster at the us oil rig, europe is also seeking to tighten and harmonise safety standards for offshore oil and gas production. ensuring the smooth and safe operation of production is also a security of supply priority in european waters. the north sea is the main source of european oil and gas production. i would like to draw your attention to four key aspects of the report. first of all, i consider it essential that third countries be proactively involved in the tightening of safety standards. as a significant proportion of north sea sites are controlled by non-eu member states, this is the only way to reach a satisfactory solution. secondly, i agree that instead of hysterical reactions, we need calm action: clearly define responsibilities and implement appropriate preventive measures. however, banning hydrocarbon production in european waters would be an unnecessary overreaction to what has happened. thirdly, i think it is a good initiative to focus on prevention rather than on the aftermath of damage. strengthening the licensing procedure, standardising licensing conditions, making independent audits mandatory and training staff all contribute to preventing a potential accident. finally, i support the principle that manufacturers should be held responsible for the damage they cause. only financial responsibility provides a proper incentive for market participants to comply with preventive measures and to guarantee the highest level of security.
in writing. – i would like to warmly congratulate ms bauer for her excellent report on the situation of women approaching retirement age. our continent has the highest proportion of elderly women in the world, who frequently face double discrimination in the labour market, based both on age and gender. as a consequence of the rising divorce rates and the shorter life-expectancy of men, women are often and increasingly over-represented among the isolated elderly. widows and lone elderly women in general are at a higher risk of poverty, isolation and social exclusion. yet elderly women approaching retirement age represent a significant resource for tackling the ingravescent demographic challenges the eu must face, so we must ensure that they can stay healthy and active as well as remain in the labour market if they wish. member states need to adequately address the multiple discrimination that older women are facing in seeking access to employment, also by exchanging best practices in improving the quality of working conditions of older women in order to create for them a sustainable and healthy workplace.
i am pleased that the european parliament is paying close attention to alternative dispute resolution procedures and to the implementation and transposition of the directive. an important feature of democracy is that conflicts can be resolved within a regulated institutional framework. after identifying the interests, agreements can be reached where there is a high probability of compliance with the agreement, where relations can be maintained even if the parties were previously in conflict. mediation is a tool for this, and there are many areas where it has proven effective: family disputes, child custody disputes, child custody litigation, out-of-court settlements. but in the field of economic, financial, community and minority disputes, effective solutions are often found in this way. however, we see that mediation is not sufficiently known among european citizens, and perhaps this debate and this report will contribute to a better understanding and wider use of mediation. perhaps one of the most important aspects of mediation is that it is accessible to those who are vulnerable and otherwise have difficulty accessing justice. of course, the elements that have been discussed here are important: the reduction of the burden on the courts, the reduction of the duration of civil proceedings, the question of costs and so on. the report also shows that the member states are at very different stages of transposing and applying the directive. i think there are many areas where further unification is needed. while preserving diversity, it would be good if training could be used to coordinate member states' practices in the field of accreditation, as well as in the field of confidentiality rules and the enforcement of the agreement. this is why we are awaiting the commission's communication, which it promised to deliver in 2013.
in writing. - mr president, i have to go. the eu's decision-makers are driven by the aim of making the union more citizen-friendly. one of the key elements is to make sure that european citizens feel safe and secure in using our most popular eu acquis: the schengen system. but the schengen system cannot function properly without effective external border protection. european citizens therefore increasingly need an efficient, well-organised union institution that is able to react quickly and has the appropriate powers and capacity. it is therefore necessary to strengthen frontex with legal means to make it really effective in the fight against trafficking in human beings, criminal organisations and drug trafficking. of course, respect for human rights, including the human rights of refugees, must be an essential aspect of frontex operations. my colleague mr busuttil has done an excellent job, both within parliament and in his negotiations with the commission and the council, and for that he deserves our full recognition. it should also be noted that the frontex proposal was reached through the efforts of the hungarian presidency over the past six months. with hard work, we hope to have created a good background for frontex's work.

on behalf of the ppe group. mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your work. our common objective is to achieve a single european energy market by 2014. we want a single european energy market because we believe that this is in the interests of european consumers, since it is the only way to keep energy prices under control, increase security of supply and prevent trade abuses. however, much remains to be done before the single market is established. in addition to missing pipelines, infrastructure connections, and regulation, we're lagging behind. we're behind in implementation, but we're also behind in creating new legislation. in the field of wholesale energy, the remit report fills one such regulatory gap. the legislation will in future allow for the control of crossborder wholesale trade in gas and electricity. a significant proportion of energy products are now internationally traded. therefore, current practice of checks at member state level is insufficient to effectively screen for abuses. thanks to this legislation, the energy agency will now have detailed information on wholesale transactions that have not been publicly disclosed. this legislation will now allow only registered wholesalers to trade energy in countries where no licensing system is currently in place. and this legislation will make it possible to clearly identify who is responsible for any abuse when imposing penalties. house of honor! these are important achievements for which i would like to thank the rapporteur and, as the party's spokesman, for the cooperation. i congratulate the hungarian presidency on having managed to reach the best possible compromise in such a short time for the member states and parliament - in agreement with the commission - as i believe.
officer, please. i'd like to congratulate the people who did the report and the people who did the shadow report. this report is proof that more europe means a better europe for the people of europe. the task is clear: to help optimise energy use, to help develop a reasonable infrastructure and to protect consumers. we support the european union's regulation of wholesale trade in gas and electricity, and we support and call for the preservation of the independence of this regulation and the provision of resources. and we are asking that after all, consumers, the european population, should pay for all distortions, so that this is particularly incorporated in the penalties, that this is taken into account. european consumers will judge us on this basis whether more europe is really better for them. i wish it were.
in writing. – as responsible european decision-makers, we cannot ignore the fact that european citizens do not trust european institutions and this also means that they do not necessarily trust us. we have been wondering for years how to remedy this. i think it is a two-way street: openness and efficiency. we have to let citizens get more involved in what we do (via for example the european citizens' initiative) but, on the other hand, we also have to inform them about what we do and try to make an effort to do our job better. the better legislation package aims precisely to tackle this second challenge. transparency and openness should be general principles that go without saying. eu legislation should be straight to the point and understandable for everyone, and this should apply both to the documents produced and the way they are prepared and negotiated. another important aspect is to carefully analyse the possible outcome of our actions and decisions. this will also ensure that we are not misinterpreting or infringing the principles of subsidiarity and proportionality. all this is not only a requirement to restore confidence in european institutions but it is also a must in a modern-day democracy.
a good ten years ago, i was talking to a senior labor researcher at the time, and he surprised me by saying that jobs, jobs that are now gone, will never come back. so what i'm saying is that work is changing, the ways of working are changing, and the legal frameworks that have been developed in the course of welfare capitalism are often not covered, not touched, not regulated. commissioner tajani and i have just been confronted with a problem in relation to forced labour that we could not agree on in a professional law, because, for example, there was no uniform definition of forced labour, no legal definition. so it's not just that big companies have become small jobs, micro jobs, not just that outsourcing, part-time work, home-based work has taken over, but there are so many new and creative forms that there are no rules for. does the commission intend to draw up guidelines on the regulation of unusual, atypical and new types of work? that's my question.
i'm not against flexibility. i think that the very rapid changes in technology, the economy becoming global, requires that we change our mindsets and that we change the laws. i miss the fact that we don't try to react to these things, only very hard. if it's really necessary, we try to give some, mostly unsatisfactory, answers. so, forced labor is not just about social security... from liability issues to safety regulations, there are many situations where someone is actually working as an employee, but at the same time all the burdens that are normally shared with the employer are borne by the employee himself, with all the risks. i think that legal action is also needed here and that we should expect the commission to be more active and more concrete.
on behalf of the ppe group. our socialist colleagues decided to put a question to the commission on homelessness and to table a motion for a resolution on the subject. the people's party believes that the question is appropriate and justified. and the resolution that has been tabled can and will be supported by us. and we agree, of course, that homelessness is one of the most extreme forms of poverty, of extreme poverty, and we think that's unacceptable in the 21st century. it should also be seen and appropriate for that reason that the approach which is also contained in the question requires a coordinated, cooperative and integrated approach, both in respect of cooperation within and between member states. homelessness is a kind of final stop on the road to slide, to break down, to isolation. it's a terminal where a circulus vitiosus develops: the homeless person doesn't get a job. if you don't get a job, you have no chance of getting out of homelessness. other problems are added: physical and mental decline, disease and often isolated communities, crime and other problems. clearly, the only way out is through coordinated efforts and programs in the various fields of social services, health, education, and job creation. if there have been successful examples - and there have been successful examples in some countries in the past, there are also notable efforts - these should be collected, as they say, good practices should be aggregated, and on the basis of these it is reasonable and appropriate to formulate a european strategy. we welcome the question, we welcome the motion for a resolution and we support it.
i also think it is important that the commission should take action in all the areas mentioned in the draft decision of parliament and in the consensus conference on homelessness, in coordinating, monitoring and assisting the homelessness strategies of the member states. however, i would like to draw attention to two aspects which, as new phenomena, require special attention. on the one hand, the social consequences of the crisis are severe and long-term unemployment, and the debt crisis of families, the inability of many to pay the mortgage. this is particularly hard on those who have taken out loans based on foreign currencies, mainly swiss francs, which are constantly appreciating. both are associated with the risk of homelessness and require special precautions. for example, to examine whether the directive on unfair commercial practices against consumers needs to be tightened at european level. the other phenomenon i want to mention is the increasingly common police treatment of homelessness: discrimination against homeless people, their humiliating removal from public spaces, the threat of fines, of being locked up. homelessness is the extreme form of poverty, as we've heard many times today. this should be given special attention in the framework of the european platform against poverty and human rights aspects should also be given high priority.
in writing. strategy-making has always been a very important issue, but in the current economic crisis it has become even more important. member states are trying to tackle this problem with varying intensity and success. i am convinced that the eu has the competence to do so and can facilitate this process by means of appropriate financial instruments, in particular by launching new programmes under the european social fund.
my dear officer. the european union is doing better than expected in reducing its carbon emissions, according to the latest projections from the european commission. largely due to the reduction in carbon dioxide emissions, the european union can reduce its emissions by up to 25% by 2020 through implementation of the energy and climate package adopted in 2006. at the same time, as this proposal and this draft resolution very rightly state, it is important to focus on non-carbon based greenhouse gases. but i do have one critical comment, namely, that human activity is narrowly interpreted by the reporters. after all, both crop production and animal husbandry are the result of human activity. and the greenhouse effect of methane is 25 times that of carbon dioxide, and almost 300 times that of nitrous oxide. and the production of crops is also the result of human activity. reducing the use of fertilisers in crop production and using them more spatially and temporally can significantly contribute to reducing nitrogen oxide emissions. the same is true for livestock farming, where methane would have to be contained, so biogas is very important. i was a rapporteur on this in 2008 and i think it is very important that the new cap introduces a greening component in the first pillar, direct payments, from 2014, which can reduce methane and nitrogen oxide emissions from agriculture.
mr president, finance ministers these days praise the ecb for doing a great job, and they have a point, because the ecb is now becoming a fiscal institution. it does the job of the finance ministers. instead of restricting itself to monetary policy, it finances sovereign debt. that is a distortion; it is a confusion of roads in economic policy, and it cannot be successful because it will really create inflation and it will really put more debt on those countries which are now saved, apparently, from this debt crisis. the resignation of jürgen stark is a stark reminder of this confusion. i think we have to realise that the finance ministers have to do their job and the sooner they do it, the better for the eurozone.
in writing. i cannot accept that after the summer euro summits, we are still talking about theoretical analysis of the euro crisis. citizens ask us every day, and rightly so, how long and why the euro is left vulnerable, and what is the shortest way out of the crisis? some member states are of course doing so in practice, but it does not make their situation any easier to do their work in different ways while on the move, and it is mostly downgraded. i am thinking here of the irresponsible and unfounded statements of the rating agencies and some regrettable statements of the committee. it is not enough that the crisis management tools were not available in time, i feel that there are no concrete, forward-looking recommendations from the commission today, even though in the absence of clear guidelines member states are becoming increasingly vulnerable. i have already said that we are not talking about a euro crisis, but a debt crisis. well, if you drag it out too long, there could even be a euro crisis.
in writing. it is an old fact, and reality has repeatedly proved it, that the european union has always developed through crises, crises have contributed to deepening cooperation and integration. this development includes the establishment of the schengen system and the introduction of the single currency. however, the latter was only partially successful: monetary union is not complemented by a common economic and fiscal policy. this led to the break-up of some of the countries in the union and, with it, the crisis of the euro area as a whole. the european union is faced with a new choice: either it retreats towards looser cooperation, with all the serious consequences that this entails, or it achieves economic and financial integration within the euro area relatively quickly. this requires strong european governance, as has long been urged by the european parliament, the eu's most democratic institution. hungary and the other non-euro area countries clearly have an interest in a stronger monetary union and thus belong to a community that also provides them with economic and financial stability. the eurosceptic, war-mongering, populist rhetoric, the false emphasis on our sovereignty, destroys the possibility of hungary catching up and strengthens the arguments of those who are already promoting the plans for the creation of a multi-speed europe. hungary should support all efforts to deepen economic integration between member states and to strengthen the eu's basic democratic values. we have a vital interest in a strong, democratic europe.
in writing. we are witnessing and suffering from a crisis of trust. the massive indebtedness of households and public finances - fuelled by the illusion of cheap money, a rosy future - has suddenly shattered the glamour of the financial markets, and suddenly mistrust and fear of loss have taken over. this panic fear is as exaggerated as the euphoria was before. but while the bubble or the national debt was growing, everyone seemed to be doing well, and now there's a real contraction. not everyone is affected by the crisis in the same way, but everyone is getting less than if the european economy as a whole were healthy and confidence restored. in my country, tens of thousands of people took out swiss franc loans, which were handed out by banks to the hilt until october 2008. since then, the crisis has led to many job losses, a collapse in the property market and a strong appreciation of the swiss franc as a result of the crisis of confidence mentioned above. all of this has left thousands in a hopeless situation. more europe, more solidarity would be the way out, but in some places selfish bias and in others fear of the pain of the new order are standing in the way of quick and decisive decision and action. and in this paralysis, europe is being dragged helplessly into the vortex of crisis. i hope we all get out of here before it's too late. a fractured and weak europe is unlikely to meet the challenges of the future. it's not too late, but there's no time to procrastinate.
in writing. as a first step to overcome the crisis, we must adopt the six-pack of strengthened economic governance legislation as soon as possible, and go beyond that. the introduction of eurobonds and the inevitable closer political coordination that will follow will also have to be seriously considered in the near future, as the only alternatives are unlimited bailouts or the bankruptcy of distressed states. i am glad that the european commission has recognised this need when it promised to draw up a proposal. it is equally welcome that the french-german summit this summer seemed to have finally brought home to the european right that austerity is not enough, but that the burden of the crisis must be shared much more fairly than it is now between the already overburdened european citizens and the financial sector, which has not yet taken its share of the burden. that is why the european right is now supporting the introduction of a financial transaction tax, which was initiated by the socialists. we must introduce this tax, wisely calibrated but as quickly as possible, and its introduction must be accompanied by much stricter supervision of the financial markets. i hope that the commission will make a proposal on this in the near future.
member of the commission. − madam president, this is another issue where the commission is working exceptionally closely with the external action service. in the face of the unacceptable violent repression unfolding in syria, the commission has reacted swiftly and was the first european union donor to suspend its bilateral cooperation with the syrian authorities on 25 may. the suspension was, for the first time in the region, based on the human rights violations committed by the syrian regime. the national indicative programme for the years 2011-2013 was also suspended. in fact, it has lost its relevance given the lack of will on the syrian side to further implement reforms. it is clear that the commission does not envisage further cooperation with the syrian authorities at the moment. due to major operational constraints and the most recent political developments, the commission is working jointly with the european external action service on clear instructions regarding the participation of syria in regional programmes. this participation will be thoroughly assessed on a case-by-case basis. in parallel, the commission is currently working with the european investment bank on the suspension of its technical assistance to the eib loan operations provided through the facility for euro-mediterranean investment and partnership support fund. however, the commission has not suspended all its activities in syria. the projects directly benefiting the palestinian and iraqi refugees are ongoing and will continue. europeaid has also been actively exploring ways to further support syrian civil society. under europeaid thematic instruments, several activities are also being implemented or planned in the field of support for civil society and human rights defenders. the european instrument for democracy and human rights allows confidential emergency support to human rights defenders, as well as support for the documentation of human rights violations, digital security, media and networking of activists. let me elaborate a little more on this issue. there are considerable risks in engaging with civil society within syria under the current circumstances, especially for potential beneficiaries, and the commission is cautiously preparing any new activity in light of the ‘do no harm’ principle. we are exploring ideas for supporting the so-called syrian diaspora or civil society also outside syria. it should be stressed that the commission is not losing sight of syria. once there is the possibility, we will do all we can to support syria on its path toward a peaceful transition to democracy.
president-in-office of the council. − mr president, thank you for this important debate and all the contributions that were made. i think it is clear that we must push for a coherent approach in the international community. since this issue has been raised concerning china and russia, i just want to stress and confirm that, of course, the syrian issue is raised in all meetings with those partners, so there can be no doubt about our commitment to bringing all the international community on board on the question of syria. i think we also have to be very clear about our objectives. we have to act against the regime and in the interests of the people, so it is essential that when we talk about sanctions we understand what effects those sanctions may have on the population. i could, referring to the number of arguments that were made, give you a very long list of the sanctions we have imposed and, yes, i want to confirm that we are ready to go even further, but we all know that we have to measure those sanctions in such a way as not to harm the population but to make life difficult for the regime. i also want to emphasise once again the need for unity of the people and a strict message of non-violence. this must also be very much present in our communication on syria. i also want to confirm briefly that indeed the eu delegation in damascus has contacts with civil society in syria and those contacts involve the network of member states’ embassies. of course this is not a very easy process; you can understand that the security situation in syria is not conducive to easy contacts between the eu delegation and civil society, so if you allow i would refrain from further details in the plenary of the european parliament.

the next item is the statement by the vice-president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy on the current state of play of the middle east peace process.
mr president, most of the attention attracted by the arab spring has focused on internal developments, and rightly so, but from the perspective of the middle eastern region as a whole, it is vital to recognise that the arab spring is also transforming the geostrategic environment. the balance of power between israel and its neighbours has shifted. mubarak’s egypt was committed to a policy of zero change, and this included relations with israel. this is moving in a different direction as hitherto marginalised political forces in egypt are emerging. israel likewise had a stable neighbour under the repressive assad regime in syria, where the outcome of the pressure for change remains unclear. there is no guarantee that a putative end of the assad regime will be of benefit to israel. at the same time – others have mentioned this – turkey has initiated a far-reaching improvement of its links with the arab world and it has inevitably downgraded its relations with israel. in an ideal world, this should provide an incentive for israel to find a settlement with the palestinians and to do so quickly. time is running out – i am entirely in agreement with mr de rossa here – and it is vital for israel’s future security that it come to terms with the changes. if not, it will very likely be coerced into them in much less favourable circumstances.
on behalf of the s&d group. madam president, madam commissioner, i have to go. i would also like to congratulate the commission, the rapporteur, and thank you for the effective work you have done with the co-rapporteurs. i think it is very important that we set ourselves four strategic objectives, and i would like to express these on behalf of the socialists of europe. the first is that the 80 billion euros earmarked for the next multiannual budget of the european union should be seen by heads of state and ministers as an investment, not as a reserve fund from which money can be withdrawn occasionally. it's very important that investors and businesses want to multiply that amount. our second strategic objective should be to achieve the 2020 priorities. let us not give up these priorities in favour of other research which is also important, but perhaps of less importance for the future of the european union. if we are determined to build a green, energy-efficient and smart economy, then we must also align our r & d and innovation resources with this. the third strategic objective is human resource management. we must do everything possible to ensure that excellent researchers born in the european union do not have to move north of the pyrenees or west of the oder. these countries also need to build up research and development capacities to achieve their maximum excellence in their own countries. finally, madam commissioner, the european parliament has agreed that we need a new industrial policy. the research, development and innovation results generated by these resources should benefit from horizontal and vertical cooperation between small and medium-sized enterprises and larger enterprises in the union.
in writing. - my fellow members of parliament! europe is one of the most developed regions in the world. but some emerging countries have already overtaken us in investing in research, development and innovation. there is no doubt that european investment has increased, but we are still exploiting only a fraction of the eu's innovation potential. there are still significant differences between countries, with only a fraction of the resources going to the new member states. we need to make structural changes to increase the union's competitiveness. in my view, two aspects are of critical importance. first and foremost, i would like to emphasize the need to simplify administration. the excessive administrative complexity is costly and makes it difficult for smes to participate. the elimination of parallel structures and the faster mobilisation of funding will greatly contribute to the involvement of as many actors as possible in r & d. secondly, i agree with the excellence approach. the most effective way to use the financial resources of the framework programme is to select research projects for funding on the basis of excellence. but in the future, we need a more nuanced approach. the new member states do not have the research infrastructure to enable them to meet the criteria of excellence. we must therefore develop forms of financing that facilitate the development of lagging infrastructure. this is the way to ensure that all member states are treated equally in the case of a decision on the basis of excellence.
in writing. - my colleagues! i would like to congratulate my colleague carlo fidanza on his report. in today's difficult economic environment, it is vital to maximise the potential for job creation. the development of tourism, making it sustainable and attractive, therefore plays a key role in many regions of europe. i would also like to draw attention to the fact that we live in an ageing society, and the proportion of people over 60 will increase by 70% in this decade. thus, the need for accessible and accessible tourism is becoming more pronounced, as is the need for active outdoor activities for older people. carlo fidanza recognised the importance of these criteria and incorporated my proposals for accessibility and accessible tourism in his report, thus enabling families with small children, the elderly and the 80 million disabled citizens in europe to have equal access to the services of this area, to actively spend their well-deserved rest and thus generate market demand for european tourism.

in writing. - mr president, i have to go. the debate on strengthening the eu's response to disasters provides a good opportunity to look at the european disaster response system in a broader context. while i agree with the findings of the report, i think there are still a number of questions that have not been answered: does the union have adequate financial means to protect itself against industrial accidents with serious consequences in addition to natural disasters? do we have a tool that allows for quick and effective damage control in the event of industrial disasters? if we don't have answers to these questions, and i think we don't, then wouldn't it be timely to start working on a new system of effective financial instruments as soon as possible? i am convinced that the solution must be found in a european system of disaster insurance which, based on the principle of compulsory liability insurance, would provide an adequate amount of additional eu funding from the contributions of potential industrial polluters for rapid and effective intervention. the victims would receive immediate compensation, while the risk of non-payment of the polluter could be spread across the eu. in addition, the scheme would also benefit the payers by allowing them to apply for aid for their safety and environmental investments from the unused funds through calls for tenders. i think we need to start the debate on the merits as soon as possible, not wait for another disaster to warn us of the need for it.

mr president, european integration was born as a means of overcoming divisions and providing a framework for achieving economic prosperity and sustaining – even reinforcing – europe’s role in the world. we have, since then, created impressive political and institutional structures and are dealing with many different policies in the framework of the european union. however, our achievements are now being challenged as we face major internal and external challenges. the euro crisis represents our greatest test so far. no matter how hard all of us have worked for democratic governance, the legitimacy of the european construction is being questioned. this puts a strain on europe’s solidarity, something that has only made us stronger in the long run. this was our standing point and should continue to be our beacon, even during the crisis. i would like to thank president barroso for addressing us today and draw his attention to an important point. all things considered, we are working for the people – all european citizens. no matter how clever the economic solutions we propose, we need a good foundation. we have to respect a principle which ensures that not a single person is left behind.
in writing. today, when we talk about economic policy in europe, we are talking almost exclusively about monetary policy. this is despite the fact that the financial sector is only a very small part of economic policy. we - eu decision-makers and national legislators - should discuss the whole thing and weigh each instrument before making our decisions. economic policy is more than financial measures. it should provide guidance to industry, allow for innovation and research and development, and encourage investment. it has to take care of the three 'i's: innovation, industry, investment. if we do not decide soon on the direction of the european union's economic engagement, it will be extremely difficult to guarantee common, effective and successful action in these times of crisis. we have a responsibility to the next generations, we must now secure their future, and this requires innovation, a strong european industrial policy once again and investment incentives.

i would like to say a few words about the european commission's report on serbia, which was published today. the report lacks one of the most problematic elements of the belgrade asset restitution law passed in september. the law of particular importance contains a provision on restitution which seems to confirm the principle of collective guilt, collective responsibility, instead of individual responsibility. and as we know, the cornerstone of european justice is individual responsibility. this fact seems to have escaped the commission's attention. the law does not apply to persons and their heirs who took part in the second world war as members of the occupying forces stationed in the territory of serbia, irrespective of whether any individual responsibility can be established in their case. this is how almost the entire hungarian minority in the past, who were drafted against their will, were forced to leave. this article of the law gives serbia a bad image and will certainly make it difficult for it to obtain candidate status, but it also calls into question the commission's position.
my dear officer! first of all, i would like to thank the rapporteur for this excellent work, for he has developed an element of economic policy which, without harmonisation, without a minimum of coordination, is unthinkable in a well-functioning competitive european union economy. when vat was introduced 40 years ago, it was in line with the needs of the economy. however, since then, many modifications, so-called modernizations, have been made to it, which have made it more and more complex. it imposed unnecessary burdens on taxpayers, tax authorities and was also an obstacle to the integrated european union market. he had many weaknesses that made him vulnerable. it was the basis for tax evasion and tax avoidance, which resulted in a very significant loss of tax revenue, hundreds of billions of dollars. but the other side of it is that vat is a very important source of revenue for the budget, because it allows us to finance the supply systems. i would also like to say that we need exceptions and we need exemptions, but we must think very carefully about the areas in which we grant exemptions and we grant exceptions, because they cannot distort competition in the future. i welcome the reinforcement of the anti-fraud measures in the report and the simplification of administration. at the same time, i support and consider it important to ensure transparency and traceability in the system, especially in the case of cross-border activities. however, i also think it is important to note that it would be good to forget about the categories within the border and within the european union. these categories are the very barriers to the european union's internal integrated market. i also find data warehouse models useful. i propose to introduce what has already been proven in member states and which actually leads to a budgetary surplus. once again, i congratulate the rapporteur and thank him for accepting my amendments.
my fellow members of parliament! jobbik representatives have spoken here many times against the centralization of the european union, against its centralization. but we are dealing with a typical vat issue where harmonisation is the only solution. it's a very bad message for the people of europe that we are carrying the burdens together into this union, but the welfare measures are already being shared. well, look at what wages are in eastern europe, western europe. and not only is there such a social difference, but even in eastern europe the general sales tax can almost be said to be several times or several percent higher than the general sales taxes or value added tax in western europe. in hungary, for example, a record-breaking outbreak of absinthe was predicted. the government will cut the vat rate to a record 27% in hungary, which will mean unprecedented cuts for the people. and considering that wages are fractions of what they are in western european countries. it is time for the european union to intervene and not allow such restrictions. the european union should not only look at various abstract issues, but also at the well-being of the european population.
of course, we must be cautious and act on us protectionist initiatives. but i also want to talk about another aspect of this american job creation package, which is that it's going in a radically different direction than the current leaders of europe. and this is something that we should pay attention to and take into account when we think about european plans. while it's not going to be passed by congress, we're interested in the many proposals in this package, like infrastructure investments that create jobs, cutting taxes on employment, investing in education -- which is based on the same recognition that we've emphasized a lot -- that without education, there really is no economic recovery -- helping to preserve public sector jobs, supporting public sector jobs, extending unemployment benefits -- we know that many people in america will be out of unemployment benefits in the near future -- reducing long-term unemployment through a variety of means. and this package is based on the recognition that there is no better response to the deficit than economic growth, job creation and support for education. this is a lesson that we, the leaders of europe, should learn. europe's decisions have been delayed, they have been weak. they have exacerbated and prolonged the crisis rather than actually alleviating it. the next european council will be historic. and the real question is whether a european plan for investment and growth is emerging, or at least whether we can see the outlines of it in the near future.
the rapporteur. - my fellow members! it has been said many times in the european parliament that there are some 80 million people with disabilities in the european union. this huge social group has not received much attention, much care from policy makers. these are unfortunate facts, and the european parliament has drawn attention to them on many occasions. i think we have now reached a turning point in the history of the european union. the spanish, belgian and hungarian presidencies, together with the european commission, have made disability a priority, finding many links with different policies. i'd like to point out that this is a long time coming. so it's a great honor for me to be able to record this report as a representative of the deaf. i'm honored to have the full cooperation and assistance of the shadow reporters in this enormous undertaking. and i'm honored that about 400 amendments were submitted, and opinions were received from three other committees. these amending opinions resulted in the full, final text of the report. i was guided by one objective in writing this report: to take a position that was realistic and achievable during the economic crisis and that was tangible to people with disabilities, to reflect the european parliament's deep commitment to the situation of people with disabilities, to improve their situation and to call for active action by the european commission as well. among the many proposals, i would like to highlight two: one is a key element of the strategy proposed by the european commission the european accessibility act , which the report expects a lot from, as we expect a mechanism that can be clearly verified, and we also expect progress in the area of information communication accessibility. on the other hand, it is redefining the role of the european parliament, which we emphasise, so that the un convention on the rights of persons with disabilities has a prominent role in parliament. we expect a lot from the proposal which calls for the creation of a so-called european disability committee. and i would like to emphasize that much of this report is based on compromise. various proposals were received, and the most promising parts were compiled. this has enabled the report to include various tools or other forward-looking elements, such as the recognition of sign languages. i would urge the european commission and the governments of the member states to be proactive and to enable people with disabilities to be integrated into society as soon as possible, to become active members of the european union. that would be a huge opportunity for 80 million people. and one thing is certain: since the lisbon treaty, people with disabilities are no longer second-class citizens on paper, but it is time to make that reality, that all citizens are equal.
i support the report of adam the bald. i think it is particularly important to reduce the impact of the crisis and to pay special attention to those who are having more difficulty integrating into society than others in these times. the eu 2020 strategy also places a high priority on inclusive growth, and we have a responsibility here too. this requires that people with different disabilities can participate in education, employment, access to various basic services and goods, and we must create an environment that allows them to live independently. the freedom drive delegation was here recently and in their conversation with the european parliament they expressed this wish and handed over their petition. an important requirement of this report is that the council should move forward within a deadline to adopt a horizontal anti-discrimination directive, which also creates equal opportunities in all areas of life. i would like to draw attention to the issue of people with psychosocial disabilities, whose representation in all member states is weaker than that of other groups of people with disabilities. they should have a say in public affairs, they should be supported in decision-making. the principle of 'nothing about us without us' must apply to all issues, including the allocation of eu resources and the principle of integration.
the rapporteur. − i am grateful to all my colleagues for their comments, because they have all pointed to the urgent need for action and have given me further confirmation that the time has come to improve the living conditions of people with disabilities. we can't wait any longer, whether it's education, job creation or access. legislative initiatives and programmes are needed that are clear and feasible, controllable and punishable, both at eu and national level. and i would like to show you another aspect: the job creation target of the eu 2020 strategy cannot be achieved without people with disabilities, and only with their inclusion can the 75% level be reached. the u.s. government has calculated that it costs no more than $500 -- the price of an iphone -- to create jobs for people with disabilities. that is why i am deeply committed to and consider important the commission's proposal for a european access package next year to make progress on the issue of non-discrimination. i thank all my fellow congressmen once again for their honorable cooperation. i have learned a lot in the last six months and i am convinced that this report has made the european parliament even more committed to people with disabilities.
in writing. i would like to congratulate my colleague adam kósa on his report and welcome the fact that he places great emphasis on improving the situation of women with disabilities, as both surveys and everyday experience show that they face the most serious disadvantages in their lives. the current eu directives mention but do not precisely define the phenomenon of multiple discrimination and are not well suited to effectively combating it. disability-based exclusion also affects women in another way: they are generally responsible for the disabled and dependants, and thus are cumulatively affected by the decline in public care services. women and girls with disabilities are also at a much higher risk of being abused, neglected, and humiliated in the evening, and are three times more likely to be victims of violence. moreover, women with disabilities have statistically significantly lower levels of education than men with disabilities or even healthy women. access to information and communication technologies, which are usually the primary means of integrating disabled people, are of paramount importance in this regard. on average, one in three to four people with disabilities in the eu is employed, but many member states fall far short of this average. support is needed to help them enter and stay in the labour market, and governments should work with employers and ngos to develop incentives to promote the employment of people with disabilities.
first of all, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on a very good job that has resulted in a balanced report. however, i would like to stress that better regulation requires, first and foremost, greater legal certainty for the parties concerned. if we think that the complex interplay of european legislation and national transposition has resulted in a simpler procurement procedure, we are wrong. in fact, it has resulted in higher costs, longer procedures and more work for legal counsel. i therefore consider it necessary to coordinate the appeal and procurement procedures and, as the commissioner has mentioned, to take account of the full life-cycle cost. i agree with the rapporteur that it is necessary to abandon the minimum price requirement and to abolish the excessive guarantees and safeguards which place a greater burden on smes.
in writing. i joined the green paper debate on the modernisation of eu public procurement policy as a shadow rapporteur for the people's party in the environment committee. i am pleased to say that there was broad agreement in our committee that by encouraging the wider use of environmentally friendly so-called 'green public procurement', we can make real progress towards a resource-efficient and low-carbon economy. in view of all this, i believe that in future public procurement, rules on sustainability should be given greater emphasis. one of the main means of doing this is to end the monopoly of the lowest price for the award criteria and to create instead the possibility of selecting the most economically advantageous tender, taking into account the full life-cycle cost of the services and the environmental benefits. the report rightly points out that, of course, for all this to be practical, it is also necessary for the commission to develop a methodology widely available to contracting authorities for the voluntary and rapid calculation of life-cycle costs. as a shadow opinion-giver, another important aspect for me was to increase the participation of smes in green public procurement. the opinion of the committee on the environment therefore recommends that, where the product or service advertised for procurement is also available locally, the proximity principle should be used to maintain the procedure for local smes, thereby reducing the environmental impact of the procurement, such as co2 emissions from transport. the same objective is to reduce administrative burdens and to establish clear and transparent standards at the level of directives.
mr. president, mr. officer, i have to go. today's questions to the commission on the small business package show that we still have a lot of work to do to ensure that this european business community can actually operate in a growth-friendly market environment. the economic crisis has actually hit this business class hardest, in the sense that it has further reduced their already limited funding opportunities, precisely in the business class that we call start-ups or innovative businesses. in my view, the recovery of the european union from the economic crisis depends largely on how we can position the group of enterprises, which accounts for 99% of enterprises - what we call smes - how we can make better use of the tools that allow them to operate smoothly. for example, i support the sme test, i support simplifying administrative procedures.
