in writing. the implementation of the services directive is a major step towards a better functioning of the single market for services. but i think there's more to be done. the main findings of the peer review show that the commission needs to take further steps to enable the european economy to exploit the full potential of the single market for services. in order to provide a realistic and useful overview of the functioning of the internal market for services, other union instruments than the services directive should also be taken into account. the commission's approach is welcome, and will not be an abstract assessment of performance, but will be based on specific sectors and activities that illustrate how the single market works and where problems remain. given that constraints in the services sector and the legislative instruments applicable to them are often of a global nature, performance evaluation is also useful for identifying issues of a horizontal nature. it should be emphasised that the performance evaluation should in all cases allow for the formulation of sector-specific conclusions on the functioning of the single market for services and, where appropriate, identify the need for other actions, including legislative intervention where necessary. in closing, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur and thank him for his thorough work and his openness. i think this work has resulted in a great report.
my dear officer. the topic of economic governance is a hot topic these days. however, in order to speak of the global nature of economic governance and to deliver results at the forthcoming g20 summit, it is important to be aware of its european dimension and its risks. although the european council has discussed and will discuss very pressing issues, unfortunately it seems to be doing so very slowly. the failure to resolve the greek situation for months now could pose further risks to the euro and to the union. we have accepted the six-pack, but that is not enough to make progress. in this context, it is welcome that this report goes further and calls for global cooperation on financial supervision or even talks about more effective macroeconomic policies. however, it must also be seen that unless europe can put the common economic policy in order, restore the stability of the euro and the confidence of investors, it is useless to expect the next world economic summit to produce results.
in writing. the debate on global economic governance has come too late, as the outbreak of the financial crisis in 2007 has shown. since the early 2000s, economic and financial regulation has not kept pace with developments in the financial sector. national regulators tried to control global financial processes, and this was accompanied by a system of banking supervision that left it to speculators to follow the rules. international economic management organizations such as the imf and the world bank suffer from a serious lack of legitimacy. their structure does not reflect the realities of the world economy, the increased role of emerging economies, especially the bric countries. the g20 has not yet been able to transform itself into a truly gender-balanced, legally binding decision-making body. the european union has recently taken important steps towards a more forward-looking approach to financial processes. the financial transaction tax would make financial transactions more transparent and controllable, and ensure that the financial sector plays a greater role in mitigating the effects of the crisis, while avoiding excessive anti-bank measures that could impede growth. the european union should seek to convince the major players in the world economy at the g20 summit in november that stronger and more effective solutions, prevention rather than reparation, are needed to kick-start economic growth, job creation and a fairer sharing of the burden of the crisis. the above solutions can be considered as a good starting point for the establishment of a global economic governance system.

this report proves to me that if we make an effort to understand and respect each other's positions and to find common solutions, a balanced parliamentary resolution and a good agreement can be achieved. one thing i would mention, which is perhaps beyond the specific reporting - but which seems to have fallen into the background of our conversations these days - is the aspect of reciprocity, which is not just giving data, but also receiving passenger traffic data from our partners. this is obviously a very controversial issue, and it concerns the creation of the eu's own pnr. i'm aware that there are very different opinions on this. those who fear it, reject it, those who fear it will give even more scope for the violation of personality rights. but i think that if one stands up as an equal party within the federal system, in full respect and respect for the relationship with one's allies, and equality is a condition of reciprocity, then perhaps one is in a stronger position even in the area of human rights guarantees. it is no coincidence, for example, that in defence policy too the idea is now being formed more and more strongly that somehow the european entity must be defined more strongly, must be strengthened within the federal system, of course. so i think that in the same way that this report was done, with consensus building, but we should also accelerate thinking about the eu's own pnr. moreover, if the commission were to decide to do so and parliament voted to set up such an autonomous pnr system, there would be a lot of work to do, which obviously cannot be enumerated for lack of time. i would be very interested to hear the commissioner's opinion on this, but we certainly need to set up the organisational framework for this, but it would probably also be of great help to the member states if the requests for data were not made only on a bilateral basis.
despite the reservations and concerns expressed here, it is likely that tomorrow the european parliament will give its consent for the first time to a pnr agreement with the passenger name record file transfer more or less in line with the conditions set by parliament in 2010. we know it's hard to strike a balance between security and privacy. these criteria are nevertheless important and this draft recommendation contains a large number of them. there are still some unresolved issues, such as the long-term storage of data for 5 and a half years, but this agreement still provides citizens with access to appropriate remedies. indeed, data can only be collected and stored if it is necessary for the prevention and detection of terrorist offences and serious international crime, and close monitoring of the implementation of the agreement provides an additional guarantee that parliament can propose the suspension or termination of the agreement if necessary. such an agreement could serve as a reference point for further agreements with, for example, the usa, canada and countries that have approached the commission on this issue.
in our world of crisis, it is hard to overemphasize - as many of us here have done today - the importance of transparency. it is no coincidence that the ombudsman's report and mrs jotova's report both focus on the issue of transparency, which is a welcome and welcome one for citizens. to cope with the crisis, it is very important that they understand why what is happening around them and what thoughts are behind the institutional decisions. i believe that one of the most important elements of the ombudsman's mission is to never be one-sided, in other words to strike a balance between the citizen's need for transparency and, at the same time, the public interest in secrecy in other cases. why do i say that? because it creates a new situation in a certain sense, that with the establishment of the foreign service, the ombudsman's attention has also been drawn to questions of european security and defence policy, and this is an area where, in many cases, in addition to the legitimate demands for access to information, there are also actions that i would rather classify as political marketing and which, in some cases, may endanger our security. it is therefore extremely important that we finally establish clear standards for encryption, that we make this clear to people, because i believe that the ombudsman can also take a position on encryption with the necessary decisiveness and moral weight, where appropriate - as has already been shown - if the standards are clear. let me also mention that we can also consider ourselves representatives of the people. for example, in my work on security policy, i often find myself in situations where very important council documents are classified and i cannot read them. it would be very good to see the reasons for this clearly, and i would encourage the ombudsman to do so. let me end with a sentence from the report that goes like this: "openness is the goal, and encryption is the exception".

the rapporteur. - my dear officer dalli. my fellow officers. my report focuses primarily on bee health, but i also want to address other important challenges facing the sector. the european commission's directorate-general has produced an excellent communication, and i have based my report on it and tried to build on it. i would like the commission, the member states and the beekeeping sector to implement the many concrete proposals contained in this report. it's not primarily european honey production that's at stake. the beekeeping sector contributes to the whole of agriculture and to the conservation of biodiversity through pollination. in fact, 85% of crop production would not exist without the activities of bees and other pollinators. in other words, the beekeeping sector generates important environmental public goods, which is why we must support the beekeeping sector from community funds. the problem is that there hasn't been enough medicine for decades. big pharmaceutical companies are not interested enough in developing new medicines, but the beekeeping industry doesn't have enough money to support the development of new medicines. it's been a problem for decades. if we cannot make progress in this area with community resources, then this crisis in which european and global beekeeping is currently found will not be resolved. the other big problem is that this sector is not organized enough. there are no reliable data, no statistics on the bee health situation, not even on the number of bee hives, the number of families, so it would be very important at both member state and eu level to finally have reliable data. and a very big problem is the training of many of the beekeepers. it would be very important for member states to link beekeeping to some form of vocational training. i think it's very important, and this is the biggest debate, that we have about the impact of pesticides on bee health. there are some in this parliament who want to blame all the problems on pesticides, and i do not agree with that. others try to minimize it. i regret that certain forces have not allowed me to include in my report the pesticides which are particularly harmful and the active substances in these pesticides which could be replaced by active substances which are less harmful to bee health. these are neonicotinoid agents, and i will fight with my friends in the future who are environmentalists and bee protectors to get these agents out of pesticides as soon as possible. i would ask the commission to amend the honey directive because at present the quality parameters of honey are not sufficiently defined, so we cannot adequately tackle counterfeiters and counterfeiting of honey, and the quality problem of imported honey is also a problem. finally, as far as genetically modified, genetically engineered crops are concerned, there is a great deal of controversy about how harmful or not harmful they are. i would ask the european commission to make a credible, objective scientific study, because this is not an emotional issue, it is a scientific issue. no such study is currently available. as for antibiotics, i would ask the commissioner to regulate them at eu level. for example, there is a limit for antibiotic content in meat, even though we eat much more meat than honey. for honey, this is unregulated. it would be nice to have this on a community level. and finally, albert einstein once said, and i think that's an exaggeration, that if bees die out in the world, in four years, humanity dies out. even if it's a formula, now that we've seen the impact of beekeeping on food security and biodiversity, i think we should take the advice of albert einstein. thank you for your attention.
madam president! poverty is not just a social problem, it is a human rights problem, a violation of human dignity. the result is often stigma, discrimination. in many countries, we see the criminalization of poverty. he talks about the extreme crime of gypsies, homelessness, and even social workers who stand up for the poorest can be put on trial, as in hungary, if they act according to the ethical code of their profession. when examining national poverty reduction strategies, the commission should also monitor whether public policies serve this purpose, whether the tax system, the family support system reduce social disparities, contribute to reducing child poverty, whether the school system compensates for the disadvantages brought from home, prevent the generational transmission of poverty, whether social transfers guarantee the right to economic, political, cultural, social participation and access to universal services.

in writing. – even though the european union was designed to be an area of prosperity and progress, the europe of today is a continent of 80 million poor people for whom existential instability and inequality of opportunities are a daily experience. the reintegration of the unemployed and marginalised population can, however, release this dormant potential to revitalise the eu’s ailing economies. crisis management must not therefore overshadow but strengthen anti-poverty measures, since they are indispensable for ensuring the sustainable and inclusive growth enshrined in the europe 2020 strategy. in this light, i would like to welcome this report on this important flagship initiative of the europe 2020 strategy and the fact that it dedicates a whole section to the inclusion of roma, who are way over-represented among the poorest. equally important and mutually reinforcing with the europe 2020 national reform programmes are the national roma inclusion strategies, which are to be submitted to the commission by the end of this year, and i truly hope that despite any political upheaval or budgetary hardship in some of the member states, the commission will thoroughly evaluate the professional substantiation, complexity and financial feasibility of the national strategies, and that it will also present specific recommendations for their revision.
rapporteur. − madam president, according to rule 167 and rule 168(1), as the rapporteur on honeybee health and beekeeping, i would like to request a roll-call vote on the alternative motion for a resolution tabled by 40 members of the european parliament and also on the final vote on my report on the agri committee resolution.
in writing. − the economic and social impacts of demographic change are one of the key factors that must be reckoned with regarding the future prosperity of europe and the fulfilment of justice, human rights and freedom. marginalization and demographic dynamics often go hand in hand, as can most conspicuously be seen in the case of the roma. not only are they the continent’s far youngest minority with the lowest socio-economic status, but their communities can be characterized by reversed demographic trends compared to the majority population. hence, in most new member states, while there is a rapid ageing of the majority population, the proportion of roma is quickly increasing within the active age cohort, which carries the social security system on its shoulders. as a demographic gap opens up, so it is usually followed by the exacerbation of intra-regional disparities, further polarizing those micro-regions that are peripheral and declining and those that are dynamic and developing. the fact that there are areas which are lagging behind may hamper general social development and threaten to disrupt cohesion – not only in territorial terms, but also in social terms. their specific needs must therefore be targeted through an equivalent, complex and intensive programme based on a pan-european crisis map.
in writing. − i supported mrs westphal's own-initiative report because i believe that to stop demographic trends and encourage childbearing, we must give hope to the citizens of europe. the logical consequence of hopelessness, of economic hopelessness, is emigration, moving to regions that promise better futures. the depopulation of many european regions could be reversed by pursuing a targeted cohesion policy that takes account of differences within the region. we must therefore consider how sustainable planning based on a regional development indicator based solely on gdp is when the overall picture covers the poverty corners. europe needs to take better care of these micro-regions, helping them to integrate into the application systems and avoid red tape. this requires much more flexibility from the union. we need to focus on programs that make small-scale farming attractive again, that create jobs. however, i am convinced that cohesion policy is not the primary answer or solution to demographic issues.
in writing. - the european parliament adopted a resolution on bees today, which i myself supported. the whole of agriculture is threatened by the extinction of bees, the causes of which are not yet fully understood. the ep believes that more research should be devoted to the phenomenon than is currently the case, because without knowing the causes, the process cannot be stopped. the amount of money available is only 10 million euros, which has to be multiplied, because we don't even know if it's an infectious disease or environmental damage. but honey production has other problems. poor quality, sometimes counterfeit, honey is flooding the market. accurate designation of origin and strong quality control are needed to make beekeeping a profitable industry and to put this healthy food on the table of europeans. to this end, the targeted support of some 700 000 european beekeepers needs to be addressed.

mr president, a couple of months ago i made a speech here indicating that the best solution to the greek crisis would be an orderly default, with a significant ‘hair cut’ to private and public bond-holders. i made a distinction between insolvency and liquidity, and argued that insolvency cannot be addressed by throwing good money after bad. commission rehn dismissed my proposal because he felt it would trigger a domino effect and the commission feared contagion. now we do have contagion – see the example of italy – without having made any significant move to solve the greek crisis. the commission and the whole european political class is dangerously behind the curve. what needs to be done? eurozone governments have to push through an orderly ‘hair cut’ immediately; the efsf needs to be recapitalised before france loses its triple-a rating; greek banks should sell their balkan empires, and the remaining carcasses have to be taken over by well-capitalised financial institutions; privatisation should go ahead at full steam; the tax system must be streamlined, and its rules enforced with rejuvenated administrative vigour; societal solidarity needs to be rebuilt. for the sake of preserving democracy in europe, capitalism must be saved.
in writing. only together can we overcome the deepening crisis in the euro area. steps are needed to deepen integration through more effective use of community instruments and methods. markets reject the half-baked, slow-born solutions, the special ones. the deepening debt crisis makes it clear that only swift, jointly decided and implemented decisions, and credible action by community institutions with extended mandates, can stop the eurozone and, with it, the european union from breaking up. i agree with those who believe that strengthening european economic governance and deepening political integration are necessary if the eu is to emerge from a period of crisis on a scale not seen since the second world war. the crisis threatening the existence of the monetary union is having an impact on the whole of europe. the member states outside the euro area are therefore keen to see closer cooperation between the countries using the single currency. we also reject the idea of creating a two- or multi-speed europe, intergovernmental solutions. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' in the context of the directive. the eu delegation should be fully involved in the decisions that shape the future of integration. a community governed together, committed to closer integration, needs a budget that reflects its ambitions and a strong cohesion policy.
almost a month after 26 october, europe is still powerless to define such cardinal issues as calming european markets or treating credit ratings objectively. europe can only be taken seriously if it can provide real, forward-looking answers for its citizens. i therefore see our activities as being twofold: short-term, which concerns immediate problems, and long-term, which spans several decades and can guarantee economic growth and job creation, i.e. european prosperity. however, in order to maintain our long-term strategies, based on common solidarity and often requiring serious compromises, such as the establishment of common financial supervision or the launching of economic governance, we must not allow short-term objectives and non-european actors to make our common objectives impossible in any way. while it is welcome that the commission is taking the initiative to regulate and hold credit rating agencies more strictly and more legally accountable after a long time, it is shocking and incomprehensible that its proposals to temporarily suspend ratings and to set up a european credit rating agency have been postponed. i therefore call on the commission to establish as soon as possible a european credit rating agency, which is really aware of the mood of european markets and which is fundamentally interested in the recovery of the european economy, in order to create a stable and predictable european economic climate that can be seen as a guarantee for the future.
in writing. it is unacceptable that the global economic developments and the assessment of the economy of individual countries should be influenced by credit rating agencies which, in recent years, have been responsible for the outbreak and deepening of the economic crisis through a series of faulty and sometimes self-serving ratings. it is time for europe to act as one against erroneous and harmful credit rating practices. in this respect, yesterday's announcement by the eu's internal market commissioner michel barnier that the european commission is proposing on several fronts tougher regulation of credit ratings and, in some cases, to bring agencies to justice is welcome. however, it is disappointing that the commission has so far postponed the decision on the temporary suspension of ratings and the establishment of a european credit rating agency. it is in the interest of the whole of europe that the european commission should take a decision on these issues as soon as possible. the hungarian people's party group supports any endeavour of the european commission to further harmonise and tighten the rules on the operation of credit rating agencies. in the meantime, however, the opinions of credit rating agencies must be treated with a number of reservations, because, as has been unfortunately demonstrated in recent years, their validity often raises serious doubts. unfounded and unjustified downgrades, by increasing the uncertainty in financial markets, will only deepen the crisis and could push already struggling european states into further recession. in the worst crisis in the history of the european union, this is an unforgivable irresponsibility.
in writing. - mr president, i have to go. europe's economy is in tatters. the economic growth of european countries is forecast to slow sharply next year, owing to the austerity measures imposed in the past and the misguided economic policy measures. at the same time, the budget deficit is rising again, and financing public debt is becoming more difficult and expensive. there's only one way out of this double bind. implement the economic governance package for sound budgetary management as soon as possible and adopt further rules to strengthen economic cooperation. in the medium term, however, investment-enhancing policies are needed to accelerate sustainable economic growth in europe, primarily through spending on energy efficiency, the development of renewable energy sources, research and development, education and health. without stable and sustainable economic growth, it is impossible to renew the european social model and at least partially preserve its main achievements.
in writing. - today we approved five payments from the globalisation adjustment fund, one to greece, three to ireland and one to austria. the money isn't going to the companies or the governments, it's going to the people. i always vote for such a proposal, but i know there's a flaw in the concept. secondary action. like social policy itself, the management of unemployment is a matter for the member states. there is, of course, some truth to the idea that if global impacts are causing the harm, then global help is appropriate. but i don't think that's the real answer, even though all governments benefit from having money for the house. if a company decides that it is worthwhile to set up production in ukraine, for example, because there, say, it pays a tenth of the wages as in greece, it will certainly move. the union shall contribute to the retraining or assistance of those made redundant as compensation. but the problem is we're gonna have to deal with it. tomorrow another company will be taking over, and for the sake of greater profits, they will abandon their workers. this is how waiter europe is slowly becoming a place where masses of photographer-enthusiastic asian tourists come to admire the memories of our past. but there is no production. this phenomenon should be taken more seriously than the issue of a european pain-money. before it's too late.
in writing. the tracking and processing of both banking and passenger traffic data provides law enforcement agencies with an effective tool in the fight against terrorism and organised crime. it is welcome that agreements have been or are being concluded between the eu and its allies on both tftp and pnr that address the majority of the european parliament's human rights concerns by incorporating guarantees for the prevention and remediation of possible violations into the agreements. i would like to draw attention to one aspect, and not the first: the need for reciprocity. the union should not only give but also receive information from the us, as an equal partner, which is essential for law enforcement. however, this would require the union to make clear to the us which of its bodies or agencies is fully empowered to receive and evaluate data from the us in accordance with the merger principle and to provide member states with information relevant to them on an ongoing basis.
author. − mr president, half a year ago, after the adoption of the european framework for national roma inclusion strategies, we all agreed that the strategy was not the end, but the beginning, of our work. let me share with you the four most important tasks and areas that await us in the very near future and that are essential for this strategy to be a success in the end. firstly, the institutional and coordination starting line is to be finalised. for this purpose, the commission’s confirmation to parliament at the end of october that, despite any political upheaval or budgetary hardship in member states, it was expecting the national roma inclusion strategies to be submitted by the end of this year was of great importance. the commission also confirmed that, after a thorough evaluation covering the professional substantiation, complexity and financial feasibility of the national strategies, the commission would also present specific recommendations for their revision, and would report annually on the progress of the integration to parliament and to the council. on 8 april 2010 in budapest, vice-president reding promised that the commission would keep a close eye on the actions of all the member states and would not tolerate any window-dressing regarding the national strategies. secondly, parliament’s report called for the establishment of a so-called crisis map to identify and measure the under-developed areas which are struggling with serious structural disadvantages. this will enable complex and holistic development programmes for immediate intervention under the cross-sectoral integrated approach. i truly hope that the poverty mapping carried out by the fundamental rights agency will fulfil these requirements. thirdly, last year parliament proposed serious and important changes to the next multiannual financial framework in order to ensure that eu and national funds can contribute to the substantial and enduring improvement of living conditions for the roma. complex, flexible and sustainable programmes are necessary, with longer time coverage and greater territorial relevance, focusing on the most disadvantaged micro-regions. the scope of eu funding must be extended in the near future so that provision of quality public services can also become eligible. the rules of co-financing should be revised and possibly differentiated so that projects targeting the roma could possibly be required to be funded with a lower proportion of national co-financing and a higher share from the eu. the idea of performance, that parliament stood so firmly behind last year, should remain on the agenda. the fourth point that i would like to raise is also related to the european parliament’s report last year, when we stated that the eu policy to improve the socio-economic status of the european roma must not compromise, but complement, anti-discrimination legislation. therefore, directive 2000/43/ec on equal treatment must be finally implemented in the member states and enforced by the commission. the national equality bodies must be strengthened and all violations of these legal principles must be immediately reported and sanctioned.
mr. president, i have to go. i'm also very pleased to have commissioner vassiliou here and i miss commissioner reding very much, who has been practically invisible on this case since last summer. when he speaks, he expresses satisfaction rather than problems. and yet we see that the forced expulsion of roma continues in france. between april and october 2011, nearly 600 people were affected. in slovakia, a bill offers free contraception and sterilization to roma women. in the north of the czech republic, protesters attacked a roma settlement. there are a series of anti-gypsy protests in bulgaria with threats, radical demands. in italy, 26% of roma women mentioned that they had suffered police violence, humiliating treatment. almost all of europe is experiencing segregation in education, housing, violence against roma, hate speech. the commission is listening and awaiting our roma strategy, as we have heard here, which we know several member states have indicated that they do not consider it necessary to draw up. there are no sanctions. the whole strategy has very little, if any, anti-discrimination in it, so it is almost impossible to take it into account. member states will have to come up with anti-poverty social measures which the commission would like to monitor with the fundamental rights agency, which has neither the capacity nor the experience to do so. we will inform you in time, and we expect the commission to act against all forms of discrimination and to ensure that the strategy is effectively implemented.
member of the commission. − mr president, one thing i want to assure this house of is that we shall not content ourselves with empty words. let me remind you that the strategy that we have adopted asks member states to submit their national strategies by the end of the year. we have to wait until the end of the year to evaluate these national strategies, and we shall report to this house at the beginning of 2012. and this is just the beginning. i assure you that these strategies will not remain empty promises. to take the case of the free movement directive, the majority of member states, including france, have already modified their laws or announced amendments – or at least this is what we have been told. the commission is closely monitoring how these member states are delivering on their commitments. for those member states not yet there, the commission has made full use of its powers under the treaty, and between april and october of this year launched infringement proceedings against 12 member states. the framework decision on racism and xenophobia obliging member states to penalise public incitement to violence or hatred against groups obliges member states to provide that racist or xenophobic motivation of any offence constitutes an aggravating circumstance, or obliges judges to take this element into account in determining penalties. this is particularly pertinent for attacks against roma people, such as those which have taken place recently in member states, but let me remind you that it is the national courts which will determine whether these statements were inciting hatred – not the commission. member states were obliged to transpose the framework decision into their national legislation by 28 november. 22 member states have notified their implementing measures, which the commission is analysing. the missing five are belgium, estonia, greece, spain and poland. the fact that the other 22 have notified does not, of course, mean that they have transposed the framework decision. what we have done is appoint a group of experts to follow closely its transposition and implementation. the implementation report will be presented to us in 2013. let me remind you that under the lisbon treaty there is a transition period, and we cannot currently enforce the framework decision. we can only do this after 2013. so we shall have the report of the expert group by 2013. in 2014, we can take infringement measures against those countries which have not duly transposed this directive. so i want to assure you that we shall not be sitting idle, waiting for things to happen on their own. we shall follow and monitor the issues very closely. once we have received the national strategies at the end of the year, we shall monitor the situation and we shall decide what other measures to take next year.

diamonds. there is not a large roma community in poland and indeed we have been doing well for 10 years running a programme for the roma community, which does not mean that we do not have much to do. the polish parliament and this year we passed a resolution in the polish sejm making august 2 the day of remembrance of the genocide of roma and sinti. on august 2, 1944, the gypsy camp was finally liquidated in the german concentration camp auschwitz-birkenau. and it's the kind of day that would commemorate the holocaust of the roma as a community that was stigmatized during world war ii and eliminated solely because of its ethnicity. we, as the polish presidency, have asked the president of the european parliament, mr buzek, to make this day a memorial day for the roma and sinti. i would like to take this opportunity to ask all members to support this initiative to acknowledge the presence of the roma and the tragedy they experienced just a few decades ago here in europe. in conclusion, i would like to thank you very much for including this item on roma integration as an item on the agenda of the european parliament. i know these are long-term challenges. we cannot forget these difficult problems even in the most difficult times, especially since this crisis is already a daily reality in every european country. but all the more so must we make an effort to use money effectively, to be really effective in action, so that our european roma really feel at home in europe, in our countries. i am very grateful for the role played by the european parliament in this matter. thank you very much, sir.

in writing. it is common knowledge that eu member states should present their national strategies by the end of this year as part of the framework of the european roma strategy for roma inclusion. it is extremely depressing and sobering that despite the alarming approach of the deadline, only hungary and romania have so far prepared their 10-year strategic programme and sweden has prepared a draft law on this matter. this unfortunate incident rightly reminds us of the disgraceful failure of the european programme for the roma decade (2005-2015) which the eu framework programme for roma inclusion was created to remedy and replace. it is time for the european union and its member states to take seriously not only the more than ten million roma who live on the fringes of society and in dire poverty, but also themselves and their own commitments. it's a shame that we can't or don't really want to make our own decisions and agreements work. it is in our common european interest, both from a moral, economic and political point of view, and in the national interest of each member state, to put an end to this situation and to put into practice the eu roma strategy that we have unanimously adopted.

madam president! a normal person, if his house catches fire, instead of watering his walnut seedlings in the garden, which may turn into a crop in 15 years, he sprays his house with all the water available. for years, internationally recognized energy scientists have warned that our planet is running out of hydrocarbons and coal, and that within ten years there will be a global energy crisis, the effects of which are sadly already being felt. the realistic greens also know that the only way out of this situation is to accelerate the construction of the third generation nuclear power plants currently under investment, restart the shut down reactors and then as soon as possible broadly deploy environmentally friendly fourth generation plants with breeder reactors and brine-solvent plants, so as not to have to burn coal and hydrocarbons to produce electricity. and that solves the problem of carbon dioxide emissions right away. ladies and gentlemen, we're already 10 or 15 years behind. therefore, the training should not be for dismantling but for operating, and specialists should be trained immediately, as there is a shortage of such specialists. and fourth-generation research needs to be accelerated to a very high degree immediately. we can take funding for this from fusion research, because it makes as much sense today as irrigating the facets instead of putting out fires. thank you for your kind attention.
in writing. with the euratom strand ahead, we aim to complement and simplify other research policy actions of the union needed to implement the europe 2020 strategy, in particular those related to education, training and employment, competitiveness, innovation and the environment. 1. education, training, employment increased attention and budgetary contributions are needed to promote initiatives in nuclear research, in particular in terms of human capital and actions to address the risk of shortages of specialists in the coming years (e.g. grants to nuclear researchers). competitiveness, innovation and the environment secondly, the framework programme should contribute to the implementation of the innovation union, a flagship initiative of the europe 2020 strategy. it should strengthen competition for scientific excellence to accelerate the implementation of key innovations in nuclear energy, while playing an appropriate role in addressing the challenges of the energy sector and climate change. the implementation of iter in europe will expand new research infrastructures with a significant european dimension and will lead to the creation of new research infrastructures supporting iter experiments in the context of the european additional programme. simplification thirdly, the implementation of the framework programme (2012 to 2013) should be based on the principles of simplicity, stability, transparency, legal certainty, consistency, excellence and trust, in line with the recommendations set out in the european parliament's report on the simplification of the implementation of the research framework programmes.
in writing. according to many researchers, three quarters of the world's oceans are overfished, meaning that the fish stocks affected are no longer able to replenish themselves. since nearly two thirds of the oceans are not under the exclusive jurisdiction of any one country but are international waters, international cooperation and regulation are needed to curb illegal fishing. at the same time, the world's growing population is increasingly dependent on marine protein. in addition to combating illegal fishing, there is therefore a need to promote freshwater fisheries as the most sustainable form of fishing.

in writing. − as one of the shadow rapporteurs, i would like to congratulate ms svensson and mr gustafsson on the acceptance of the report on gender mainstreaming in the work of the european parliament. gender mainstreaming is a strategy that has been actively pursued in the european parliament for many years. this report which is the first gm report in this legislature, aims to follow the progress and summarise the different tools available in order to help other political bodies of the ep to integrate the gender perspective into their work. i believe that the objective of gender equality needs to be explicitly integrated into all social and economic policy, rather than being handled in isolation. it is important to keep in place what has already been achieved and keep the gender mainstreaming network active. but we also need to move forward and transform the procedure of adopting gender mainstreaming amendments so that it becomes a formally recognised working method of the parliament. looking back at the gm reports adopted by the plenary in the past it is reassuring to see that even if the strategy of mainstreaming is not a quick fix solution a steady progress has been made during the years.

in writing. - my fellow members of parliament! the council meeting on 8 and 9 december will be marked by the search for a solution to the euro crisis. however, i believe it is important to pay due attention to the europe 2020 strategy and the eu's energy policy, as well as to short- and medium-term crisis management. a possible gas crisis would only add to the negative effects of the financial crisis. it is gratifying to see that efforts to achieve the eu energy market continue at the level of investment despite financial difficulties. this is illustrated by the signing of a declaration of intent by the eastern european countries on the eve of the energy council last week to establish a north-south energy corridor. the basic objective of the participating member states is to reduce the region's dependence on imports by enabling the north-south transport of energy. this is to be achieved by appropriate interconnection of gas, oil and electricity networks. the haste of the signatory countries is understandable, as the citizens of the region still vividly remember the winter of 2009, when russia closed the pipelines on the coldest of days. however, intersections are a major problem not only in times of gas crises but also in everyday life. in the case of central and eastern european energy markets, the infrastructure is not adequate and the flow of energy is often not smooth. in my view, cooperation is a good example of the need for coordination at regional and eu level in the event of an economic crisis. it also highlights that there is no time to delay if we want to achieve a truly functioning single energy market by 2014.

member of the commission. − madam president, i would like to thank honourable members very much for this debate – a debate which was overall very positive and inspiring. i am encouraged by the broad support you have given to the accession of croatia. i hope this will be translated into a decisive vote tomorrow. i also heard your encouragement to croatia to continue its preparation for membership. the findings of our autumn monitoring show that croatia has maintained the momentum in its preparation for membership and has achieved a very high level of preparation to assume the responsibilities of membership upon accession. completing these preparations should be a key priority for the government that will be formed after the upcoming elections. i am making that remark to stress the point that there will be no exceptions for croatia from meeting the obligations we have negotiated with croatia, and that these obligations will be met by the time that it accedes to the european union. let me also confirm that the ratification of the accession treaty will indeed be a stand-alone act based on article 49 of the treaty of the european union. from the commission’s side, i reiterate my firm commitment to continue our close cooperation and i will make sure that you are adequately informed about the progress achieved by croatia in the months to come, up to accession. the enlargement policy is the response to the legitimate aspiration of the people of europe to join the endeavour of a unified europe. let us work together so that the lesson of croatia – that hard work pays off – remains the hallmark of a credible enlargement policy.

in writing. croatia has increased its efforts in recent months. this hard work and commitment can serve as an example for countries preparing for eu membership. however, as the crisis deepens, some countries are unfortunately reluctant to enlarge, believing that this will exacerbate economic problems. i do not agree with those who believe that a period of consolidation after accession is necessary. croatia has proved its worthiness to be a member of the union. this is a common goal both politically and economically in the region. i am confident that croatia's accession will give a new impetus to the enlargement process as a whole, providing a european perspective for the western balkans. as is well known, the possibility of eu membership in the balkans has a strong impact on the introduction of democratic reforms. it should be stressed that the negotiations also contribute to the containment of conflicts in the volatile region and will continue to have a positive impact in the future. the balkan countries must also keep this vision in mind and continue to reform. citizens must not forget that reforms are not for brussels, but for citizens. but we also need to be clear about our commitments and promises. the final phase of the croatian accession negotiations has demonstrated the effectiveness of the eu institutions' ability to work together. i believe that other countries can draw new energy from croatia's achievements to meet the challenges they face.

the rapporteur. - i thank you for the points made in the debate and for the problems raised here. i want to emphasize that i fully understand those colleagues who are concerned that we are setting a negative precedent here. we all share the desire to have effective, usable and coherent financial instruments for the union, and this is particularly true in the field of human rights and democratisation. if the citizens of europe are to really believe that these millions are for a good cause, they need to develop a strategy that they will shape together with parliament. so the most natural demand on our part is that a multiannual framework, a strategy, should not be adopted without our consent, without our co-decision. it is therefore incomprehensible and bitter for everyone to see why the commission and the council are so opposed to this. as i said, i understand the reservations of the rapporteurs, but at the same time we must see that we have reached a compromise and we consider this to be a major step forward in terms of the financial instrument for democratization and human rights. we also expect that this agreement, this resolution, will be applied in the development of the next strategy and framework. i would also like to stress our dual responsibility, or our multiple responsibility and credibility, which is now increasingly binding on all of us in this debate that has been going on for two years.

mr president the purpose of my speech is to call attention to the harmful activities of the credit rating agencies and the irresponsible statements of some of the commissioners. rating agencies form opinions, i.e. ratings, based on short-term interests, market sentiment and without scientific methodology. credit ratings assess sentiment elements in terms of market interests and prospects, i.e. they are not reliable in terms of their feedback on the business environment, but they can cause billions of dollars in damage. the question is why are you still waiting for a european credit rating? we are fighting an extraordinary battle to protect and defend the euro. i therefore cannot understand exactly whose interest is being served by a commissioner or by the vice-president himself when he makes irresponsible statements that highlight certain countries in a negative and disparaging way in relation to their economic policies.
in writing. – today reminds us that the numbers are terrible: 30 years - 60 million hiv infections, 30 million deaths. what is even worse is that we are talking about a fully preventable disease, but with a growing number of infections! the targets are ambitious; the spread of hiv/aids should be reversed by 2015. but how do we get there? i would like to emphasize three points. first, prevention. equitable, universal access to prevention is necessary, together with adequate information, sex education and access to means of protection. of course, when it comes to prevention, we shall mention vaccination as well which has already helped to lower and then freeze the number of new hiv infections to 2.7 million per year. second, anti-discrimination. we should make sure that people suffering from hiv/aids are not discriminated and not stigmatized. third, the gender aspect. we should bear in mind that women are more vulnerable than men in getting affected. this again shows the importance of and the need for ensuring women's rights and autonomy. all this requires serious, long-term political and financial engagement and support.

in writing. −it is undeniable that the 2012 budget of the european union has been shaped by the financial problems of the member states. the financial crisis is affecting the union severely because of its economic vulnerability. however, i am pleased to announce that the eu institutions have finally agreed to adopt a modest, frugal budget. the main priority now is to restore the financial system, which requires job creation, employment growth and investment to stimulate economic growth. i believe that the prerequisite for all this is the adoption and implementation of a responsible budget, which i believe should be accompanied by strict economic discipline. these requirements apply not only to the union but also to each member state. it is worth noting that with the adoption of the new hungarian fundamental law, domestic fiscal law has also undergone a major reform in order to effectively manage the crisis and stimulate the economy. constitutional provisions now state that hungary operates according to the principle of balanced, transparent and sustainable budgetary management. the hungarian parliament and the government of the day bear the primary responsibility for the implementation of this principle.
in writing. − the report aims to make financial instruments regulation clearer and more differentiated. the member states' experience with the use of financial instruments was very positive in the 2000-2006 programming period and they have continued to use these instruments in the 2007-2013 period accordingly. i am pleased that the european commission wants to clarify the use of an existing instrument. in order to provide the commission with adequate information on the implementation of financial instruments, it is essential that specific reporting provisions are introduced in relation to them, but i consider it important to underline that more rigorous supervision of financial instruments by the european commission should not result in a significant increase in the reporting and administrative burden for member states.
in writing. − the european union is facing a prolonged economic and financial crisis which is having a very negative impact on the member states' budgets and macroeconomic stability. the use of regional resources is thus seriously jeopardised, as it is becoming increasingly difficult to secure the member state's share of co-financing. in this context, the european commission's proposal to temporarily increase the co-financing rate by 10 percentage points for eu member states facing particular difficulties in terms of public debt and financial stability is welcome. the total cohesion policy support for the countries concerned (hungary, romania, latvia, portugal, greece, ireland) would remain unchanged overall. i am pleased that there was no major disagreement between the political groups and that the negotiations were concluded in a short time, so that the report can be voted on at the current plenary session and the payment of the grant can begin at the beginning of 2012.
in writing. − i am concerned that the canary islands have been particularly hard hit by the financial and economic crisis, with unemployment more than doubling in the island as a result of the crisis (26.2% in 2009, compared to between 10.4% and 12% between 2001 and 2007). increasing employment is a fundamental objective of the european union's regional policy, and this objective is given special emphasis in times of economic crisis. in the light of all this, i consider it appropriate to extend the period of application of the tax credit for products produced in the canary islands by one year.
in writing. − the development of the outermost regions of the union, particularly the french overseas departments, is hampered by their remoteness, small size and unfavourable hilly and climatic conditions. this is the reason why the french authorities are able to grant a reduction in excise duty on traditional rum produced in the french overseas departments in the european territory. in this context, i would like to stress the importance of the union's particular attention to supporting and upgrading the most disadvantaged regions, and this aspect should be consistently applied in the negotiations on the future of cohesion policy.
in writing. − one of the five priorities of the europe 2020 strategy is to reduce the school drop-out rate to below 10% and to increase the proportion of 30-34 year olds with a university degree or equivalent to at least 40%. in hungary, the rate of dropouts in 2009 was 11.2 percent, and the rate of those aged 30-34 with a university degree or equivalent was 23.9 percent. it is also true that in our country, school dropout is particularly common among poor and disadvantaged children. in the case of roma children, further measures are needed to achieve the goal of education that provides everyone with an equal chance of success. i support this report with my vote, which follows the political agreement reached in parliament during the hungarian presidency on reducing the number of early school leavers.
in writing. − roma pupils are frequently subject to low-quality education throughout the eu, often aggravated by segregated education and gypsy-only classrooms. their difficulties in receiving quality education result in their astoundingly low educational achievements: 20% of roma children are not enrolled in school, 30% are early school leavers and some 50% of roma are illiterate or semi-illiterate. local governments must take appropriate measures for the reintegration of early school leavers up to the maximum age of compulsory education and the institution must inform the parents and the local government about school-leavers. assistance mechanisms, such as scholarships and mentoring support should be established for young roma, to inspire them not only to obtain diplomas, but also to enrol in higher education. a system of perks should also be considered, which would increase the participation of roma youth in such institutions and improve their qualifications. teachers who teach underprivileged students or are employed in lagging-behind regions should be financially compensated and receive appropriate training. it is also necessary to create specific programmes targeting pre-school education and to develop semi-boarding programmes and ‘tutoring’ in schools, simultaneously targeting the adult age group with vocational training and lifelong learning programmes.
in writing. − croatia's accession negotiations to the european union have been ongoing for six years. the european people's party and hungary strongly supported the inclusion of croatia in the negotiations. i also voted in favour of the accession of the republic of croatia to the european union and the council decision accepting its application for accession. the draft accession treaty was drawn up in the council with the agreement of hungary. the hungarian interests were identified in the document during the drafting process, which took more than a year. croatia is scheduled to become the 28th member state of the union on 1 july 2013. however, i would draw attention to the continued need for croatia to cooperate with the icty (international criminal tribunal for the former yugoslavia), to improve the efficiency of the judiciary and to continue the fight against corruption and organised crime.

in writing. − the crisis has made it clear to all of us that the union needs to exercise stricter budgetary control. accordingly, our primary objective in the european semester is to coordinate the fiscal and economic policies of the member states, in line with the stability and growth pact and the eu 2020 economic strategy. our priority now is to create an effective instrument at community level to prevent future sovereign debt crises! however, the introduction of more stringent sanctions is an essential condition for this. the six-pack of legislation drawn up under the hungarian presidency has its greatest merit in placing the emphasis on controlling public expenditure and the importance of the debt criterion. while greater coordination of member states' economic policies is necessary, it is not sufficient to overcome the crisis. in order to manage the consequences of the recession and to be able to develop a sustainable economic policy, stricter budgetary control is essential. hungary is already on this path. unfortunately, our country is one of the most indebted countries in europe. and the current extraordinary level of our national debt is the result of the irresponsible management of previous socialist governments. in order to put an end to the indebtedness of our country once and for all, hungary has this year adopted at the fundamental level provisions on a responsible budget and the limit on public debt. i suggest that the member states of the european union follow the example of the new hungarian basic law. let's have a constitutional barrier to debt so there's not another greek, irish or portuguese crisis.
in writing. − although there is a slight decrease of new hiv infections on a global scale, the numbers in the eu and neighbouring countries are still rising. according to estimates, almost 30% of european hiv-positive people are totally unaware of their status, resulting in their 3.5 times higher probability of transmitting the virus. additionally, half of the infected prove to be late for care and progress to late stages of the infection before even being diagnosed. women and girls are disproportionately affected by hiv and aids and current prevention options – such as condoms and abstinence – have proven inadequate in handling the risks that those women face, who are married, want children or are exposed to sexual violence. the four-year action plan of the commission to reduce new hiv infection throughout europe must be fully implemented, with special regard to improving access to prevention, treatment and care as well as improving the life conditions of those already infected by hiv. in a broader perspective, universal access to health care, as well as to std and hiv/aids-related prevention, care and support must be facilitated and key populations must be involved in the design, implementation and monitoring.

in writing. – we are about two weeks after the international day for the elimination of violence against women. it was a symbolic day to remind us about the ‘ugly face of abuse’ and the terrifying numbers got more attention than usual: more women between the age of 16 and 44 die because of violence than because of cancer or traffic accidents. every third woman is a victim of violence during her life. we have said many times before and i will say again: it is our responsibility to protect these victims! the european protection order is a very important step in this direction. furthermore, victims (not ‘only’ women) of all kinds of violence will enjoy the same protection in every member state which can contribute – via inspiring european criminal law – towards realising a european area of justice. i think the day when the regulation on the epo is applicable (in three years) will be an important day. it may not be an international day of anything but, more importantly, it will give victims a real chance for protection – throughout europe, an equal treatment of protection, no matter which member state they choose to live in. this is why we are here.
the ageing societies of the european union are in great need of regulated migrants, workers who contribute to the competitiveness of the union, to the maintenance of our social security system. in the coming years, about 700,000 workers will be lost in the it sector, and nearly 2 million workers in health care. europe is becoming less attractive to highly qualified workers from third countries. it's less than the united states of america, or canada, or australia, or switzerland. to make us more attractive, it is important to have a common migration policy, and this proposal, the single permit directive, is a big step in that direction. after lengthy negotiations, a political compromise was reached on the scope of the directive. there was also a great deal of debate about equal treatment. we are glad that the portability of pensions, the right to further vocational training, finally came into play. i would say to my epp colleagues that our abstention meant, expressed that we wanted to achieve more in the negotiations. we need to continue this work in several areas to have a truly common european migration policy. on the one hand, there are delays in the transposition and application of the adopted directives in the blue card. but progress is also needed on the directive on intra-corporate transfers and seasonal workers. i would like to conclude by saying that if europe is to be truly competitive, it must open its labour market to workers from the new member states. we're going to talk about romania, bulgaria, partly in seasonal work, partly in highly skilled work.

vice-president of the commission/high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. − mr president, this has been an interesting – and i think very important – debate, not least because, in the context of looking at the economic situation that member states and institutions find themselves in, it is absolutely vital that we discuss how best to make sure that we are able to respond to some of the crises and security threats that we are faced with as individual nations, as parliamentarians and as institutions. i do not think that anyone in this house will be surprised to know that this forms part of the discussions that i have on a regular basis with secretary-general rasmussen and when i take part in nato meetings, at their request, both in foreign minister and defence minister formation. it is really vital that we understand what it is we want to try to achieve and how best to go about it. it is worth remembering the kind of missions that our people are currently engaged in and how those missions work effectively with others that are also operating in the field, not least nato. let me remind honourable members that we have monitoring missions operating in georgia as a result of the crisis that georgia faced when russia behaved as it did in august 2008. we still have people now who are helping to make sure that there is confidence built on the borders and making sure that people are able to go about their business in an ordinary way. a number of people have talked about the horn of africa and the incredible work that the atalanta mission does, made up of different member states which very willingly bring together their expertise, their ships and their helicopters, led by the british in northwood – very effectively by admiral potts currently serving in command from northwood – and actually playing a vital role in ensuring that the world food programme can deliver food to people who are literally starving, and helping to support countries which are grappling with how to tackle piracy off their coasts, the threat that poses for individuals, the threat of hostage taking and hijacking, and the threat for those who rely on tourism. these are really important issues where the european union has a contribution to make, not trying to do what nato does but doing things with nato. perhaps the best example to illustrate this is what is happening currently in kosovo, with eulex kosovo providing what is called the second response line: police officers willing to go in to try to stop the kind of violence that we have witnessed recently in the north of kosovo, working hand in hand with kfor, with the nato forces which are there as the third response unit. this is an international operation because the dangers and difficulties of what has happened between serbia and kosovo required us all to provide an international response. member states freely and willingly want to engage together in a way in which they think they can be effective, which is to provide this kind of police support. that is quite different to what nato is doing in providing its military support. throughout all of these operations we work very closely with nato. indeed, when we were involved with nato in discussing how best we could support what was occurring in libya, nato was very conscious of being engaged in a military fashion, yet needing others to think about how to engage military assets, the kind of equipment that you need in order to support humanitarian aid. so all i would say to those who are worried about the overlap is that we work incredibly hard to make sure that does not exist and we respond to the support that nato needs and the way in which member states want to take this forward. i do not have to remind anyone that, as you look around at what is happening in countries to which we are close or where we are operating, there are many issues to be concerned about, as i was especially aware when i met the four ministers from the sahel over the last few days. they are looking at our comprehensive and strategic approach to how we support them with their concerns about the hunger of their citizens, about terrorism and their worries about arms now moving down from libya into the sahel. they need a comprehensive approach to help them do this and that means thinking about how to support them in a security way as well. we have also got to remember that the european defence agency is working collaboratively with nato. again, improvised explosive devices are a problem that affects not only our servicemen and women from all over the world in afghanistan, but in many other parts of the world too. if you can provide a joined-up way of actually looking at how you can identify where those explosive devices might be, who might have made them, the kind of signature that is left by those who make bombs of different kinds, if you can do that you can save lives everywhere. so if this is done by people who can provide the expertise working together, it seems to me an effective use of time, effort and resources. i agree with those who say we also need to think about cyber defence and the importance of how we might respond to that. in this regard we have been approached by the us deputy secretary of defense to work with them because one of the challenges in cyber defence is that it does not stop at member state borders, but works across them. many of the industries and businesses who are most challenged by cyber defence are european as regards their outlets, factories and businesses. they often are a combination of different countries’ businesses working together. so it is logical to try to think of things across the european union when that makes greater sense. i want to say two things to end with. first of all, i agree with all those who say that most of our work should be preventative, that the mediation, the work we do on conflict prevention and using resources to try to prevent conflicts and prevent problems is absolutely right. i agree with that wholeheartedly. again, that is complementary to those engaged in theatre in other ways. i agree with what has also been said about the need to be extremely proud of the servicemen and women who are operating in theatre at the present time. as we move to what, for us, is the christmas and holiday season, i am very conscious that men and women are serving across the world and, especially, as was mentioned, in afghanistan. i am extremely proud of the work that they do and i know that members of this house are too. finally, a word for mrs dodds because she asked me a very specific question about whether we should make sure that those doing lonely, complex jobs in places like afghanistan actually have the procedures that will help them and that we should get the procedures right. in response to that, can i agree with her and pay tribute to mr white for the incredible work that he did in iraq.
i'll close this discussion. the vote will take place on wednesday 14 december 2011 at 11.30.
