my fellow members of parliament! for a few speeches on the european neighbourhood policy, i felt that we were not living in a single europe. i have often heard him say that the problems of the countries neighbouring the european union are more important to a few of the members than the problems of the european union itself. everyone, we all acknowledged that the arab spring is a good thing, a good thing. we all want the institutions of democracy to be strengthened, to be built up in the neighbouring states. but, commissioner füle, if i heard you correctly, you said that we were spending 350 million euros on the new spring programme, for example. please, europe is in trouble. with that kind of money, think of the jobs that could have been created, the factories, the processing plants that could have been built in europe, that would have served the interests of the people of europe. europe does have to protect its borders, europe does have to protect its markets.
mr. president, i have to ask you... i'm sorry to hear that mrs. de lange has left. i hope your fellow mps won't defend the stupid things you said. because there is no populist, liberal or socialist juice. you either have good quality juice or you have bad quality juice. that is why i fully support the proposal before us. i have already stressed, a few months ago, in the debate on the directive on food labelling, that the regulation must be primarily in the interests of consumers. in the case of fruit juices, it is particularly important to identify methods of misleading consumers. it's a pity mrs. de lange doesn't know that. the general belief about these products is that they are of natural origin and healthy. that's not what this is about. consumers are deceived in many cases. at present, the packaging of fruit juices may contain a number of indications which are liable to mislead the consumer. the directive rightly seeks to eliminate those practices. i agree that we should prohibit manufacturers from displaying on the packaging fruit which is not included in the packaged fruit juice. i also think it is appropriate to make it compulsory to clearly indicate that the fruit juice is made from concentrate and to prohibit the use of the term 'fresh fruit juice' in such cases. it's important to point out if you've added artificial flavor enhancers to your juice. and fruit juices should be clearly distinguished from fruit nectar produced from concentrate or powder with a high sugar content. all these requirements are in the interests of consumers, and i therefore agree with the proposal, the directive.
i think all of us in this room agree with the basic tenets of this report. but the magic number, as my colleague mr kelly put it, is that 82% of the fruit juice in the european union comes from imports. and in order to comply with the directive, to achieve a healthy, normal ratio, we need to increase fruit production in europe. on the other hand, the percentage of fruit juices from european fruit in fruit juices available in the european union should be increased. it is extremely ambivalent to talk about this while at the same time, at the behest of the european union, at the instigation of the european union, huge orchards in central and eastern europe are being cut down. in hungary, for example, the european union is forcing farmers to cut down orchards. and if the european union spent a fraction of what it spends on replanting fruit farms, on rebooting the processing industry, on what it spends on african countries, then all of eastern europe would be flourishing!
it's a two-card draw. we thank mr. newton dunn for his report. i shall vote in favour of this report, but my question is whether this report is currently concerned with reducing the phosphate content of detergents. when will there be a strategy that will include, say, fertilizer? when we reduce the phosphate content there, too, because the report says it's fertilizer, but like water, it's not dangerous. now, in many cases, this can actually threaten water supplies, groundwater supplies. so will there be a strategy, and if so, when will it be developed?
mr prime minister! my fellow members of the house of representatives! i commend the polish presidency for standing up to the challenge. despite the extraordinary economic situation, as a hungarian representative, let me also say that, well balanced with the legacy of the hungarian presidency, you have carried out your programme. the sixth package of measures to strengthen the common economic governance has finally entered into force. it is a good thing that the treaty of accession of croatia was signed a few days ago. and yes, the success of the eastern partnership summit. the polish presidency has also mobilised a great deal of energy policy. the commission has been given a mandate to negotiate with the eu in order to strengthen import competition. this will make the eu's action towards third countries more effective, in the common interest of 500 million european consumers. the start of negotiations on the caspian gas pipeline system is also a significant achievement. this could significantly improve the security of supply in the union. finally, we continue to work with all our might to achieve the single market, which can reduce the disparities between the energy markets of the member states. my fellow members of parliament! the above examples show that the successive hungarian and polish presidencies have achieved significant results in the past year, reinforcing each other. as a result, europe was undoubtedly stronger in these areas in 2011.
mr president my fellow members of the house of representatives! the polish presidency has chosen progress over staying, openness over turning in, efficiency over noise, and the union over defiant secession! so we can say: "polska, europa, dwa bratanki, i do sabli, i do szklanki" - and that's what we are doing.
in writing. mr prime minister, dear members of the house of representatives! six months ago, in my speech at the inauguration of the polish presidency, i said that central europe has never been more at the heart of the eu. we have to prove that the enlargement of the european union has been a success! i think we have succeeded in this, hungary and poland have also successfully overcome this obstacle, and we can all be proud of that! at the same time, i asked the polish presidency to pay attention to the disabled. this was a major issue in the previous spanish-belgian-hungarian presidency. unfortunately, my concern was not unfounded: the polish presidency has not continued the work it had started in this area, which is unacceptable to me. i hope the danish presidency will put an end to this scourge. i believe that 80 million people with disabilities deserve to be treated with dignity by successive presidents.
in writing. the polish and hungarian nations have a centuries-old friendship. no wonder, because before the most unjust dictatorship in world history, the tragedy of trianon, hungary and poland were neighbouring countries. in the light of this historical common heritage, i respectfully ask the polish government to do everything in the few days remaining before the eu presidency so that the hungarians in the carpathian basin can freely live out their national identity and, in particular, in slovakia, to guarantee the free use of the hungarian language for the hungarians in the upper carpathian basin. i wish our polish friends more good work.
in writing. during the polish presidency, we have made significant progress in both enlarging the european union and deepening integration. in september, a six-pack of legislation to deepen eu economic governance was agreed. in december, the accession treaty was signed for croatia and it was decided that accession negotiations with montenegro could start next year. i hope that in february a decision will be taken on granting serbia candidate status. the polish presidency was able to build on the successes of the hungarian eu presidency's excellent work on vocational training. on economic governance, the hungarian presidency managed to build consensus in almost all areas, and it was also during the hungarian presidency that the croatian accession negotiations were concluded. in the case of hungary, the professional work was successful, while the poles showed a clear commitment to the european union. they did not make any eurosceptic statements, as viktor orbán has allowed himself to do on several occasions. while the polish government sought national consensus during the eu presidency, and this willingness to cooperate was also reflected in the brussels forums, the hungarian government postponed the adoption of a media law restricting freedom of speech and a new one-party constitution curtailing the rule of law until the eu presidency. the deteriorating international standing of the hungarian government has overshadowed the professional performance of the rotating presidency.
in writing. − i accept the commission's proposal, which proposes three amendments to council regulation (ec) no 1085/2006 of 17 july 2006, which measures do not require additional expenditure. the first involves moving iceland and montenegro from the list of potential candidate countries to the list of candidate countries. the european people's party has always supported the enlargement process in the western balkan countries and iceland has already started accession negotiations, as it meets by far the copenhagen criteria. i agree with the commission's second amendment proposal, which would allow participation in cbc programmes cross-border cooperation programmes under the instrument for pre-accession assistance (ipa) only for local actors. as a border country of the european union, it is in hungary's interest to limit the possibility of participation in calls for proposals to applicants from countries participating in the relevant ipa cbc programmes. in our neighbourhood, there was one potential candidate country, serbia. i also support the third amendment, which is designed to enable the phare committee to continue to assist the commission in the management of financial aid to the turkish cypriot community.
in writing. − in order to more effectively promote democracy, human rights, social justice and the rule of law within the wider region, the european neighbourhood policy needs to be adjusted following the political and economic developments of the related neighbouring states. as the rapporteurs point out, it is necessary to differentiate between the policy’s southern and eastern dimension to target the specific properties and needs of each geographical area. those regions that extend beyond the external borders of the eu must be involved in development cooperation in the wider sense, for example the european groupings of territorial cooperation. sub-regional and cross-border cooperation needs also to be promoted and euroregions might serve as a catalyst for realising the goals of cohesion policy with special regard to border regions.
in writing. − i rejected the report on the eu's counter-terrorism policy because its content is unacceptable on the basis of the political views of the european people's party. the report is a one-sided, offensive and biased attack on the european union's counter-terrorism policy. the report lacks a strategic concept, as it considers that the prevention, detection and prosecution of terrorist offences are critical policies at eu level that should be part of a systematic approach based not on emergency norms but on a coherent strategy based on necessity. it points to the need to broaden and deepen strategic partnerships to counter terrorism, provided that these partnerships ensure full respect for human rights. this clause suggests that if the current actions of european counter-terrorism violate human rights. it called for a more precise assessment of the measures already taken to combat terrorism, taking into account, inter alia, fundamental rights and budgetary considerations. by this remark, it refers to an exaggeration of costs, which is an unfounded assertion. overall, the release of intelligence information examined in the report would fundamentally jeopardise european security.
in writing. − my reservations to the report are twofold: a matter of both principle and practical approach. the text’s tone is infiltrated and determined by prejudices and accusations that counter-terrorism simply keeps violating human rights, that it operates with excessive budgets and exaggerates threats. these propagandistic concepts are based on hypotheses to which no evidence was provided by the rapporteur. on the other hand, goals laid down in the report are hardly feasible in practice. one example of many: with data being classified for security reasons, to what extent and on which benchmarks should the proportionality between the cost of a preventive counter terrorism operation and the threat level be assessed? why does the rapporteur believe that either secret services of member states or eu security agencies would grant civilians access to their most sensitive data thus putting european security at risk? how could we assess proportionality without the knowledge of these extremely sensitive bits of information? as staunch supporters of accountability, the epp group stands for overseeing counter-terrorism policies, however within the established competence of designated authorities. demoralising counter-terrorism, especially nowadays, is proof of irresponsible behaviour and thus i am not in a position to support the report.
the rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on petitions. − any initiative that encourages the expansion of transparency and the development of good administration techniques is welcome. in times of crisis, it is in the vital interest of citizens to have a clear view of the issues and facts underlying political debates and institutional decisions. but at the same time, if we were to extend access to information to the entire world and try to achieve it with a little bit of propaganda overreach, then the noble intentions could easily backfire. getting to know the documents can't be a party game. it should not cover cases where access to information infringes other fundamental rights, i am thinking in particular of the rights of sensitive individuals and, on the other hand, of the rights and interests of the community in relation to security. so if you violate or weaken these, you could reverse the very value of the pursuit. the balance of our fundamental rights must not be upset, as it is one of the guarantees of european democracies. i therefore sincerely regret that we cannot support mr cashman's otherwise important and respected report because of its excessive and one-sided conception on many points. but i'm really sorry about that, because your primary objective is very important.
my fellow citizens! mr grosch has done an excellent job, which i hope will give the right impetus to the transport sector to move forward in the right direction. i have had the opportunity to stress my view on several occasions before that an inclusive, sustainable, modern, environmentally friendly and efficient transport system is urgently needed. an important part of this is the single european passenger charter, which sets out the rules for the different transport sectors. i would like to thank the rapporteur in particular for mentioning disabled people in this chapter. but i don't want to talk about them exclusively. because europe, as we know, is a society that is aging severely. and we, as legislators, will be faced with the need to meet the demands in the legislative field. in the coming period, there will be a greater need for older citizens and persons with disabilities to be able to use transport freely under the same conditions. i think the message is clear in the report and i myself would like to stress that there is a real need for the european commission to come forward with a proposal to this end at the beginning of 2012.
first of all, on behalf of the jobbik delegation, let me thank you for your work so far and wish you every success in the future. well, our position is that we need to tighten the prison system, not ease it. and i would like to introduce a new concept to the public, here in the european parliament: the concept of a self-sustaining, self-sustaining prison system. the criminological significance of prisons is not only that they isolate the criminal from society for a certain period of time, but also that they have a deterrent effect. it's completely incomprehensible that in hungary, for example, the state spends 800-900 euros a month on a prisoner. but a kindergarten, a college, a high school, the state spends a fraction of that. in fact, a fraction of that is the minimum wage. a disproportionate amount that is simply incomprehensible. the detainees, in our view, in some cases, in most cases, have to bear the costs of detention. that is something that should be discussed in the future here in the european parliament!
my fellow members of parliament! the european union is strategically important and should be important in uzbekistan. not just uzbekistan, but actually the whole of the inner asian region. there are three things that must be taken into account in any agreement, if it is to be concluded between the european union and uzbekistan. the first is that the more the european union has, the better. of course, we should not be vulnerable to chinese cotton exports either, so it is good that the european union has good relations with the world's third largest cotton exporter, uzbekistan. but uzbekistan must make progress on human rights and the european union must not give in to child labour. child labor is a very serious business. i say that as a mother of two. the third requirement is that the interests of the european textile industry must also be taken into account.
in writing. i hope that the member states which still keep their labour markets closed to bulgarian and romanian workers will lift the transitional restriction early next year. no survey has confirmed previous fears that, following the accession of bulgaria and romania in 2007, their workers would flood the member states, causing serious disruption to the labour market. rather, we find that people from both countries are in the shortage of jobs and are doing jobs that have few applicants within the country. i therefore consider it unjustified that eu member states should wish to maintain the restrictions imposed on them during the final phase of the seven-year transition period. the exclusion of bulgarian and romanian nationals from the labour market of the member states opens up the possibility of semi-legal, undeclared work and of fake businesses, which is accompanied by a curtailment of workers' rights. it's important that the right job finds the right workforce. this requires member states to take seriously the eu's right to free movement of labour. it is the responsibility of the european commission to ensure that countries wishing to maintain transitional restrictions beyond 1 january 2012 do so only on the basis of convincing arguments and justification supported by credible data.
the intelligent energy europe programme is an excellent initiative from an absolutely credible source. there is no longer any doubt that the eu's energy strategies will be delivered, and that the eu's competitiveness will indeed soar, soon overtaking emerging countries and perhaps later the rest of the world. and of course, all of this in a totally sustainable way. poor chinese people are watching with trembling knees the incredible progress of the european union. or wait, if i look closer, they'll be holding their bellies in their laughter and their knees will be shaking. don't you see how you can take a teaspoon out of a sinking boat with a leak, and not put a lid on it? let's have a serious talk, my fellow members. the first step is to immediately repair the broken backbone of the energy system, as i have said many times in previous speeches. once the sick energy sector, especially electricity generation, is back on its feet, it is time to tackle the beautification, i.e. the 'intelligent energy for europe' programme. but it's not worth spending a lot of money on either. unfortunately, only 2% of the population can afford the so-called green energy at a real price, and maybe the other 98% should be considered at some point. unfortunately, despite all the talk about nuclear power plants, china has already committed to them 20 years in advance. and if we continue like this, sooner or later europe will be a canteen, lit by candles.
madam president, we have the eu 2020 strategy, we have a programme called intelligent energy europe, we have an institution called the executive agency for competitiveness and innovation and the elena facility, and the commissioner is saying to us that the best way ahead is to cut all these things. in fact, such a cut would be the most expensive way to get right away from our target! therefore, what we have to do is to improve efficiency throughout europe in order to get more smes involved. that is the way forward, and this is what parliament will stand for – debating horizon 2020, cosme and the mff in the coming months.
in writing. − the european union also provides financial support to external countries. the committee on budgetary control has prepared an own-initiative report on the financial control of union assistance to afghanistan. the european parliament also has the task of carrying out financial control of the use of resources outside the union. the report calls on the european commission to implement the recommendations made by the european court of auditors and urges the effective and rapid handling of the problems that remain. the measures proposed by the rapporteur are to be welcomed, as the control of the use of the eu budget within and outside the union is in the interest of all european citizens and therefore an obligation of the european parliament.
in writing. − i did not support the french request for aid in respect of the dismissal of 4445 people (of whom 3582 were entitled to aid) at renault and seven automotive suppliers. the report decides how european taxpayers' money is spent. i am not convinced that the european globalisation adjustment fund should be used for such a purpose, which i believe is the responsibility of the member state to address. the budget allocated to this aid application could be used by the union to help create jobs in regions where unemployment is indeed high. the history of the report shows that the aid will be paid directly to renault and peugeot and to dutch and german companies.
in writing. − i did not support the report on the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council on public access to european parliament, council and commission documents because its content is not in line with the european people's party and my own political values. i consider it unacceptable that anyone should have access to documents of the institutions, bodies, offices or agencies of the union. furthermore, the requirement of full public access to documents is difficult to achieve in practice, and i therefore believe that full public access is not the answer to facilitate citizen participation in decision-making. it is not justified if the disclosure of these documents infringes other fundamental rights, such as the right to privacy. however, i would be happy to support a less extreme and unilateral compromise solution which is closer to the people's party's position, as the subject of the report would be extremely important.
in writing. − in the scoreboard assessments, the selection of indicators focuses on the areas most relevant to macroeconomic imbalances and competitiveness developments, with particular emphasis on the smooth functioning of the euro area. i welcome the commission's statement on the data sources and statistical transformations it intends to use, and i believe that this is the right direction and i support it. it is also important that the commission takes account of the most recent data available and that it ensures full transparency as to which data are used. it is also important to stress the importance of aligning and integrating the new macroeconomic imbalance monitoring procedure with the next european semester. however, it should also be borne in mind that the scoreboard plays an important informative role, as the selection of indicators sends a clear perception-building message to policy-makers and stakeholders about the types of macroeconomic developments that may be of concern and that therefore need to be monitored more closely. in summary, economic analyses must ensure that all the information from the scoreboard or other sources is evaluated together in a comprehensive analysis, and i can therefore support any proposal to that effect.
in writing. − there are disturbing signs of the deterioration of european detention conditions, such as prison overcrowding, high proportion of pre-trial detainees or alarming occurrences of death and suicide, although appropriate conditions increase the detainees’ chances to return to society and decrease the probability of re-offending. special emphasis must be placed on the review of the detention conditions of children, as the most vulnerable of the prison population and the most in need of development and reintegration. depriving children of their freedom must be viewed as the last resort, for the shortest possible period and, when detained, they must be kept separated from adults and provided the possibility of regularly contacting their families. member states that fail to reach the level required by international obligations in terms of prison conditions and related legal guarantees must take specific steps to improve the fulfilment of fundamental rights of the detained and dedicate proper resources for restructuring and modernising their prisons. most importantly, the right to defence and the right not to be subject to degrading or inhuman treatment must be enforced and, to this end, ratifying the protocol to the convention against torture and cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment or punishment would be beneficial.
in writing. − eu standards on the free movement of workers have evolved considerably since they were codified. encouraging the free movement of labour is of paramount importance for europe as a whole, as it increases competitiveness and boosts economic growth. that is why i welcome the outcome of today's vote. coordination at union level is in the interest of all european workers and is also a guarantee of real labour mobility. it should be noted that the expected wave of immigration has not occurred, and the actual data are not even close to the forecasts. far fewer workers than expected have entered the western european labour market since the restrictions on citizens from the countries of central and eastern europe, which joined the european union in 2004, were fully lifted in may this year. this shows that we must do everything we can to ensure that eu citizens are able to work freely throughout the european union as soon as possible, thus ensuring the completion of the single eu labour market.
in writing. − there is a growing view that enforcement alone is not enough to achieve the eu's objective of reducing accidents at work set out in the community strategy on health and safety at work. further steps are needed to ensure that prevention policies are applied to all workers and that fundamental rights to health are better respected. this report, which i support, identifies the categories of workers at risk and those not covered by prevention policies in order to provide appropriate responses. the commission has also proposed that the member states should be required to comply fully with the letter and spirit of the framework directive. it is important that the actors directly concerned, i.e. workers and all persons in the workplace, have the opportunity to participate in the process of identifying and preventing risks.
